The calls run were `ccd(2, n0=c(3,3), alpha="rotatable", randomize=FALSE)`, `ccd(4, n0=c(4,2), alpha="orthogonal", randomize=FALSE)` and `ccd(5, n0=c(2,2), alpha="faces", randomize=FALSE)`.
   - rsm `bbd(3, n0=3, block=FALSE, randomize=FALSE)` equals item 5 row-for-row. `bbd(5, n0=3, block=FALSE, randomize=FALSE)` equals it as a multiset of rows, because rsm lists the pairs in another order (OBSERVED).
   - The item-4 block-mean equality holds exactly, with `α²` as a `Fraction`, for `(k, n_c, n_s) ∈ {(2, 3, 3), (4, 4, 2), (5, 6, 1)}`.
   - No held-out input (H22 or H23) was evaluated.
   - An independent fresh-context reviewer rebuilt both families from this text and checked items 3, 4 and 6 exactly with `Fraction` in 13 CCD cases (orthogonal, off-`α` and rotatable-only) and in Box–Behnken designs with `k ∈ {3, 4, 5}`, `n_0 ≤ 3` (held-out inputs excluded). Verdict: PASS.
8. **Estimability (OBSERVED, not a guarantee).** Zero center counts are allowed but can make the second-order model singular. A Box–Behnken design with `n_0 = 0` has `Σ_i x_i² = 2` on every row, and a CCD with `n_c = n_s = 0` has a singular blocked `M_B`. These designs are returned as specified; nothing here claims they are estimable.
9. **Cost.** `O((n_F + 2k + n_c + n_s)·k)` output elements, checked with `max_elements` as in `ff2n`. The Box–Behnken output is at most `40 + n_0` rows.

## 41. API decision: `central_composite` and `box_behnken`

- `central_composite(k, *, alpha="orthogonal", center=(4, 4), dtype=torch.float64, device=None, max_elements=1_000_000)` returns the §40.1 rows with `center = (n_c, n_s)`.
  - `k` is a Python `int ≥ 2`, and `center` is a tuple of two Python `int`s ≥ 0.
  - `alpha` is `"rotatable"`, `"orthogonal"`, `"faces"`, or a positive Python `int` or `float` that is not a `bool`. Anything else raises `ValueError`.
- `box_behnken(k, *, center=3, dtype=torch.float64, device=None)` returns the §40.5 rows. It accepts `k ∈ {3, 4, 5}` and a Python `int` `center ≥ 0`. Any other value raises `ValueError`.
- Both functions return a coded tensor of the given dtype on `device` (CPU by default), are exported from `torchoed`, and do not randomize.
- No block labels are returned: the block sizes are those of §40.1.

## 42. Not in this section

A fractional cube portion. Spherical or inscribed CCDs. Box–Behnken designs for `k = 6, 7` (a follow-up tested against frozen rsm `bbd` output) and blocked Box–Behnken designs. Randomization. Optimal augmentation of these designs. GPU timing. Any claim about the held-out cases H22 and H23 beyond what the week-end run records.

## 34. Fractional factorials by `(k, runs)` from the FrF2 catalogue (W10 C1, part 2)

This section extends §28. The design, generator convention and word-length pattern are those of §28 items 1, 2 and 6.

1. **Source (decision; CITED).** The table is, for each size, the first catalogue entry `"<k>-<p>.1"` (`p = k − m`, `m = log₂ runs`) of `FrF2::catlg`. It is frozen in `reports/w10-rref/frf2_catlg.csv` by #257, together with the FrF2 version, `sessionInfo()` and the catalogue citation copied verbatim from the installed FrF2 documentation. The covered sizes are `runs ∈ {8, 16, 32, 64}` and `m < k ≤ min(11, runs − 1)`, restricted to the rows present in that file. Each row's generator numbers are distinct. Rows that fail the check of item 3 are left out of the TorchOED literal. TorchOED holds the `gen` column as a fixed Python literal of Yates numbers. There is no search and no file read at run time.
2. **Yates conversion (decision and PROVED).** A catalogue generator number `g` (`1 ≤ g < 2^m`, at least two bits set) names the column `Π_{c : bit c of g is set} x_{·c}` of the full factorial in base factors `0 … m−1`, with bit 0 = factor A = base column 0 (Yates order: 1 = A, 2 = B, 3 = AB, 4 = C, …). Generator `t` of the TorchOED design is `bits(g_t)`, the set bits of `g_t` in ascending order, so factor `m + t` is that product. This is §28 item 1 with `generators[t] = bits(g_t)`. By §28 item 2, `A_j` of the result is the number of length-`j` words of the defining contrast subgroup generated by the words `{m + t} ∪ bits(g_t)`, `t = 0 … p−1`. Because each generator has at least two bits, every generating word has length ≥ 3. A product of two or more generating words contains at least two generator columns, so it has length ≥ 2. Hence `A₁ = 0` always. Yates bit order only names columns. The row order of `ff2n` permutes rows and leaves every `J_S` unchanged, so it does not affect the WLP.
3. **Agreement with the catalogue WLP (OBSERVED).**
   - Let `j₀` be the word length of the first entry of the catalogue `WLP` vector. The installed FrF2 help, quoted in `reports/w10-rref/sessionInfo.txt` (#257), states `j₀ = 1`: "starting with words of length 1".
   - Let `L` be the number of entries stored for the row. In the frozen file, `L = 7` for every row, so the stored vector covers word lengths `1 … 7`. It is zero-padded when `k < 7` and truncated when `k > 7` (OBSERVED in the file, not cited).
   - `word_length_pattern` returns float `A_j`. Each must equal its rounded value exactly; that check is the integer test.
   - A row agrees if all three of these hold, compared as integers:
     - `A_j` equals the stored entry for every `j` with `j₀ ≤ j ≤ min(k, j₀ + L − 1)`;
     - every stored entry for a length `j > k` is 0;
     - `A_j = 0` for every `j < j₀`.
   - The catalogue does not store lengths `j > j₀ + L − 1`, that is `A₈ … A_k` when `k > 7`, so they are never checked against it.
   - Under this rule, all 22 frozen rows agree. Two counts confirm it independently: an Architect Python-integer defining-subgroup count (#260 review) and a fresh-context reviewer's J-characteristic count (OBSERVED).
   - `reports/w10-rref/run.py` printed 7/22 because it also compared the zero padding and the truncated lengths.
   - A row that does not agree is left out of the TorchOED literal, and its `(k, runs)` raises (§35).
4. **Worked cases (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-17).** `m = 3`, `g = (3, 5, 6, 7)` gives the generators `[[0,1],[0,2],[1,2],[0,1,2]]` and WLP `(0, 0, 7, 7, 0, 0, 1)`, which is the first §28 item 7 case. `m = 4`, `g = (7, 14)` gives `[[0,1,2],[1,2,3]]` and WLP `(0, 0, 0, 3, 0, 0)`, which is the third §28 item 7 case.
5. **Cost.** A table lookup and `O(p·m)` bit operations. The design cost is that of §28.

## 35. API decision: `fractional_factorial_generators(k, runs) -> list[list[int]]`

- `k` and `runs` are Python ints (`type is int`). `(k, runs)` must be a covered size of §34.1: `runs ∈ {8, 16, 32, 64}` and `m < k ≤ min(11, runs − 1)` with the row in the literal. Any other pair raises `ValueError` naming the covered sizes. This includes `k ≤ m`, which is a full factorial: use `ff2n`. The function returns a fresh list of fresh lists, `bits(g_t)` for `t = 0 … p−1` (§34.2).
- Usage: `fractional_factorial(m, fractional_factorial_generators(k, runs))` with `m = runs.bit_length() − 1`. This is the C1 `(k, runs)` path.
- It is exported from `torchoed`, with an API entry, a guide snippet and a CHANGELOG line.

## 36. Not in this section

Catalogue entries other than `.1`, `k > 11`, 128 or more runs, blocked fractional factorials, minimum-aberration search, signed generators, choosing a design by resolution alone, clear-effects or estimation-capacity criteria, and GPU timing.

## 43. Box–Behnken designs for k = 6, 7 (W10 C2, part 3)

This extends §40.5–§40.6 and §41. All entries are exact integers in `{−1, 0, 1}`.

1. **Block designs (decision; OBSERVED source).** The three-factor blocks are taken from the rsm 2.10.6 output `bbd(k, n0=3, block=FALSE, randomize=FALSE)` frozen in `reports/w10-rref/rsm_bbd67.csv` (#257). Factors are 0-based, and the blocks are listed in rsm row order:
   - `k = 6`: `(0,1,3), (1,2,4), (2,3,5), (0,3,4), (1,4,5), (0,2,5)`.
   - `k = 7`: `(3,4,5), (0,5,6), (1,4,6), (0,1,3), (2,3,6), (0,2,4), (1,2,5)`.
   TorchOED holds them as a fixed Python literal. Nothing is read from the file at run time.
2. **Rows (decision).** For each block `(a, b, c)` with `a < b < c`, in the item-1 order, there are 8 rows for `r = 0 … 7`. In each, `x_a = 2·bit_0(r) − 1`, `x_b = 2·bit_1(r) − 1`, `x_c = 2·bit_2(r) − 1`, and all other coordinates are 0. Then come `n_0` center rows. That gives `8·6 + n_0 = 48 + n_0` rows for `k = 6` and `8·7 + n_0 = 56 + n_0` for `k = 7`.
3. **Agreement with rsm (OBSERVED; Architect, 2026-11-17, sandbox, Python ints).** With `n_0 = 3`, the item-2 rows equal `rsm_bbd67.csv` row-for-row: 51 × 6 and 59 × 7.
4. **Moments (PROVED).**
   - Rotatability here means the second-order moment conditions of §40.3. All moments of order ≤ 4 with an odd exponent are 0, and `Σ x_i⁴ = 3 Σ x_i² x_j²` for all `i ≠ j`.
   - Each block's `2³` factorial is invariant under flipping any single coordinate, and every coordinate outside the block is 0. So every moment of order ≤ 4 in which some exponent is odd is 0.
   - Each factor lies in exactly 3 blocks for both `k` (a count over the item-1 literals). So `Σ x_i² = Σ x_i⁴ = 24`.
   - For `i ≠ j`, `Σ x_i² x_j² = 8 λ_{ij}`, where `λ_{ij}` is the number of blocks that contain both `i` and `j`.
   - For `k = 7`, the blocks are the lines of a Fano plane, so every pair lies in exactly one block (`λ = 1`). Then `Σ x_i⁴ = 24 = 3 · 8 = 3 Σ x_i² x_j²`, and the design is rotatable for every `n_0`.
   - For `k = 6`, `λ_{ij} ∈ {1, 2}`: three pairs, `{0,3}`, `{1,4}` and `{2,5}`, lie in two blocks. Equality fails for those pairs, so the design is not rotatable for any `n_0`.
5. **Cost.** At most `56 + n_0` rows of `k ≤ 7` entries.

## 44. API amendment: `box_behnken`

- `box_behnken(k, *, center=3, dtype=torch.float64, device=None)` now accepts `k ∈ {3, 4, 5, 6, 7}`.
- For `k = 3, 4, 5` the output is unchanged (§40.5). For `k = 6, 7` it is the §43.2 rows.
- Any other `k` raises `ValueError`. Blocked Box–Behnken designs, randomization and `k > 7` stay out of scope.

## 45. Simplex centroid and box-constrained mixture regions (W11 D1, part 1)

All arithmetic in this section is exact. Each bound is converted to a `fractions.Fraction` once (decision): an `int`, a `Fraction` or a `str` such as `"1/10"` by `Fraction(v)`, and a `float` by `Fraction(repr(v))`, its shortest round-trip decimal, so that `0.1` means `1/10` and `[0, 0.1, 0.9]` sums to exactly 1. Every step below runs on `Fraction`, and the result is converted to the requested floating dtype once, at the end. This is the decimal the user typed, not the float's binary value. (Independent review found that `Fraction(float(v))` turns decimal single-point regions into empty regions or tiny triangles; this rule removes that.) A bound with no short decimal form, such as `1/3`, must be passed as `"1/3"` or `Fraction(1, 3)`: the float `1/3` sums to `1 − 10⁻¹⁶` over three components. The guide snippet says so. A `bool` is not a valid bound.

1. **Simplex centroid (definition; PROVED count).** For `q ≥ 2` components, the design has one point per nonempty subset `S ⊆ {0, …, q−1}`: `x_i = 1/|S|` for `i ∈ S`, otherwise `0`. Distinct subsets give distinct points (the support of the point is `S`), so there are exactly `2^q − 1` points. Order: by `|S|` ascending, then `S` (as a sorted tuple) lexicographically ascending.
2. **Region (definition).** For bounds `l, u ∈ ℚ^q` with `0 ≤ l_i ≤ u_i ≤ 1`, let `R = {x ∈ ℚ^q : Σ x_i = 1, l ≤ x ≤ u}`. `R` is a polytope in the hyperplane `Σ x = 1`.
3. **Feasibility (PROVED).** `R ≠ ∅` iff `Σ l ≤ 1 ≤ Σ u`. Necessity: sum the bounds. Sufficiency: if `Σ u = Σ l` then `Σ l = 1` and `x = l ∈ R`. Otherwise `t = (1 − Σ l)/(Σ u − Σ l) ∈ [0, 1]` and `x = l + t(u − l)` satisfies `l ≤ x ≤ u` and `Σ x = 1`.
4. **Vertex characterisation (PROVED).** A point `x ∈ R` is a vertex iff at least `q − 1` distinct coordinates are at one of their bounds (`x_i ∈ {l_i, u_i}`). Proof: `x` is a vertex iff the constraints active at `x` have rank `q`. The active constraints are the row `1ᵀ` and the rows `e_iᵀ` for each coordinate `i` at a bound. If `T` is the set of such coordinates, the rank of `{1} ∪ {e_i : i ∈ T}` is `|T| + 1` when `|T| ≤ q − 1` and `q` when `|T| = q`. So the rank is `q` iff `|T| ≥ q − 1`.
5. **Enumeration (McLean and Anderson 1966; PROVED complete and sound).** For each free index `i` and each of the `2^(q−1)` assignments `c ∈ {l, u}^{q−1}` of the other coordinates, set `x_j = c_j` for `j ≠ i` and `x_i = 1 − Σ_{j≠i} c_j`. Keep `x` iff `l_i ≤ x_i ≤ u_i`, and drop exact duplicates. Soundness: each kept `x` is in `R` and has `≥ q − 1` coordinates at a bound, so it is a vertex by item 4. Completeness: a vertex has a set `T`, `|T| ≥ q − 1`, of coordinates at a bound. Take `i ∉ T` if `|T| = q − 1`, else any `i`. Assign each `j ≠ i` the bound it attains (either one if `l_j = u_j`). The enumeration then produces `x`, because `x_i = 1 − Σ_{j≠i} x_j`. Bounds that are never attained (redundant bounds, such as `u_i = 1` when `Σ_{j≠i} l_j > 0`) need no preprocessing: they simply give no kept point. Cost: `q · 2^(q−1)` candidates, each `O(q)`.
6. **Face centroids (definition; PROVED that every face is found once).** For `d ∈ {1, …, q − 1}`, a `d`-face centroid is the mean of the distinct vertices of a face of `R` of dimension exactly `d` (the arithmetic mean of the face's extreme vertices, as in McLean and Anderson 1966, not its centre of mass). Construction: for each set `S` of `q − 1 − d` coordinates and each assignment `b ∈ {l, u}^S`, let `V_{S,b}` be the vertices with `x_S = b`. Keep it iff it is nonempty and `rank{v − v₀ : v ∈ V_{S,b}} = d` (exact `Fraction` elimination, `v₀` any member). Drop duplicates by vertex set. Proof that this yields exactly the `d`-faces: (a) `G = R ∩ {x_S = b}` is a face of `R` (each `x_j = b_j` is a supporting hyperplane of the valid inequality `x_j ≥ l_j` or `x_j ≤ u_j`, and an intersection of faces is a face), its vertices are `V_{S,b}` (vertices of a face are the vertices of `R` in it), and the rank test keeps it iff `dim G = d`. (b) Conversely, a `d`-face `F` has `aff F = {Σ x = 1, x_T = b_T}`, where `T` is the set of coordinates tight on all of `F`, and `d = q − 1 − |T|`. Taking `S = T` gives `G = R ∩ aff F = F`. If some `l_i = u_i`, or `Σ l = 1`, or `Σ u = 1`, `R` has dimension below `q − 1` (the last two collapse it to the point `l` or `u`) and the higher `d` give no face; that is not an error. The overall centroid is the case `d = dim R`.
7. **Order (decision).** Vertices in lexicographic order of their exact coordinates. Then, for each requested `d` in the order given, the `d`-face centroids in lexicographic order.
8. **Architect examples (OBSERVED; Architect, 2026-11-17, sandbox, exact `Fraction`; vertices cross-checked against 3000 random-direction HiGHS LPs per region in float64, sets equal; independently re-derived by the fresh-context reviewer).** The bounds may be passed as the strings shown or as the decimal floats `0.2, 0.1, …`; both give the same `Fraction`s.
   - `R1`: `l = (1/5, 1/10, 1/10)`, `u = (3/5, 1/2, 3/5)`. 6 vertices: `(1/5, 1/5, 3/5)`, `(1/5, 1/2, 3/10)`, `(3/10, 1/10, 3/5)`, `(2/5, 1/2, 1/10)`, `(3/5, 1/10, 3/10)`, `(3/5, 3/10, 1/10)`. 6 edge centroids: `(1/5, 7/20, 9/20)`, `(1/4, 3/20, 3/5)`, `(3/10, 1/2, 1/5)`, `(9/20, 1/10, 9/20)`, `(1/2, 2/5, 1/10)`, `(3/5, 1/5, 1/5)`. Overall centroid `(23/60, 17/60, 1/3)`.
   - `R2` (redundant upper bounds): `l = (1/5, 1/5, 0)`, `u = (1, 1, 1)`. 3 vertices: `(1/5, 1/5, 3/5)`, `(1/5, 4/5, 0)`, `(4/5, 1/5, 0)`. 3 edge centroids: `(1/5, 1/2, 3/10)`, `(1/2, 1/5, 3/10)`, `(1/2, 1/2, 0)`. Overall centroid `(2/5, 2/5, 1/5)`.
   - `R3` (`q = 4`): `l = (1/10, 1/10, 1/10, 1/10)`, `u = (3/5, 1/2, 2/5, 3/10)`. 11 vertices, 17 edge centroids, 8 two-face centroids and 1 overall centroid `(18/55, 14/55, 13/55, 2/11)`. Euler check `11 − 17 + 8 = 2`.
9. **Cost.** Vertices `O(q² 2^(q−1))` exact operations. Centroids for dimension `d`: `C(q, q−1−d) 2^(q−1−d)` subsets, each filtered over the vertex list and ranked in `O(|V| q²)`.

## 46. API decision: `simplex_centroid` and `mixture_vertices`

- `simplex_centroid(q, *, dtype=torch.float64, device=None) -> Tensor` returns the §45.1 points, shape `(2^q − 1, q)`. `q` is an int ≥ 2, else `ValueError`.
- `mixture_vertices(lower, upper, *, face_dims=(), dtype=torch.float64, device=None) -> Tensor` returns the §45.5 vertices, then the §45.6 centroids for each `d` in `face_dims`, in the §45.7 order.
  - `lower` and `upper` are sequences of `int`, `float`, `Fraction` or `str` values, converted as in the §45 preamble.
  - Raise `ValueError` if their lengths differ or are below 2, if a value is a `bool` or does not convert, if `0 ≤ l_i ≤ u_i ≤ 1` fails, if the region is empty (§45.3), or if any `d` in `face_dims` is not an `int` (a `bool` is not accepted) in `1 … q − 1`.
- Both are exported from `torchoed`, with an API entry, a guide snippet and a CHANGELOG line.

## 47. Not in this section

Optimal mixture designs with Scheffé models and certificates (§45 candidates feed `optimal_design` in part 2), mixture-process variables, general linear constraints (non-box regions), ABCD designs, pseudo-components and GPU timing.

## 48. Scheffé mixture models and optimal mixture designs (W11 D1, part 2)

1. **Scheffé model columns (definition).** For mixture rows `X ∈ ℝ^{m×q}`, `q ≥ 2`, the Scheffé canonical polynomials have no intercept. Columns, in this order:
   - `"linear"`: `x_0, …, x_{q−1}` (`p = q`).
   - `"quadratic"`: the linear columns, then `x_i x_j` for `i < j` in lexicographic order of `(i, j)` (`p = q + C(q, 2)`).
   - `"special_cubic"`: the quadratic columns, then `x_i x_j x_k` for `i < j < k` in lexicographic order (`p = q + C(q, 2) + C(q, 3)`).
2. **Why no intercept (PROVED).** On `Σ x = 1` the intercept equals `Σ_i x_i`, and `x_i² = x_i − Σ_{j≠i} x_i x_j`. So adding an intercept or squares to the quadratic Scheffé columns makes the model matrix rank-deficient on any mixture candidate set, and the Scheffé quadratic spans the same functions on the simplex as the full quadratic.
3. **Optimal mixture designs (decision; no new search).** An optimal mixture design is `optimal_design(scheffe_features(X, model), n, criterion=...)` on mixture candidates `X` (for example `simplex_lattice`, `simplex_centroid` or `mixture_vertices` rows, concatenated). The exchange engine, `fixed_ids` and the efficiency certificate apply unchanged, because they take any candidate feature matrix `F` with `rank F = p` and need no intercept. The I criterion keeps its existing candidate-average moment matrix `B = FᵀF/m`, the average over the candidate rows, not the integral over the region (decision; say so in the guide).
4. **Well-posedness (OBSERVED; Architect, 2026-11-17, sandbox).** On the 13 §45.8 R1 candidates (6 vertices, 6 edge centroids, the overall centroid) the quadratic Scheffé matrix has exact `Fraction` rank 6. The special-cubic matrix of `simplex_centroid(3)` has rank 7.
5. **Exhaustive oracle (PROVED exact).** `optimal_design` searches exact designs without replacement: `ids` are distinct candidate IDs (M1/M2 contract A3). For a candidate set of size `m` and `n` runs, the exact optimum is the best of the `C(m, n)` subsets. A replicated run is a repeated candidate row with its own ID: appending `r` copies of row `j` allows up to `r + 1` runs at that point. For `m = 13`, `n = 6, 7` that is 1 716 subsets each. (Amended 2026-11-18, #284: the earlier multiset count `C(m + n − 1, n)` described replication that the engine does not search; on R1 with `n = 7` the A optimum over multisets, 2208.2256 at `{1, 3, 4, 5, 7, 12, 12}`, is below the subset optimum 2472.3874.)
6. **Cost.** `scheffe_features` is `O(m p)`. The search cost is that of `optimal_design` with `p` columns.

## 49. API decision: `scheffe_features`

- `scheffe_features(X, model="quadratic", *, max_elements=1_000_000) -> Tensor` returns the §48.1 columns in that order, with the dtype and device of `X`, and preserves autograd.
- `X` is a floating 2-D tensor with at least 2 columns and only finite values; `model` is one of `"linear"`, `"quadratic"`, `"special_cubic"`; the output shape is checked against `max_elements` as `polynomial_features` does. Otherwise `ValueError` (`TypeError` if `X` is not a floating tensor). Rows are not checked for `Σ x = 1`: mixture-process crossing (part 3) may pass pseudo-components.
- Exported from `torchoed`, with an API entry, a guide snippet (an optimal 10-run D design on `mixture_vertices` candidates with its certificate) and a CHANGELOG line.

## 50. Not in this section

Mixture-process variables (part 3), region-integrated I moments, non-box regions, ABCD, pseudo-components, Cox or log-contrast models, GPU timing.

## 51. G, V and I as criterion functions (W11 D3, part 1)

All three functions are maximisation-oriented, like `d_optimality` … `c_optimality`: each returns the negative of a quantity to be minimised. `M` is a symmetric positive definite `(p, p)` or `(B, p, p)` floating tensor, checked exactly as `a_optimality` checks it (same Cholesky pivot rule, no ridge). Solves use the Cholesky factor `L` of `M`; no explicit inverse.

1. **Prediction variance (definition).** For a row `f ∈ ℝ^p`, `d(f; M) = fᵀ M⁻¹ f = ‖L⁻¹ f‖²`. For candidate rows `F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}` (or `(B, m, p)`, matched to `M`'s batch), `d_i = d(F_i; M)`.
2. **I (definition).** `i_optimality(M, W) = −tr(M⁻¹ W)` for a symmetric positive semidefinite moment matrix `W` of the same shape as `M` (checked symmetric and finite with the same rule as `M`; PSD is not checked). With `W = FᵀF/m`, `tr(M⁻¹ W)` is the candidate-average quantity that `optimal_design(criterion="I")` minimises, so it maximises `i_optimality`.
3. **G (definition).** `g_optimality(M, F) = −max_i d_i`.
4. **V (definition).** `v_optimality(M, F) = −(1/m) Σ_i d_i`.
5. **V = I with the candidate moment matrix (PROVED).** `(1/m) Σ_i F_iᵀ M⁻¹ F_i = (1/m) tr(M⁻¹ Σ_i F_i F_iᵀ) = tr(M⁻¹ FᵀF/m)`, by linearity and cyclicity of the trace. So `v_optimality(M, F) = i_optimality(M, FᵀF/m)`.
6. **Kiefer–Wolfowitz lower bound for G (PROVED).** Let the design have `n` rows `X ∈ ℝ^{n×p}`, each equal to some row of `F`, and `M = XᵀX/n` nonsingular. Then `(1/n) Σ_j X_jᵀ M⁻¹ X_j = tr(M⁻¹ XᵀX/n) = tr(I_p) = p`. A maximum is at least a mean, and every `X_j` is a row of `F`, so `max_i d_i ≥ p`. Hence the G-efficiency `p / max_i d_i = p / (−g_optimality(M, F))` lies in `(0, 1]`. The bound needs both preconditions: every design row is a row of `F`, and `M` is exactly `XᵀX/n` (no ridge, no other scaling). `g_optimality` never sees `X` and cannot check them; the docstring says that an `F` omitting a design row can give a ratio above 1. With unnormalised `M = XᵀX` the same argument gives `max_i d_i ≥ p/n`, so the efficiency is `p / (n max_i d_i)`. Repeated design rows do not affect the argument.
7. **Cost.** One Cholesky `O(p³)` and one triangular solve with `m` right-hand sides `O(m p²)`; `i_optimality` solves with `p` right-hand sides.

## 52. API decision: `g_optimality`, `v_optimality`, `i_optimality`

- `g_optimality(M, F, *, max_elements=1_000_000) -> Tensor`, `v_optimality(M, F, *, max_elements=1_000_000) -> Tensor`, `i_optimality(M, W, *, max_elements=1_000_000) -> Tensor`, in `src/torchoed/criteria.py`, exported from `torchoed`.
- Shapes: `F` is `(m, p)` with `M` `(p, p)`, or `(B, m, p)` with `M` `(B, p, p)`; `m ≥ 1`; no broadcasting. `W` has exactly `M`'s shape. `F` and `W` must have `M`'s dtype and device and only finite values; `W` must be symmetric (same rule as `M`). `max_elements` has the meaning it has in `c_optimality`: each input's shape is checked against it. Otherwise `ValueError` (`TypeError` for a non-tensor or a dtype mismatch), as `c_optimality` does for `c`. The output has shape `()` or `(B,)`.
- Not in this section: G or V as `optimal_design` search criteria, region-integrated moments, compound criteria (D3, part 2).

## 53. Whole plots nested in fixed blocks: D, A and I (W11 D2, part 1)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D2.** Status: `P2_D2A_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Whole plots, labels, feasibility, `c_k = η/(1 + ηn_k)`, moves and start as in §7, §8, §16, §17 and §25; fixed blocks as in §1 and §12. This section supersedes the "`blocks` with `whole_plots` still raises" line of §26 and the "blocks together with whole plots" items of §18, §24 and §27.

Model `y = Fθ + Z_bβ + Zγ + ε` for a design of `n` rows in `w` whole plots of sizes `n₁…n_w` (rows of plot `k` contiguous, plot `k` holding positions `n₁ + … + n_{k−1}` to `n₁ + … + n_k − 1`), grouped into `g` blocks of sizes `N₁…N_g` (runs; `Σ N_b = n`). Every block is a union of consecutive whole plots: each block boundary `N₁ + … + N_b` is a plot boundary. `Z_b[n, g]` is the 0/1 block-indicator matrix, `β` free fixed block effects, `Z[n, w]` the plot-indicator matrix, `γ ~ (0, ησ²I_w)` independent of `ε ~ (0, σ²I_n)`, `η > 0` known, `V = I + ηZZᵀ`. `F` must not contain an intercept (it is absorbed by the blocks). For plot `k` in block `b(k)`: `s_k = Σ_{i∈k} fᵢ`, `w_k = 1/(1 + ηn_k)`; for block `b`: `u_b = Σ_{k∈b} w_k s_k`, `τ_b = Σ_{k∈b} w_k n_k`.

1. **Information (PROVED).** The GLS information for `θ` after eliminating `β` is `M_C = FₛᵀPFₛ`, `P = V⁻¹ − V⁻¹Z_b(Z_bᵀV⁻¹Z_b)⁻¹Z_bᵀV⁻¹`. Closed form: `M_C = Σ_k [F_kᵀF_k − c_k s_k s_kᵀ] − Σ_b u_b u_bᵀ/τ_b`. Proof: by §7.1 `V⁻¹ = ⊕_k (I − c_k11ᵀ)`, and `(I − c_k11ᵀ)1 = w_k1`, so `Z_bᵀV⁻¹Fₛ` has row `b` equal to `u_bᵀ` and `Z_bᵀV⁻¹Z_b = diag(τ_b)`. Fraction check (Architect): closed form equals the dense `FₛᵀPFₛ` in 30/30 random cases (`p = 2, 3`; plots/blocks `[2,2,3]/[4,3]`, `[3,2,2,1]/[5,3]`, `[2,2,2]/[2,4]`, `[1,1,1,1]/[2,2]`; rational `η ≥ 0`).
2. **Reductions (PROVED).**
   - **Singleton plots.** If every `n_k = 1`, then `c_k = η/(1 + η)`, `w_k = 1/(1 + η)`, `τ_b = N_b/(1 + η)` and `M_C = M_B/(1 + η)`, `M_B` of §1.1. With `η = 0` (allowed in this identity only; the API requires `η > 0`) `M_C = M_B` exactly. Fraction check: 20/20.
   - **One block.** If `g = 1`, then the §7.1 information of the features `[1, F]` is `M_W = [[τ, uᵀ], [u, M_S]]`, `M_S` the §7.1 information of `F`, and `M_C = M_S − u uᵀ/τ` is the Schur complement of `τ` in `M_W`. Hence `det M_W = τ · det M_C` and, when `M_C ≻ 0`, `(M_W⁻¹)[1:, 1:] = M_C⁻¹`. Fraction check: 30/30 (`p = 3`, plots `[2,3,1]`, `[3,3]`, `[1,2,2,2]`). This is also the closed form of §19's whole-plot `C` with `X = F`.
3. **Rank and domination (PROVED).** `P Z_b = 0` and `P = V⁻¹ − (PSD)`, so `P ≼ V⁻¹ ≼ I`. For any `A ∈ ℝ^{g×p}`, `M_C = (Fₛ − Z_bA)ᵀP(Fₛ − Z_bA) ≼ (Fₛ − Z_bA)ᵀ(Fₛ − Z_bA)`. With every row of `A` equal to a constant `a`: `M_C ≼ Σ_{i∈S} (fᵢ − a)(fᵢ − a)ᵀ`. `M_C ≻ 0` iff `[Z_b Fₛ]` has full column rank `g + p` (`V^{−1/2}` is invertible and `P` is `V^{−1/2}` times the projector off `V^{−1/2}Z_b` times `V^{−1/2}`); hence `n ≥ g + p` is necessary, and a column of `F` that is constant within every block (an intercept, or a hard-to-change column when every block holds one label) makes `M_C` singular. Fraction check (Architect): the domination quadratic form was nonnegative on 1500/1500 random vectors over 30 designs with random rational shifts.
4. **Within-plot exchange is a rank-2 update with `det K = −1` (PROVED).** In plot `k` of block `b`, replace the row `f_o` at position `o` by a candidate `f_j`, `d = f_j − f_o`. With `q = FₛᵀPe_o = f_o − c_k s_k − w_k u_b/τ_b` and `κ = e_oᵀPe_o = 1 − c_k − w_k²/τ_b`: `ΔM_C = d qᵀ + q dᵀ + κ d dᵀ = W K Wᵀ`, `W = [d q]`, `K = [[κ, 1], [1, 0]]`, `det K = −1`, `K⁻¹ = [[0, 1], [1, −κ]]`. This is §16.2 with `(1 − c, f_o − cs)` replaced by `(κ, q)`. The ratio, the gain `γ`, `N = K⁻¹ + G` and the positive-definiteness rule are exactly §16.3 (the §12.4 argument needs only `det K < 0`). Fraction check (Architect): `q`, `κ` and `ΔM_C = WKWᵀ` exact against the dense `P` in 30/30 random exchanges.
5. **Criteria (decision).** D: maximize `logdet M_C`. A and I: minimize `φ_B(M_C) = tr(B M_C⁻¹)` with **A**: `B = I_p`; **I**: `B = W_c` of §12.1 (centered, because the mean response is not estimable with fixed blocks). Public sign as in the W1 gate.
6. **Moves (decision).** Exactly §7.5 and §16.4: within-plot exchange with an unused candidate of the plot's label, and the greedy relabel; both scored by item 4. Blocks never change: a plot stays in its block, and no move changes `n_k`, `N_b` or the plot-to-block map. No between-plot or between-block interchange. Local optimality is only with respect to these two moves. `fixed_ids` follows §25 for whole plots unchanged (fixed positions `0 … n_f − 1`, the §25.2 label rule, only free positions change, no relabel of a plot with a fixed position).
7. **Floor (PROVED).** By item 3 with `a = f̄` (the mean of all `m` candidate rows), `M_C(S) ≼ n·M̄_g(S)` with `g_j = f_j − f̄`, which is the §22.2 blocks domination. So §22.3 applies verbatim with `G = F − f̄`, and the `_dual_floor` I-matrix `GᵀG/m` equals `W_c` (§22.4). Positive definiteness of the first iterate: as §22.5 (blocks), since each `fᵢ − f̄` is some `g_j`. With `fixed_ids` the floor stays valid (§25.5).
8. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-18).** The §7.6 candidates (`(h, x)`, `h ∈ {−1, 1}`, `x ∈ {−1, 0, 1}`, in this order, listed twice, `m = 12`, IDs 0–11), `F = [h, x, x², h·x]` (no intercept, `p = 4`), labels `h`, `W_c` of §12.1. Optima over all feasible designs (distinct IDs, plots of constant label):
   - (C-a) whole plots `[2, 2, 2, 2]`, blocks `[4, 4]`, `η = 1`: `max det M_C = 640/9`, `min φ_A = 177/103`, `min φ_I = 127/144`.
   - (C-b) whole plots `[2, 2, 2]`, blocks `[2, 4]`, `η = 1/2`: `max det M_C = 16`, `min φ_A = 19/8`, `min φ_I = 97/72`.
9. **Complexity (target).** As §16.7: one Cholesky of `M_C` per iteration; exchange scores `O(m p² + n m p)` (apply `M_C⁻¹` and `M_C⁻¹BM_C⁻¹` once to the candidate rows, the plot sums and the block vectors `u_b`); each relabel proposal `O(n_k (p³ + m p²))`; no `m×m` or `n×n` matrices; workspace checked against `max_elements`.

## 54. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, blocks=…, whole_plots=…, hard=…, eta=…)`

- `blocks` and `whole_plots` may now be given together, for criteria D, A and I, with or without `fixed_ids`. `blocks` is validated as in §2 (positive Python ints, `sum(blocks) == n`), and every partial sum `N₁ + … + N_b` must be a partial sum of `whole_plots`, else `ValueError`. `n ≥ len(blocks) + p` is required (necessary only, item 3), else `ValueError`. `whole_plots`, `hard`, `eta`, `fixed_ids` and CPU float64 are validated as in §8 and §26.
- Start: exactly the §8 / §25.4 whole-plot draw procedure (the same sequence of draws for the same `generator`, whatever `blocks` is); a draw is rejected and redrawn if `M_C` fails the blocked start rule (Cholesky plus pivot floor), with the §8 failure count and error, so the accepted start can differ from the call without `blocks`. An `F` with an intercept (or any column constant within every block) is not checked up front: every draw is singular and the §8 "No feasible start …" error is raised.
- Search: the §17 order and acceptance rule (exchanges first, then relabels; dense recomputation of `M_C` by item 1 and the `_tolerance` test), scored by item 4.
- Result: the §8 keys plus `blocks` (the sizes). `ids` plot by plot, each plot sorted ascending; `objective = logdet M_C` (D) or `−φ_B(M_C)` (A, I); `certificate="LOCAL_SPLIT_PLOT_EXCHANGE"` if no improving move remains else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`; `efficiency_lower_bound = _dual_floor(F − F.mean(0), M_C, n, criterion, 0.0)` (item 7).
- Without `blocks`, or without `whole_plots`, results are byte-identical to `main`.

## 55. Not in this section

Structure-aware diagnostics and power with blocks and whole plots together (D2 part 2), covariates (D2 part 3), random block effects, between-plot or between-block interchange, other block layouts (a plot split across blocks), split-split-plot, unknown `η`, GPU or float32.

## 56. Effect-group F-test power (W11 D3, part 2)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D3.** Status: `P2_D3F_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as in §11 (unstructured: `S = F[ids]`, `M = SᵀS`, `ν = n − p`), §19.4 (blocks: `M_B`, `ν = n − g − p`) and §19.5 (whole plots: the GLS `M`, whole-plot / subplot classification, `νⱼ` by the containment rule). `σ² = 1`; `δ ∈ ℝ^p` is the `effect` vector of §11.9. This section supersedes the "joint (multi-degree-of-freedom) F-test power" item of §11.10 and the "joint F-test power" item of §21.

1. **Definition (decision).** A group is a set `G` of `r ≥ 1` distinct coefficient indices. The level-`α` Wald F-test of `θ_G = 0` rejects when `F_G = θ̂_Gᵀ[(M⁻¹)_{GG}]⁻¹θ̂_G / (r s²) > F_{1−α}(r, ν_G)`. Here `M` is the information whose inverse diagonal gives the per-coefficient `λⱼ` (the matrix of §11.9, §19.4 or §19.5), `ν_G` is the common `νⱼ` of the members of `G`, and `s²` is the stratum residual mean square on `ν_G` degrees of freedom divided by that stratum's error variance under the model. That variance is 1 for unstructured designs, blocks and subplot groups, and `(1 + ηk)/k` for whole-plot groups in the exact case of §19.5. Outside the exact case, the statistic is only approximately F′ (§56.2). Equivalently, the whole-plot test is the ordinary F-test of the regression of the plot means on the plot rows of `S_w`. `λ_G` is the same in every case. Only `λ_G` and `ν_G` enter `group_power`; no `s²` is computed. Under `θ_G = δ_G`, `group_power = P(F′(r, ν_G, λ_G) > F_{1−α}(r, ν_G))` with `λ_G = δ_Gᵀ[(M⁻¹)_{GG}]⁻¹δ_G`. If `δ_G = 0` then `group_power = α`.
2. **Exactness (PROVED / decision).**
   - **Unstructured and blocks (PROVED).** `θ̂ ~ N(θ, M⁻¹)` is independent of `ν s² ~ χ²_ν` (normal errors; §19.4 for blocks). So `θ̂_G ~ N(δ_G, (M⁻¹)_{GG})`, and the numerator `θ̂_Gᵀ[(M⁻¹)_{GG}]⁻¹θ̂_G ~ χ′²(r, λ_G)` (a quadratic form in the inverse covariance). Hence `F_G ~ F′(r, ν, λ_G)` exactly.
   - **Whole plots, exact case of §19.5 (PROVED).** All members of `G` lie in one stratum, and the plot-row matrix of `S_w` has full column rank. That stratum's estimator is `N(δ_G, (M⁻¹)_{GG})` (block-diagonal `M`), and it is independent of the stratum's residual mean square on `ν_G` degrees of freedom. With `s²` normalised as in item 1, the same argument applies.
   - **Whole plots otherwise (decision: APPROXIMATE).** The same `λ_G` and `ν_G` are used, as in §19.5.
   - **Mixed strata (decision).** A group containing both whole-plot and subplot coefficients has no common `ν_G` and raises `ValueError`.
3. **One-term reduction (PROVED).** For `G = {j}`: `[(M⁻¹)_{GG}]⁻¹ = 1/(M⁻¹)ⱼⱼ`, so `λ_G = δⱼ²/(M⁻¹)ⱼⱼ = λⱼ`, and `F′(1, ν, λ)` is the law of `T²` in §11.1. So `group_power` equals `powerⱼ`.
4. **Beta form (PROVED; standard noncentral-F / Beta mixture).** If `Y ~ F′(r, ν, λ)`, then `X = rY/(rY + ν)` is a Poisson(`μ = λ/2`) mixture of `Beta(r/2 + k, ν/2)`, `k ≥ 0`. The map `f ↦ rf/(rf + ν)` is increasing. So the critical point `x*` solves `I_{x*}(r/2, ν/2) = 1 − α`, `F_{1−α}(r, ν) = ν x*/(r(1 − x*))`, and `group_power = Σ_k w_k (1 − I_{x*}(r/2 + k, ν/2))` with `w_k` of §11.4.
5. **Finite sums (PROVED; recurrences of §11.3).** Let `a₀ = 1/2` (r odd) or `1` (r even), and `b₀ = 1/2` (ν odd) or `1` (ν even). The bases are `I_x(1/2, 1/2) = (2/π) asin √x`, `I_x(1/2, 1) = √x`, `I_x(1, 1/2) = 1 − √(1 − x)` and `I_x(1, 1) = x`.
   - The base value is `I_x(a₀, ν/2) = I_x(a₀, b₀) + Σ_{i=0}^{(ν − 2b₀)/2 − 1} U(a₀, b₀ + i)`. Sums whose upper limit is `−1` are empty.
   - The power uses `Q₀ = (1 − I_x(a₀, ν/2)) + Σ_{i=0}^{(r − 2a₀)/2 − 1} T(a₀ + i, ν/2)`, which equals `1 − I_x(r/2, ν/2)`, and `Q_{k+1} = Q_k + T(r/2 + k, ν/2)`, which equals `1 − I_x(r/2 + k + 1, ν/2)`. All added terms are nonnegative. The only subtraction is `1 − I_x(a₀, ν/2)`, as in §11.
   - The quantile `x*` is found by the §11.6 bisection (64 steps on `[0, 1]`) on `Q₀(x) = α`, where `Q₀` is continuous and decreasing in `x`. Truncation is as §11.5; the bound does not depend on `r`.
   - For `r = 1`, `a₀ = 1/2` and the empty `T` sum make items 4–5 exactly the §11.2–11.6 computation, so the implementation may generalise `_coefficient_power` with an `r` argument. Implementation decision: the `r = 1` path performs no extra floating-point operations other than exact ones (such as `×1`), so `power` stays byte-identical.
6. **Architect check (OBSERVED, 2026-11-18, plain float64, SciPy 1.18.1 as a dev-only reference).** Items 4–5 agree with `scipy.stats.ncf.sf(f.isf(α, r, ν), r, ν, λ)` (and `α` at `λ = 0`) to `1.1·10⁻¹⁰` max abs on 1164 cases. The cases are a grid `r ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 10}` × `ν ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 30, 200}` × `λ ∈ {0, 10⁻⁸, 4, 100, 10³, 10⁴}` × `α ∈ {0.001, 0.01, 0.05, 0.5}`, plus 300 random cases with `r ≤ 12`, `ν ≤ 200`, `λ ≤ 10⁴` and `α ∈ [0.001, 0.5]`. `ν x*/(r(1 − x*))` matches `scipy.stats.f.isf` to relative `6.8·10⁻¹⁰`. For `r = 1`, the result equals the two-sided noncentral-t power `P(|T′(ν, √λ)| > t_{1−α/2})` to `10⁻¹⁵`.
7. **2ᵏ oracle (PROVED).** For `F = [1, x₁ … x_k]` on the full coded 2ᵏ factorial (`k ≥ 2`): `(M⁻¹)_{GG} = 2⁻ᵏ I_r`, so `λ_G = 2ᵏ Σ_{j∈G} δⱼ²`. For the §19.6 blocked 2³ (`F = [A, B, C]` on all 8 runs, blocks `[4, 4]` confounded with `ABC`, `g = 2`, `p = 3`, `M_B = 8I₃`): `λ_G = 8 Σ_{j∈G} δⱼ²`, `ν = 3`.
8. **Cost.** For each group, one `r × r` Cholesky of `(M⁻¹)_{GG}` (from `cholesky_inverse(L)`, already formed for `power`) plus `O(ν + r + K)` for the sums and `64·(ν + r)` for the bisection.

## 57. API decision: `design_diagnostics(…, effect=…, groups=…)`

- `groups` is `None` (default: results byte-identical to `main`) or a non-empty list of non-empty lists of distinct Python ints in `[0, p)`. Groups may overlap. Otherwise `ValueError`. `groups` requires `effect`, else `ValueError`.
- The dict gains `group_power`, a `(len(groups),)` tensor in `F`'s dtype and device, in the given group order. With `blocks` or `whole_plots` it also gains `group_df`, an int64 tensor of the `ν_G` used. A whole-plot group with mixed strata raises `ValueError` (§56.2). `alpha` and the `ν ≥ 1` rules are as in §11.9 and §20.
- `power` and `df` are unchanged.

## 58. Not in this section

Kenward–Roger or Satterthwaite degrees of freedom, general linear hypotheses `Lθ = 0` beyond coordinate groups, sample-size search, Monte Carlo or GLM power (week 15), power with blocks and whole plots together (D2 part 2), one-sided tests, and a public incomplete-beta or quantile function.

## 59. Mixture-process variables (W11 D1, part 3)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D1.** Status: `P2_D1MP_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Scheffé columns as in §48; mixture candidates as in §45 (`simplex_centroid`, `mixture_vertices`) or any other mixture rows; process candidates and process features are user-supplied (for example `ff2n`, `full_factorial` or `feasible_candidates` rows passed through `polynomial_features`). This section supersedes the "Mixture-process variables (part 3)" item of §50.

1. **Crossed model (definition; Cornell 2002, *Experiments with Mixtures*, 3rd ed., ch. 7, combined model).** Let `S ∈ ℝ^{m₁×p₁}` be Scheffé features of `m₁` mixture candidates and `P ∈ ℝ^{m₂×p₂}` features of `m₂` process candidates. The crossed model of a run at mixture candidate `i` and process candidate `j` is `f(i, j) = S_i ⊗ P_j ∈ ℝ^{p₁p₂}`, with coordinate `a·p₂ + b` equal to `S_{ia} P_{jb}`. If the column span of `P` (over the process candidates) contains the constant, the span of the crossed model contains every Scheffé term on its own; this holds in particular when `P` has a column of `1`s (decision: the user chooses `P`).
2. **Crossed candidates (definition).** The crossed candidate set is all pairs `(i, j)`, with crossed candidate ID `i·m₂ + j`. Its feature matrix is the Kronecker product `F = S ⊗ P ∈ ℝ^{(m₁m₂)×(p₁p₂)}`, whose row `i·m₂ + j` is `S_i ⊗ P_j`. Mixture index `i = id // m₂`, process index `j = id % m₂`.
3. **Rank (PROVED).** `rank(S ⊗ P) = rank S · rank P`. Hence `F` has full column rank `p₁p₂` iff `S` and `P` both have full column rank.
4. **Product information (PROVED).** For the full crossed design (every crossed candidate once), `FᵀF = (SᵀS) ⊗ (PᵀP)` (mixed-product rule), so `det FᵀF = det(SᵀS)^{p₂} · det(PᵀP)^{p₁}`. More generally, for approximate designs `ξ` on the mixture candidates and `ζ` on the process candidates, the product design `ξ × ζ` has `M(ξ × ζ) = M(ξ) ⊗ M(ζ)`. Its variance function factorises: `(s ⊗ r)ᵀ(M(ξ) ⊗ M(ζ))⁻¹(s ⊗ r) = (sᵀM(ξ)⁻¹s)(rᵀM(ζ)⁻¹r)`. If `ξ` and `ζ` are D-optimal on their candidate sets, both factors are at most `p₁` and `p₂`, the product is at most `p₁p₂`, and by the Kiefer–Wolfowitz equivalence theorem `ξ × ζ` is D-optimal on the crossed candidates. Consequence for diagnostics: on the full crossed design with region = all crossed candidates, the §11 scaled prediction variance `n fᵀM⁻¹f` at crossed candidate `i·m₂ + j` equals `spv_S(i) · spv_P(j)`, the product of the two component SPVs (each with `n` its own run count). Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-18): on the 13 §45.8 R1 candidates (quadratic Scheffé, `p₁ = 6`) crossed with the 2² factorial (`P = [1, z₁, z₂]`, `p₂ = 3`, `m = 52`): `det FᵀF = det(SᵀS)³ · det(PᵀP)⁶` exactly, `FᵀF = (SᵀS) ⊗ (PᵀP)` entrywise, and the SPV product holds at all 52 candidates.
5. **Search, certificate and diagnostics (decision; no new search).** An optimal mixture-process design is `optimal_design(mixture_process_features(S, P), n, criterion=…)`. The exchange engine, `fixed_ids`, the dual efficiency floor and `design_diagnostics` apply unchanged, because they accept any candidate feature matrix `F` with `rank F = p` (item 3), with or without an intercept. The I moment is the existing candidate average `B = FᵀF/m`, which equals `(SᵀS/m₁) ⊗ (PᵀP/m₂)` (item 4); it is the candidate average, not the region integral (as §48.3). Designs are without replacement (§48.5). Appending a copy of a row of `S` duplicates its `m₂` crossed candidates, and a copy of a row of `P` duplicates `m₁`; to replicate one crossed run only, append the row `S_i ⊗ P_j` to `F` itself.
6. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-18).** `I` uses `B = FᵀF/m` of item 5. Optima over all `C(m, n)` subsets of distinct crossed IDs:
   - (MP-a) `S = scheffe_features(simplex_centroid(3), "linear")` (`m₁ = 7`, `p₁ = 3`), `P = [[1, −1], [1, 1]]` (`m₂ = 2`, `p₂ = 2`), `m = 14`, `p = 6`. `n = 7`: `max det = 96`, `min φ_A = 17/6`, `min φ_I = 139/216`. `n = 8`: `max det = 144`, `min φ_A = 8/3`, `min φ_I = 451/756`.
   - (MP-a, `fixed_ids = [12, 13]`, the overall centroid at both process levels), `n = 8`: over completions, `max det = 1024/9`, `min φ_A = 11/4`, `min φ_I = 17/28`.
   - (MP-b) `S = scheffe_features(simplex_centroid(2), "quadratic")` (`m₁ = 3`, `p₁ = 3`), `P = [[1, −1], [1, 0], [1, 1]]` (`m₂ = 3`, `p₂ = 2`), `m = 9`, `p = 6`, `n = 7`: `max det = 3/8`, `min φ_A = 70/3`, `min φ_I = 7/9`.
7. **Cost.** `mixture_process_features` is one Kronecker product, `O(m₁m₂p₁p₂)` time and memory, checked against `max_elements` before it is formed. The search cost is that of `optimal_design` with `m = m₁m₂` candidates and `p = p₁p₂` columns.

## 60. API decision: `mixture_process_features`

- `mixture_process_features(S, P, *, max_elements=1_000_000) -> Tensor` returns `S ⊗ P` (item 2 order), with the dtype and device of `S`, and preserves autograd.
- `S` and `P` are floating 2-D tensors with the same dtype and device and at least one row and one column each; the output shape `(m₁m₂, p₁p₂)` is checked against `max_elements` as `polynomial_features` does. Otherwise `ValueError` (`TypeError` if either is not a floating tensor). Rows of `S` are not checked for `Σ x = 1`, and rank is not checked (the search reports a singular start as today).
- Exported from `torchoed`, with an API entry, a guide snippet in the mixture section (a D-optimal design on R1 `mixture_vertices` candidates crossed with the 2² factorial, with its certificate and the `id // m₂`, `id % m₂` decoding) and a CHANGELOG line.

## 61. Not in this section

Split-plot mixture-process designs (mixture or process factors hard to change), reduced crossed models (dropping selected cross-products), process variables that are themselves mixtures, region-integrated I moments, GPU timing.

## 62. Covariates: fixed per-run values, D, A and I (W11 D2, part 3)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D2.** Status: `P2_D2C_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above; criteria, kernel algebra and floors as in §12, §16.3, §22 and §53.

Model `y = β₀1 + Fₛθ + Zζ + ε` for a design of `n` runs in a fixed run order. `Fₛ[n, p]` holds the chosen candidate rows, with row `i` belonging to run `i`. `Z[n, c]` (`c ≥ 1`) holds the covariate values of the runs. They are known, fixed and not chosen by the design (JMP Covariates). `β₀` and `ζ` are free nuisance parameters, and `ε ~ (0, σ²I_n)`. `F` must not contain an intercept, which is absorbed by `X = [1 Z]`. Write `P = X(XᵀX)⁻¹Xᵀ`, `Q = I − P` and `R = QFₛ` (`r_i` is row `i` of `R`).

1. **Information (PROVED).** The information for `θ` after eliminating `(β₀, ζ)` is `M_Z = FₛᵀQFₛ = RᵀR`, since `Q` is a symmetric idempotent. With `X̃ = [1 Fₛ Z]`, `det X̃ᵀX̃ = det XᵀX · det M_Z` (Schur complement; this holds also when `M_Z` is singular), and if `M_Z ≻ 0` the `θ` block of `(X̃ᵀX̃)⁻¹` is `M_Z⁻¹`. `XᵀX` does not depend on the design, so maximizing `det X̃ᵀX̃` and maximizing `det M_Z` are the same problem. Fraction check (Architect): 30/30 random cases (`n = 6…8`, `p = 2, 3`, `c = 1, 2`, rational entries): `RᵀR` equals the dense `FₛᵀQFₛ`, and the determinant and inverse-block identities hold.
2. **Blocks reduction (PROVED).** Take blocks of sizes `N₁…N_g`, all `N_b ≥ 1`, with runs contiguous, and let `Z` be the 0/1 indicators of blocks `2…g`. Then `span X` is the span of the block indicators, and `M_Z = M_B` of §1.1 exactly. Fraction check: 20/20 for blocks `[3,4]`, `[2,3,2]` and `[4,4]`.
3. **Rank and domination (PROVED).** `M_Z ≻ 0` iff `[X Fₛ]` has full column rank `1 + c + p`. Hence `n ≥ 1 + c + p` is necessary, and `F` with an intercept column makes `M_Z` singular. Since `Q1 = 0`, for any constant row `a`, `M_Z = (Fₛ − 1aᵀ)ᵀQ(Fₛ − 1aᵀ) ≼ Σ_{i} (fᵢ − a)(fᵢ − a)ᵀ` (`Q ≼ I`). Fraction check: the quadratic form was nonnegative in 30/30 random cases with random rational shifts.
4. **Moves are rank-2 updates with `det K = −1` (PROVED).** Let `K = [[κ, 1], [1, 0]]` and `W = [d q]`. Then `ΔM_Z = d qᵀ + q dᵀ + κ d dᵀ = W K Wᵀ` with:
   - **(i) Exchange:** at run `o`, replace `f_o` by an unused candidate `f_j`: `d = f_j − f_o`, `q = r_o`, `κ = Q_oo = 1 − x_oᵀ(XᵀX)⁻¹x_o`.
   - **(ii) Interchange:** swap the candidates of runs `o ≠ o′`: `d = f_{o′} − f_o`, `q = r_o − r_{o′}`, `κ = Q_oo + Q_{o′o′} − 2Q_{oo′}`.

   This holds because `(I − P)` is applied to a change of `Fₛ` of `e_o dᵀ` in (i) and `(e_o − e_{o′})dᵀ` in (ii). With `G = WᵀM_Z⁻¹W = [[a, b], [b, e]]`, the ratio is `det M_Z′ / det M_Z = det(I₂ + KG) = 1 + κa + 2b − (ae − b²)`. The A/I gain `γ = tr(N⁻¹H_B)`, with `N = K⁻¹ + G` and `H_B = WᵀM_Z⁻¹BM_Z⁻¹W`, and the positive-definiteness rule are exactly §16.3 (the §12.4 argument needs only `det K < 0`). Fraction check (Architect): 30/30 exchanges and 40/40 interchanges matched `ΔM_Z = WKWᵀ`, the ratio against the direct `det M_Z′ / det M_Z`, and (interchanges) `tr M_Z⁻¹ − γ` against the direct `tr M_Z′⁻¹`.
5. **Criteria (decision).** D: maximize `logdet M_Z`. A and I: minimize `φ_B(M_Z) = tr(B M_Z⁻¹)`, with **A**: `B = I_p`, **I**: `B = W_c` of §12.1. `W_c` is centered because the criterion is the candidate-average variance of the factor contrast `(f(x) − f̄)ᵀθ̂`, which does not depend on `β₀`, `ζ` or the covariate values. Public sign as in the W1 gate.
6. **Moves and order (decision).** Free runs are those at positions `n_f … n − 1`. Moves are (i) exchange at a free run with an unused candidate, then (ii) interchange of two free runs `o < o′`. Both are scored by item 4 and generated, accepted and checked as `_block_moves` does for §1.3–§1.4 (first improvement, dense recomputation of `M_Z` by item 1, and the `_tolerance` test). The covariate rows never move; only the candidate assigned to a run changes. IDs are distinct, as everywhere. Local optimality holds only with respect to these two moves. `fixed_ids` occupy runs `0 … n_f − 1` in the given order, with their covariate rows, and never move.
7. **Floor (PROVED).** By item 3 with `a = f̄` (the mean of all `m` candidate rows), `M_Z(S) ≼ n·M̄_g(S)` with `g_j = f_j − f̄`, which is the §22.2 domination. So §22.3 applies verbatim with `G = F − f̄`, and the `_dual_floor` I-matrix `GᵀG/m` equals `W_c` (§22.4). With `fixed_ids` the floor stays valid (§25.5).
8. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-18).** The candidates are the six points `(h, x)`, `h ∈ {−1, 1}`, `x ∈ {−1, 0, 1}`, in this order, listed once (`m = 6`, IDs 0–5). Run order matters, and all ordered selections of distinct IDs were enumerated (720 each).
   - **(V-a)** `F = [h, x, h·x]`, `n = 5`, `Z = [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]ᵀ` (a linear time trend). 96 designs are singular. `max det M_Z = 1568/25`, `min φ_A = 149/196` and `min φ_I = 7/12`, each attained by 16 designs, for example `ids = [0, 2, 3, 5, 1]`.
   - **(V-b)** `F = [h, x, x²]`, `n = 6`, `Z` with rows `(t, (−1)ᵗ)` for `t = 0…5`. 252 designs are singular. `max det M_Z = 256/9`, `min φ_A = 19/16` and `min φ_I = 25/48`, each attained by 12 designs, for example `ids = [0, 4, 5, 2, 1, 3]`. All six candidates are used here, so only interchanges can improve a start; every interchange-local optimum is global (verified by the independent review), so first improvement reaches the optimum from any nonsingular start.
9. **Complexity (target).** One Cholesky of `M_Z` per iteration. A thin QR (or Cholesky) of `X` once per call. Exchange scores cost `O(m p² + n m p)`: apply `M_Z⁻¹` and `M_Z⁻¹BM_Z⁻¹` once to the candidate rows and to `R`. Interchange scores cost `O(n² p)`. There are no `m×m` matrices. The `n×n` matrix `Q` is formed once per call. The workspace is checked against `max_elements`, including `n²` for `Q`.

## 63. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, covariates=…)`

- `covariates` is `None` (default: results byte-identical to `main`) or a 2-D CPU float64 tensor of shape `(n, c)`, `c ≥ 1`, with finite entries and `[1 Z]` of full column rank `1 + c` (checked by Cholesky of `XᵀX` with the §8 pivot floor). Otherwise `ValueError`. Criteria D, A and I only, CPU float64 `F`, `m ≥ n` and `n ≥ 1 + c + p`, else `ValueError`. `covariates` together with `blocks` or `whole_plots` raises `ValueError`.
- Start: the `_blocked_design` draw, i.e. a random permutation of the candidates (or of the pool after `fixed_ids`), assigned to runs in order. A draw is rejected and redrawn if `M_Z` fails the start rule (Cholesky plus pivot floor), with the §8 failure count and error.
- Result: the §8 keys plus `covariates` (the input tensor, unchanged). `ids` are in **run order**: `ids[i]` is the candidate run at covariate row `i`. Nothing is sorted. `objective = logdet M_Z` (D) or `−φ_B(M_Z)` (A, I). `certificate = "LOCAL_COVARIATE_EXCHANGE"` if no improving move remains, else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`. `efficiency_lower_bound = _dual_floor(F − F.mean(0), M_Z, n, criterion, 0.0)` (item 7).

## 64. Not in this section

Covariate-aware `design_diagnostics` and power (D2 part 4), covariates with blocks or whole plots, factor × covariate interactions in the model, hard-to-change covariates (week 15), choosing the run order of the covariates, GPU or float32.

## 65. Compound D/A/I criteria (W11 D3, part 3)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D3.** Status: `P2_D3C_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Notation: `F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}` candidate features of full column rank, `M` a positive-definite information matrix, `W = M⁻¹`, `B = FᵀF/m` the candidate-average I moment of §48.3/§59.5, positive definite because `rank F = p`. Unstructured designs only (§67).

1. **Compound criterion (definition).** Weights `w = (w_D, w_A, w_I) ≥ 0` with `w_D + w_A + w_I = 1`. With `Φ_D(M) = det(M)^{1/p}`, `Φ_A(M) = 1/tr(M⁻¹)`, `Φ_I(M) = 1/tr(BM⁻¹)`, the compound criterion is `Φ(M) = w_D log Φ_D(M) + w_A log Φ_A(M) + w_I log Φ_I(M) = (w_D/p) log det M − w_A log tr(M⁻¹) − w_I log tr(BM⁻¹)`, maximised. A term with weight 0 is omitted (its matrix is not formed).
2. **Efficiency-normalised form (PROVED).** For any fixed reference matrices `M_k*` (for example the single-criterion optima), `Σ_k w_k log(Φ_k(M)/Φ_k(M_k*)) = Φ(M) − Σ_k w_k log Φ_k(M_k*)`. The two objectives differ by a constant, so they have the same maximisers over any design set. Decision: the reference optima are not computed; the search maximises `Φ` of item 1.
3. **Log-homogeneity and concavity (PROVED).** Each `Φ_k` is positive and positively homogeneous of degree 1 on positive-definite `M`, and concave: `det^{1/p}` is concave (Minkowski); `M ↦ 1/(cᵀM⁻¹c) = min{xᵀMx : cᵀx = 1}` is a minimum of linear functions, hence concave, and with `B = Σ_k c_k c_kᵀ` over the nonzero rows `c_k = f_k/√m` (zero rows contribute nothing), `1/tr(BM⁻¹)` is the harmonic sum `1/Σ_k (1/h_k)` of the concave positive `h_k = 1/(c_kᵀM⁻¹c_k)`, which is concave and nondecreasing in each `h_k`; `B = I` gives `Φ_A`. Hence `g = exp Φ = Π_k Φ_k^{w_k}`, a weighted geometric mean of positive concave degree-1 functions, is concave and positively homogeneous of degree 1, and `Φ = log g` is concave with `Φ(cM) = Φ(M) + log c` for `c > 0`.
4. **Sensitivity (PROVED).** `∇Φ(M) = (w_D/p) W + w_A W²/tr(W) + w_I WBW/tr(BW)`. For a candidate row `f`, the sensitivity is `d(f; M) = fᵀ∇Φ(M)f = (w_D/p) fᵀWf + w_A fᵀW²f/tr(W) + w_I fᵀWBWf/tr(BW)`, and `⟨∇Φ(M), M⟩ = w_D + w_A + w_I = 1` (Euler's identity for item 3). For an approximate design `ξ` with `M(ξ) = Σ_x ξ(x) f(x)f(x)ᵀ`, `Σ_x ξ(x) d(f(x); M(ξ)) = 1`.
5. **Dual efficiency floor (PROVED).** Let `M_ν` be the normalised information of any approximate design `ν` on the candidates with `M_ν` positive definite, and `M*` that of an optimal approximate design for `Φ`; it exists because the set of normalised candidate moment matrices is compact and `Φ → −∞` at singular `M` (`B` is positive definite). Concavity and homogeneity of `g` (item 3) give `g(M*) ≤ g(M_ν) + ⟨∇g(M_ν), M* − M_ν⟩ = g(M_ν) ⟨∇Φ(M_ν), M*⟩ ≤ g(M_ν) · max_f d(f; M_ν)`, since `M*` is a convex combination of the `ffᵀ`. Hence an exact `n`-run design with information `M_n` has compound efficiency `exp(Φ(M_n/n) − Φ(M*)) ≥ exp(Φ(M_n/n) − Φ(M_ν)) / max_f d(f; M_ν)`. This is relative to the compound optimum, not to the single-criterion optima. Weight 1 on one criterion reduces it exactly to the existing `_dual_floor` bound for that criterion: D gives `exp((log det(M_n/n) − log det M_ν)/p) · p/max fᵀM_ν⁻¹f`; A and I give `(φ_ν/φ_n) · φ_ν/max_f fᵀM_ν⁻¹BM_ν⁻¹f` with `φ = tr(BM⁻¹)` (`B = I` for A). Decision: `_dual_floor` gains a compound branch that uses the same multiplicative iteration with update `ν ← ν · d(·; M_ν)`, renormalised, the floor above at every iterate (maximum kept), and stop ratio `1/max d ≥ 1 − 1e-4` when `w_A = w_I = 0` and `1 − 1e-3` otherwise. With weight 1 on one criterion the iterates, stop rule and floor are those of the existing branch.
6. **Exchange step (PROVED).** Swapping an outgoing design row `v` for an incoming candidate row `u` gives `M' = M + uuᵀ − vvᵀ`. With `a = uᵀWu`, `b = uᵀWv`, `c = vᵀWv` and `r = (1 + a)(1 − c) + b²`: `det M' = r · det M`, and `M'` is positive definite iff `r > 0`. For a symmetric `Q = WCW` with `C` symmetric (`C = I` for A, `C = B` for I) and `α = uᵀQu`, `β = uᵀQv`, `γ = vᵀQv`: `tr(CM'⁻¹) = tr(CW) − ((1 − c)α + 2bβ − (1 + a)γ)/r` (Woodbury with the `2 × 2` kernel `K = diag(1, −1) + [u v]ᵀW[u v]`, `det K = −r`). The exact compound gain of the swap is `ΔΦ = (w_D/p) log r − w_A log(t_A'/t_A) − w_I log(t_I'/t_I)`, where `t` and `t'` are the traces before and after. Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-18): the determinant and trace identities hold exactly on 40 random rational cases with random symmetric positive-semidefinite `C`.
7. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-18).** Optima over all `C(m, n)` subsets of distinct candidate IDs, with `B = FᵀF/m` and `Φ` of item 1 evaluated at `M/n`, so `Φ = (w_D/p) log(det M/nᵖ) − w_A log(n tr M⁻¹) − w_I log(n tr BM⁻¹)`:
   - (C-a) `F = polynomial_features(full_factorial([3, 3]).double() - 1)` (`m = 9`, `p = 6`), `n = 6`. `w = (1, 0, 0)`: `max det M = 256`, attained by 8 subsets: 4 with `tr M⁻¹ = 13/2` and 4 with `tr M⁻¹ = 11/2`. `w = (0, 1, 0)`: `min tr M⁻¹ = 5` (at `det M = 144`). `w = (1/2, 1/2, 0)`: the optimum has `det M = 256`, `tr M⁻¹ = 11/2`, `tr BM⁻¹ = 3/2`, `Φ = (1/12) log(256/6⁶) − (1/2) log 33`. `w = (1/3, 1/3, 1/3)`: the same subset values, `Φ = (1/18) log(256/6⁶) − (1/3) log 33 − (1/3) log 9`.
   - (C-b) the §59.6 (MP-b) features (`m = 9`, `p = 6`), `n = 7`. `w = (1/2, 0, 1/2)`: `det M = 3/8`, `tr BM⁻¹ = 7/9` (3 tied subsets, 2 with `tr M⁻¹ = 151/6` and 1 with `70/3`), `Φ = (1/12) log((3/8)/7⁶) − (1/2) log(49/9)`. `w = (1/3, 1/3, 1/3)`: a unique optimum with `det M = 3/8`, `tr M⁻¹ = 70/3`, `tr BM⁻¹ = 7/9`, `Φ = (1/18) log((3/8)/7⁶) − (1/3) log(490/3) − (1/3) log(49/9)`.
   On (C-a) with `w = (1/2, 1/2, 0)`, a D-optimal subset with `tr M⁻¹ = 13/2` has a strictly smaller `Φ` (`(1/12) log(256/6⁶) − (1/2) log 39`), so the compound optimum is not an arbitrary single-criterion optimum; (C-a) with `w = (1/2, 1/2, 0)` is the discriminating oracle (4 tied optimal subsets, all with the stated values).
8. **Cost.** Per exchange pass, `O(mp² + nmp)` time for the whitened rows and the `n × (m − n)` tables of `a, b, c` and, per positive A or I weight, `α, β, γ`; `O(nm)` extra memory. The dual floor costs `O(mp²)` per iteration, as the existing branch.

## 66. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, criterion={"D": w_D, "A": w_A, "I": w_I})`

- `criterion` may be a `dict` whose keys are a nonempty subset of `"D"`, `"A"`, `"I"` and whose values are finite real numbers `≥ 0` with a positive sum. The weights are divided by their sum (item 1). Otherwise `ValueError`. A compound criterion with `blocks`, `whole_plots` or `covariates` raises `ValueError`. It requires CPU float64 features, as the structured searches.
- Search: the existing `_plot_restarts` driver with one plot of size `n` (so the returned `ids` are sorted), the existing restarts, `fixed_ids` pool and drawing, and a `value(M, L)` hook that returns `Φ(M/n)`; moves score all swaps of free positions by item 6 and yield trials with `ΔΦ > 0` and `r > 0` in descending `ΔΦ`. No second restart driver or exchange loop.
- Result: the keys of the structured results, `criterion` equal to the normalised weight `dict`, `objective = Φ(M/n)`, `certificate = "LOCAL_COMPOUND_EXCHANGE"` at a local optimum (else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`), and `efficiency_lower_bound` from item 5.
- An API entry, a guide snippet (a D/I compromise on a 3² quadratic with its floor) and a CHANGELOG line.

## 67. Not in this section

Alias-aware criteria (Jones–Nachtsheim alias trace with a D-efficiency floor; D3, part 4), E, c, G or V terms in the compound, compound criteria with blocks, whole plots or covariates, user-supplied reference efficiencies, region-integrated I moments, GPU.

## 68. Covariate-aware diagnostics (W11 D2, part 4)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D2.** Status: `P2_D2D_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Notation and model of §62: `y = β₀1 + Fₛθ + Zζ + ε`, `ε ~ (0, σ²I_n)`, `ids` in run order (row `i` of `Z` belongs to run `i`), `X = [1 Z]`, `Q = I − X(XᵀX)⁻¹Xᵀ`, `M_Z = FₛᵀQFₛ`, `X̃ = [1 Z Fₛ]`. `F` has no intercept column.

1. **Covariance and residual degrees of freedom (PROVED).** If `X̃` has full column rank `1 + c + p`, the OLS estimate of `θ` has covariance `σ² M_Z⁻¹` (the `θ` block of `(X̃ᵀX̃)⁻¹`, §62.1), and the residual degrees of freedom are `ν = n − 1 − c − p`. Float64 check (Architect): 200 random instances, `θ` block of `(X̃ᵀX̃)⁻¹` against `M_Z⁻¹` to 6e-15, and `tr(I − X̃X̃⁺) = ν` in every case.
2. **Correlation and VIF (decision).** `C = M_Z` over all `p` columns of `F` (a column constant over `ids` makes `M_Z` singular because `Q1 = 0`, so the existing non-constant-column restriction removes nothing when `M_Z ≻ 0`); `correlation = D^{−1/2} C D^{−1/2}` with `D = diag C`; `vif = diag(correlation⁻¹)`. Because `M_Z = RᵀR` with `R = QFₛ` and `Q1 = 0`, this is the correlation matrix of the covariate-adjusted, centered factor columns.
3. **Scaled prediction variance (decision).** For region rows `g(x)` (default: the rows of `F`) with mean `ḡ`, `spv(x) = n · (g(x) − ḡ)ᵀ M_Z⁻¹ (g(x) − ḡ)`, which is `n/σ²` times the variance of the estimated factor contrast `(g(x) − ḡ)ᵀθ̂`. It does not depend on `β₀` or `ζ`, and it depends on `Z` only through `M_Z` (that is, through the column span of `[1 Z]`), as the I criterion of §62.5. `fds` is `spv` sorted ascending.
4. **Power (PROVED from item 1).** With unit error variance and effect vector `δ`, the two-sided t-test for `θ_j` has noncentrality `λ_j = δ_j² / (M_Z⁻¹)_jj` and `ν` degrees of freedom. The effect-group F-test for a coefficient group `g` has `λ_g = δ_gᵀ [(M_Z⁻¹)_gg]⁻¹ δ_g` and degrees of freedom `(|g|, ν)`; for `|g| = 1` it is the t-test (§56). Power requires `ν ≥ 1`.
5. **Blocks reduction (PROVED by §62.2).** Let the blocks be contiguous with sizes `N₁…N_g`, `g ≥ 2` (so `c = g − 1 ≥ 1`), all `N_b ≥ 1`, and let `Z` be the 0/1 indicators of blocks `2…g`. Then `M_Z = M_B` and `ν = n − g − p`, which are the `blocks=` information and residual degrees of freedom. Hence `columns`, `correlation`, `vif`, `spv`, `fds`, `power`, `df`, `group_power` and `group_df` equal those of `design_diagnostics(F, ids, blocks=[N₁, …, N_g], …)`.
6. **Complexity (target).** `O(n²(1 + c) + np² + |region| p²)`: one projector `Q` (or thin QR of `X`), one Cholesky of `M_Z`, one triangular solve for the region rows.

## 69. API decision: `design_diagnostics(F, ids, *, covariates=…)`

- `covariates` is `None` (default: results byte-identical to `main`) or a 2-D tensor of shape `(len(ids), c)`, `c ≥ 1`, with `F`'s dtype and device, finite entries and `[1 Z]` of full column rank (the §63 Cholesky check with the §8 pivot floor). Otherwise `ValueError`. `covariates` together with `blocks`, `whole_plots` or `alias` raises `ValueError`. If `M_Z` is singular (some combination of `F` columns over `ids` lies in the span of `[1 Z]`), the existing Cholesky of `M` raises; there is no extra check (Simplicity Contract).
- Result: the structured keys (`columns`, `correlation`, `vif`, `spv`, `fds`), with `effect` also `power` and `df` (all entries `ν`), and with `groups` also `group_power` and `group_df`. If `effect` is given and `ν < 1`, `ValueError` (the existing structured message).
- The covariate path reuses the structured branch of `design_diagnostics` with `M = C = M_Z`, the centered region and a constant `df`; no second diagnostics function.

## 70. Not in this section

Covariates with blocks or whole plots, alias matrices with covariates, factor × covariate interactions, hard-to-change covariates, prediction variance at new covariate values, GPU.

## 71. Alias-penalised D designs (W11 D3, part 4)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D3.** Status: `P2_D3A_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Notation: candidate model rows `F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}` and candidate alias-term rows `G ∈ ℝ^{m×q}` (row `i` of both belongs to candidate `i`); for distinct IDs `ids` (`n` of them), `X₁ = F[ids]`, `X₂ = G[ids]`, `M = X₁ᵀX₁` positive definite, `W = M⁻¹`, `N = X₁ᵀX₂`, and the alias matrix of §28.3, `A = WN ∈ ℝ^{p×q}`, so that `E[β̂] = β₁ + Aβ₂` when the true mean is `X₁β₁ + X₂β₂`. `‖A‖² = tr(AᵀA)`. Unstructured designs only (§73).

1. **Criterion (decision).** For a weight `λ ≥ 0`, maximise `Ψ_λ(ids) = (1/p) log det(M/n) − λ ‖A‖²`. This is the weighted form that WEEK_GOAL D3 allows in place of the constrained Jones–Nachtsheim (2011) form (minimise `‖A‖²` subject to a D-efficiency floor); the constrained form is not implemented (§73). `λ = 0` is the D criterion.
2. **Pareto property (PROVED).** For `λ > 0`, every global maximiser `ids*` of `Ψ_λ` over the `n`-subsets is Pareto-optimal for (maximise `det M`, minimise `‖A‖²`): if some `ids'` had `det M' ≥ det M*` and `‖A'‖² ≤ ‖A*‖²` with one strict, then `Ψ_λ(ids') > Ψ_λ(ids*)`. Sweeping `λ` therefore reaches only supported Pareto points of the D–alias trade-off, not all of them: (AL-b) `(36, 7/9)` of item 6 is Pareto-optimal but is the maximiser for no `λ ≥ 0`.
3. **Exchange step (PROVED).** Swap an outgoing design row `v` (alias row `v₂`) for an incoming candidate row `u` (alias row `u₂`): `M' = M + uuᵀ − vvᵀ`, `N' = N + uu₂ᵀ − vv₂ᵀ`. With `a = uᵀWu`, `b = uᵀWv`, `c = vᵀWv`, `r = (1 + a)(1 − c) + b²` (§65.6: `det M' = r det M`, `M'` positive definite iff `r > 0`) and, for `r ≠ 0`, the symmetric `2 × 2` matrix `J = K⁻¹ = (1/r) [[1 − c, b], [b, −(1 + a)]]` of the Woodbury kernel `K = diag(1, −1) + [u v]ᵀW[u v]`:
   - `A' = A + P J Eᵀ`, where `P = [Wu Wv] ∈ ℝ^{p×2}` and `E = [e_u e_v] ∈ ℝ^{q×2}` with the alias residuals `e_x = x₂ − Aᵀx`;
   - `‖A'‖² − ‖A‖² = 2 tr(J S) + tr(J Γ J H)`, with the `2 × 2` matrices `S_{xy} = (A e_x)ᵀ W y`, `Γ_{xy} = (Wx)ᵀ(Wy)` and `H_{xy} = e_xᵀ e_y` for `x, y ∈ {u, v}`;
   - the exact gain is `ΔΨ_λ = (1/p) log r − λ (2 tr(J S) + tr(J Γ J H))`.
   For an actual swap `M'` is a Gram matrix, so `r ≥ 0`; `r < 0` arises only algebraically. `J` is symmetric, so `tr(J S)` does not depend on the orientation of `S`. Proof: `M'⁻¹ = W − P J Pᵀ` (Woodbury), `PᵀN = [u v]ᵀA` and `Pᵀ[u v] = K − diag(1, −1)`, so `M'⁻¹N' = A + P J ([u₂ v₂]ᵀ − [u v]ᵀA)`. Exact `Fraction` check (Architect, 2026-11-18): `A'`, `det M' = r det M` and the trace difference hold exactly on 60 random rational cases; a float64 vectorised implementation of the gain over all swaps agrees with dense recomputation on 2 970 swaps (200 random instances) to 1e-8 relative.
4. **Cost (target).** Per exchange pass, `O(mp(p + q) + nm(p + q))` time: the whitened rows `Wf`, residuals `e(f) = g(f) − Aᵀf` and `A e(f)` for all `m` candidates, then `n × (m − n)` tables of `a, b, c` and of the cross terms of `S`, `Γ`, `H`; `O(m(n + p + q))` extra memory. No `p × p` or `p × q` product per swap.
5. **Certificate (decision).** As a function of approximate design weights `w` (`M(w) = Σ wᵢ fᵢfᵢᵀ`, `N(w) = Σ wᵢ fᵢgᵢᵀ`), `Ψ_λ` is not concave in general for `λ > 0` (for example `p = q = 1`, support `{(f, g)} = {(1, −1), (3, 2)}`, `λ = 3`, weights `t = 1/4, 1/2, 3/4` on the first point), so there is no equivalence-theorem floor for `Ψ_λ`. The result reports the local-exchange certificate for `Ψ_λ` and, as `efficiency_lower_bound`, the existing unstructured D dual floor (`_dual_floor` with `criterion="D"`) of the returned design: a lower bound on its D-efficiency relative to the approximate D-optimum, which is the quantity the Jones–Nachtsheim constraint bounds.
6. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-18).** Optima over all `C(m, n)` subsets of distinct IDs with `M` nonsingular:
   - (AL-a) `X = ff2n(3)` (`m = 8`), `F = [1, x₁, x₂, x₃]` (`p = 4`), `G = [x₁x₂, x₁x₃, x₂x₃]` (`q = 3`), `n = 6`. All 28 subsets are nonsingular, with only three `(det M, ‖A‖²)` values; the Pareto set is `(1024, 3/2)` (12 subsets) and `(768, 1/3)` (4 subsets). `λ = 0`: `det M = 1024`, `‖A‖² = 3/2`. `λ = 1/10`: `det M = 768`, `‖A‖² = 1/3`, `Ψ = (1/4) log(768/6⁴) − 1/30`. The D-optimal designs are strictly worse at `λ = 1/10`, so this is the discriminating oracle.
   - (AL-b) `X = full_factorial([3, 3]) − 1` (`m = 9`), `F = [1, x₁, x₂]` (`p = 3`), `G = [x₁x₂, x₁², x₂²]` (`q = 3`), `n = 5`; 126 nonsingular subsets, Pareto set `(96, 31/18)` (4), `(80, 32/25)` (1), `(36, 7/9)` (4), `(20, 8/25)` (1). `λ = 1/4`: the unique optimum has `det M = 80`, `‖A‖² = 32/25`, `Ψ = (1/3) log(80/125) − 8/25` (an interior Pareto point, neither the D-optimum nor the alias minimum). `λ = 1`: the unique optimum has `det M = 20`, `‖A‖² = 8/25`, `Ψ = (1/3) log(20/125) − 8/25`. With the centre point fixed, the `λ = 1/4` optimum over completions is the same `(80, 32/25)` design.

## 72. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, alias=G, alias_weight=λ)`

- `alias` is `None` (default: results byte-identical to `main`) or a 2-D tensor `G` with `G.shape[0] == F.shape[0]`, `F`'s dtype and device and finite entries; `alias_weight` is a finite real `λ ≥ 0` (default `1.0`) and is used only with `alias`. Otherwise `ValueError`. `alias` requires `criterion="D"` (a string); `alias` with any other criterion, a compound `dict`, `blocks`, `whole_plots`, `covariates` or `hard` raises `ValueError`. CPU float64 features and `m ≥ n ≥ p`, as the compound search (§66).
- Search: the compound search of §66 with weights `{"D": 1}` and an alias term: the `value(M, L)` hook returns `Ψ_λ`, and the moves score all swaps of free positions by item 3 and yield trials with `ΔΨ_λ > 0` and `r > 0` in descending `ΔΨ_λ`. No new restart driver or exchange loop.
- Result: the keys of the compound result with `criterion = "D"`, `objective = Ψ_λ`, `alias_trace = ‖A‖²` of the returned design, `certificate = "LOCAL_ALIAS_EXCHANGE"` at a local optimum (else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`), and `efficiency_lower_bound` from item 5.
- An API entry, a guide snippet (AL-a with `λ = 1/10` beside the D design, printing both `alias_trace` values) and a CHANGELOG line. Alias diagnostics of any design remain `design_diagnostics(F, ids, alias=G)` (§29).

## 73. Not in this section

The constrained Jones–Nachtsheim form (alias trace subject to a D-efficiency floor), alias terms with A or I or compound criteria, alias-aware blocked, split-plot or covariate designs, a certificate for `Ψ_λ`, choosing `λ` automatically, a JMP alias-optimal reference, GPU.

## 74. Diagnostics with whole plots nested in fixed blocks (W11 D2, part 5)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D2.** Status: `P2_D2E_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Model, notation and layout of §53: `y = Fₛθ + Z_bβ + Zγ + ε`, `w` whole plots of sizes `n₁…n_w` nested in `g` fixed blocks of sizes `N₁…N_g` (every block boundary is a plot boundary), `V = I + ηZZᵀ`, `η > 0` known, `F` without an intercept, `M_C = FₛᵀPFₛ` (§53.1). `h` is the number of columns of `F` that are constant within every whole plot of the design ("whole-plot columns"); the other `p − h` are subplot columns.

1. **Covariance (PROVED).** If `[Z_b Fₛ]` has full column rank (equivalently `M_C ≻ 0`, §53.3), the GLS estimate of `θ` in the model with free `β` has covariance `σ² M_C⁻¹`: with `X̃ = [Z_b Fₛ]`, the `θ` block of `(X̃ᵀV⁻¹X̃)⁻¹` is the inverse of the Schur complement `FₛᵀV⁻¹Fₛ − FₛᵀV⁻¹Z_b(Z_bᵀV⁻¹Z_b)⁻¹Z_bᵀV⁻¹Fₛ = M_C`. Float64 check (Architect): dense `θ` block against `M_C⁻¹` to 7e-16 on three designs of the §53.8 candidates; exact `Fraction` check (independent review): 49/49 random cases.
2. **Correlation and VIF (decision).** `C = M_C` restricted to the non-constant columns of `Fₛ` (a constant column makes `M_C` singular, §53.3, so nothing is removed when `M_C ≻ 0`); `correlation = D^{−1/2} C D^{−1/2}`, `D = diag C`; `vif = diag(correlation⁻¹)`.
3. **Scaled prediction variance (decision).** For region rows `g(x)` (default: the rows of `F`) with mean `ḡ`, `spv(x) = n (g(x) − ḡ)ᵀ M_C⁻¹ (g(x) − ḡ)`: `n/σ²` times the variance of the estimated contrast `(g(x) − ḡ)ᵀθ̂` (the mean response is not estimable with fixed blocks, §53.5). `fds` is `spv` sorted ascending.
4. **Power and degrees of freedom (decision; containment rule, approximate).** With unit error variance and effect vector `δ`, the t-test for `θ_j` has noncentrality `λ_j = δ_j²/(M_C⁻¹)_jj` (exact by item 1) and degrees of freedom `ν_W = w − g − h` for a whole-plot column and `ν_S = n − w − (p − h)` for a subplot column. A group `G` of coefficients must lie in one stratum (else `ValueError`); its F-test has `λ_G = δ_Gᵀ[(M_C⁻¹)_GG]⁻¹δ_G` and degrees of freedom `(|G|, ν)` of that stratum (§56). If `M_C ≻ 0`, the `g` block indicators and the `h` whole-plot columns are linearly independent and lie in the `w`-dimensional column span of `Z`, so `ν_W ≥ 0`; `ν_S` can be `≤ 0` (plots `[2, 1]`, one block, rows `(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, 0)`, `η = 1`: `M_C ≻ 0`, `ν_S = −1`). Power requires every entry of `df` to be `≥ 1` (the existing structured rule). The block effects are constant within whole plots, so they take `g` whole-plot degrees of freedom; this replaces the intercept's one in §19.
5. **Reductions (PROVED).**
   - (R-a) **One block.** If `g = 1`, `M_C` is the Schur complement of `τ` in the §7.1 information `M_W` of `[1 F]` (§53.2), so `(M_W⁻¹)[1:, 1:] = M_C⁻¹`. Hence `correlation` and `vif` equal those of `design_diagnostics([1 F], ids, whole_plots=…, eta=η)`; and with `effect = (δ₀, δ)` there, `power[1:]` and `df[1:]` equal `power` and `df` here (both paths classify the columns of `F` by the same rule, and the intercept is a whole-plot column, so the whole-plot `df` there is `w − (1 + h)` and the subplot `df` is `n − w − (p − h)` in both). `spv` differs (that path does not center the region).
   - (R-b) **Singleton plots.** If every `n_k = 1`, `M_C = M_B/(1 + η)` (§53.2) and `h = p`, so `ν_W = n − g − p`, the `blocks=` degrees of freedom; `ν_S` is not used. Hence `correlation`, `vif` and `df` equal those of `design_diagnostics(F, ids, blocks=…)`, `spv` equals `(1 + η)` times its `spv`, and `power` with effect `δ` equals its `power` with effect `δ/√(1 + η)`; the same holds for `group_power` and `group_df`.
   Float64 check (Architect): (R-a) Schur complement and inverse block to 2e-14, (R-b) to 4e-15, on 200 random instances against the dense `FₛᵀPFₛ`.
6. **Complexity (target).** The existing `_split_information(…, blocks=…)` closed form (§53.1) once, one Cholesky of `M_C`, one triangular solve for the region rows: `O(np² + wp² + |region| p²)`. No `n × n` matrices.

## 75. API decision: `design_diagnostics(F, ids, *, blocks=…, whole_plots=…, eta=…)`

- `blocks` and `whole_plots` may now be given together. `whole_plots` and `eta` are validated as now; `blocks` must be positive Python ints summing to `len(ids)` and each partial sum `N₁ + … + N_b` must be a partial sum of `whole_plots`, else `ValueError` (the §54 rule). `covariates` or `alias` with either still raises.
- Result: the structured keys (`columns`, `correlation`, `vif`, `spv`, `fds`); with `effect` also `power` and `df` (item 4); with `groups` also `group_power` and `group_df`. A mixed-stratum group, or an entry of `df` below 1, raises `ValueError` with the existing messages.
- The combined path reuses the structured branch of `design_diagnostics` with `M = C = M_C` from `_split_information`, the centered region and the stratum `df`; no second diagnostics function. With only one of `blocks` or `whole_plots`, results are byte-identical to `main`.

## 76. Not in this section

Kenward–Roger or Satterthwaite degrees of freedom, unknown `η` or REML, random block effects, covariates or alias matrices with blocks or whole plots, a plot split across blocks, split-split-plot, GPU.

## 77. Uncentred diagnostics for no-intercept models (W11 D1, part 5)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 goal D1.** Status: `P2_D1E_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Notation: candidate rows `F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}`, distinct design IDs `ids` (`n` of them), `S = F[ids]` with columns `s₁, …, s_p`, `M = SᵀS` positive definite, `W = M⁻¹`. Unstructured designs only (§79).

1. **Why the existing VIFs fail (PROVED).** The unstructured branch of `design_diagnostics` centres the non-constant columns of `S`, scales them to unit length and inverts their correlation matrix. Suppose a vector `c` with at least two nonzero entries satisfies `Sc = 1ₙ`. If `M` is positive definite, no column of `S` is constant (if `sⱼ = a1ₙ`, then `S(ac − eⱼ) = 0` with `ac − eⱼ ≠ 0`). Averaging `Sc = 1ₙ` gives `Σⱼ cⱼ s̄ⱼ = 1`, so the centred columns satisfy `Σⱼ cⱼ (sⱼ − s̄ⱼ1ₙ) = 1ₙ − 1ₙ = 0`: the centred correlation matrix is singular and the VIFs are undefined. This holds for every Scheffé design with `q ≥ 2` components (§48: the linear blend columns sum to `Σ xₖ = 1` on each run, `c = (1, …, 1, 0, …, 0)`) and for every mixture-process design (§59: each run is the row-wise product of its blend and process features) whose process model has a constant column (the blend columns times that constant sum to it).
2. **Uncentred correlation (decision).** With `D = diag(M)`, define `R_u = D^{−1/2} M D^{−1/2}`, the Gram matrix of the unit-length but uncentred columns `sⱼ/‖sⱼ‖`, and the uncentred variance inflation factors `VIF_u = diag(R_u⁻¹)` (Belsley, Kuh and Welsch 1980, scaling without centring for models without an intercept). All `p` columns are used, constant or not.
3. **Closed form (PROVED).** `R_u⁻¹ = D^{1/2} W D^{1/2}`, so `VIF_u,j = M_jj W_jj = ‖sⱼ‖² (M⁻¹)_jj`. `R_u` is positive definite because `M` is; `|(R_u)_ij| ≤ 1` (Cauchy–Schwarz) with unit diagonal; `VIF_u,j ≥ 1`, with equality for all `j` iff `M` is diagonal (Cauchy–Schwarz on `1 = eⱼᵀeⱼ = ⟨M^{1/2}eⱼ, M^{−1/2}eⱼ⟩`: `1 ≤ M_jj W_jj`, equality iff `M^{1/2}eⱼ ∥ M^{−1/2}eⱼ`, i.e. `eⱼ` is an eigenvector of `M`; every `eⱼ` is an eigenvector only when `M` is diagonal).
4. **Unchanged quantities.** SPV `n f(x)ᵀWf(x)`, its sorted values `fds`, power and alias already use `M` directly and need no centring; they are unchanged by this section.
5. **Cost.** One Cholesky of `M` (already formed) and its inverse diagonal: `O(np² + p³)`.
6. **Exact oracles (PROVED by exact `Fraction` computation; Architect, 2026-11-18).** With `h = 1/2`, `t = 1/3`:
   - (MX-a) Scheffé linear, `X` = the three vertices: `VIF_u = (1, 1, 1)`, `R_u = I`.
   - (MX-b) Scheffé linear, `X = simplex_centroid(3)` (7 runs: vertices, edge midpoints, centroid): `VIF_u = 2059/1890` for each blend.
   - (MX-c) Scheffé quadratic, `X` = the `{3, 2}` simplex lattice (3 vertices, 3 edge midpoints; saturated): `VIF_u = 3/2` for all six columns.
   - (MX-d) Scheffé quadratic, `X = simplex_centroid(3)`: `VIF_u = 3799/2376` for the three blends and `16781/10692` for the three cross-products.

## 78. API decision: `design_diagnostics(F, ids, *, centered=True)`

- `centered` must satisfy `isinstance(centered, bool)` (so `1` and NumPy booleans are rejected with `ValueError`). The default `True` is byte-identical to `main`, including on Scheffé designs, where the centred VIFs stay undefined as in item 1.
- `centered=False` is unstructured only: with `blocks`, `whole_plots` or `covariates` it raises `ValueError` (those branches already define their own reference). With `centered=False`, `columns` and `correlation` change meaning: `columns = list(range(p))` (all columns, uncentred, instead of the non-constant centred ones), `correlation = R_u` and `vif = VIF_u` of §77.2–§77.3; `spv`, `fds`, `power`, `group_power` and `alias` are computed exactly as with `centered=True`.
