- An API sentence in the `design_diagnostics` entry, one runnable snippet in the guide's mixture section (MX-d with `centered=False`, printing `vif`) and a CHANGELOG line.

## 79. Not in this section

Pseudo-component or L-pseudo-component transforms, Cox-direction or Piepel-direction effects, mixture-specific power definitions, uncentred diagnostics for blocked, split-plot or covariate designs, automatic switching on detected collinearity, GPU.

## 80. Evaluation-only certificates: `fixed_ids` of length `n` (W11 held-out support)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-18 · Week 11 held-out additions H25–H26.** Status: `P2_HO_EVAL_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Notation: candidate rows `F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}`, run count `n`, a given design `T` = `n` distinct candidate IDs. Each `optimal_design` branch (unstructured D/A/I, compound, alias-penalised, `blocks`, `whole_plots`, both together, `covariates`) returns `efficiency_lower_bound`, a lower bound on the efficiency of its returned design relative to that branch's reference optimum (its approximate optimum, or for the alias branch the unstructured approximate D-optimum, §71.5).

1. **Why every existing floor is valid for an arbitrary given design (PROVED).** Write `φ` for the branch's criterion on normalised information (`φ = (1/p) log det` for D), evaluated at `M_T/n`. Each floor is the maximum of (a) a Kiefer–Wolfowitz/Atwood-type bound of the given design itself (for D, `p / max_{x∈F} SPV(x)` with `SPV(x) = n f(x)ᵀM_T⁻¹f(x)`, by AM–GM between `M_T/n` and the reference optimum; for A and I the corresponding step is Cauchy–Schwarz), and (b) a dual floor `exp(φ(M_T/n) − φ(M_w)) · r(w)`, where `M_w` is any approximate design on the candidates (W1 gate §4b) and weak duality gives `r(w) ≤ exp(φ(M_w) − φ_max) ≤ 1`. Both are pointwise inequalities in `M_T/n`: neither uses any optimality or local-optimality property of `T`, and the weights `w` may come from any iteration, including one started at `T`. They need only that `M_T` is positive definite and that `T` is feasible for the branch: distinct candidate IDs and, for structured branches, a run-order assignment (block sizes, whole-plot sizes, hard-label constancy) that puts `M_T/n` in the branch's approximate-design class. A floor relative to the approximate optimum is also a lower bound on efficiency relative to the best exact `n`-run design. Fraction check (independent reviewer, 2026-11-18): D (KW and dual forms) and A (Atwood and dual forms) on 15,439 random `(F, n, T)` cases, `m = 6–8`, `p = 2–3`, every nonsingular `T`: floor ≤ approximate efficiency ≤ exact efficiency in every case.
2. **Decision.** `optimal_design` and `d_optimal_design` accept `fixed_ids` with `len(fixed_ids) == n` (previously only `< n`); `len(fixed_ids) > n` still raises `ValueError`. With all `n` runs fixed, every restart starts from `T` and no exchange has a free position, so the result's `ids` are `T` in the branch's existing order convention (sorted for unstructured branches; run order, with `fixed_ids` filling the leading positions, for `blocks`, `whole_plots` and `covariates`). `objective`, `efficiency_lower_bound` (and `d_efficiency_lower_bound` or `alias_trace` where the branch returns them) are that branch's existing values at `T`. There is no new public path: only the length check changes, and a branch that fails with zero free positions gets a minimal fix inside that branch. No branch re-optimises the block or whole-plot assignment of fixed runs. All existing structural checks still apply (for example "fixed_ids within a whole plot must share one hard label"), and a singular `M_T` raises as for any start.
3. **Cost.** `restarts` identical evaluations of `T` plus the existing floor iterations: `O(restarts · (mp² + p³))` plus the floor's existing cost. Callers who want one evaluation pass `restarts=1`.
4. **Use.** A reference design produced elsewhere (R AlgDesign, skpr, published tables) that uses each candidate at most once can be scored by the same call as a TorchOED design: `optimal_design(F, n, fixed_ids=T, <same structure keywords>, restarts=1)["efficiency_lower_bound"]`. For structured branches the caller encodes the external design's block and whole-plot membership through the leading-position run order, with the same block and whole-plot sizes. A design that repeats a candidate row cannot be expressed as `fixed_ids` and stays `NOT_RUN` for this metric.

## 81. API decision

- The `fixed_ids` sentence of `optimal_design` and `d_optimal_design` in `website/api.md` changes from "fewer than `n`" to "at most `n`; with exactly `n`, the call only evaluates the given design (§80)". One CHANGELOG line. One test file extended; no new module, function or keyword.

## 82. Not in this section

A separate public certificate function, designs with repeated candidate rows, changes to any floor's formula or iteration limits, GPU.

## 83. Space-filling Latin hypercubes: maximin and MaxPro by batched column swaps (W13 F1)

Design `X` is an `n × k` Latin hypercube on midpoint levels: every column is a permutation of `(r + 1/2)/n`, `r = 0 … n − 1`. For rows `i ≠ j` let `S_ijl = (x_il − x_jl)²`, `D_ij = Σ_l S_ijl` and `L_ij = Σ_l log S_ijl`.

- **(83.1) PROVED.** `S_ijl > 0` for all `i ≠ j` and every `l`, because each column is a permutation; hence `L_ij` is finite.
- **(83.2) PROVED (swap update).** Swapping the entries of rows `a` and `b` in column `l` keeps the Latin property. For `j ∉ {a, b}`: `D'_aj = D_aj − (x_al − x_jl)² + (x_bl − x_jl)²`, `D'_bj = D_bj − (x_bl − x_jl)² + (x_al − x_jl)²`, and the same with `log` for `L`. `D'_ab = D_ab`, `L'_ab = L_ab`; all other entries are unchanged.
- **(83.3) Objectives (minimised).** `maximin`: Morris–Mitchell `φ_p(X) = (Σ_{i<j} D_ij^{−p/2})^{1/p}` with `p = 15`. `maxpro`: Joseph–Gul–Ba `ψ(X) = (C(n,2)⁻¹ Σ_{i<j} exp(−L_ij))^{1/k}`. PROVED: for `p > 0` and `k ≥ 1`, minimising `Σ D_ij^{−p/2}` (resp. `Σ exp(−L_ij)`) orders designs exactly as `φ_p` (resp. `ψ`), so the search uses the sums.
- **(83.4) PROVED (cost).** With `D` (or `L`) cached, one swap changes `2(n − 2)` entries, so its objective change costs `O(n)`; all `C(n, 2)` swaps of one column cost `O(n³)` in one batched tensor evaluation, versus `O(n² k)` per swap from scratch.
- **(83.5) Search.** Per restart: one random midpoint LHS from the supplied generator; then repeat steepest descent — evaluate all swaps of all columns, apply the best strictly improving one, update `D`/`L` by (83.2) — until no swap improves or `max_iter` swaps are applied. Restarts run independently; the lowest objective wins (first on ties).
- **(83.6) Oracles.** Batched swap deltas equal a dense recompute (float64, 1e-12 relative). Exhaustive enumeration over all permutations of columns 2…k on small `n` gives the exact optimum.

Independent math review (Architect loop, fresh reviewer, 2026-11-19): PASS — 300 exact `Fraction` cases, n 4–8, k 2–4; the reviewer asked that (83.3) state `p > 0`, which it does.

## 84. API decision: `lhs(n, k, *, mode="random" | "maximin" | "maxpro", restarts=10, max_iter=None, generator=None, …)`

`mode="random"` is unchanged and bit-identical for every seed. The new modes return midpoint designs (no jitter) as a tensor like `"random"`. `max_iter=None` means no swap limit beyond convergence. No new public function.

## 85. Not in this section

Uniform designs and CD2 (W13 F2), constrained regions (W13 F3), sliced or nested LHS, simulated annealing, GPU timing claims.

## 86. Space-filling diagnostics and uniform Latin hypercubes by CD2 (W13 F2)

For a design `X` with `n ≥ 2` rows in `[0, 1]^k`, let `z_il = |x_il − 1/2|`, `d_ij` the Euclidean distance of rows `i ≠ j`, `h(x) = 1 + z/2 − z²/2` and `g(x, y) = 1 + |x − 1/2|/2 + |y − 1/2|/2 − |x − y|/2` (scalars, per coordinate).

- **(86.1) Diagnostics (definitions).** `min_distance = min_{i<j} d_ij`; `phi_p = (Σ_{i<j} d_ij^{−p})^{1/p}` with `p = 15` (Morris–Mitchell, as §83.3); `maxpro = (C(n,2)⁻¹ Σ_{i<j} Π_l (x_il − x_jl)^{−2})^{1/k}` (Joseph–Gul–Ba, as §83.3; `+inf` when two rows share a coordinate); `cd2 = sqrt(CD2²)` with Hickernell's centred L2 discrepancy
  `CD2² = (13/12)^k − (2/n) Σ_i P_i + n⁻² Σ_i Σ_j K_ij`, `P_i = Π_l h(x_il)`, `K_ij = Π_l g(x_il, x_jl)`.
  OBSERVED (exact): on 60 random midpoint LHS (n ≤ 6, k ≤ 3) this formula equals, in exact `Fraction`, the defining sum over non-empty coordinate subsets `u` of `∫_{[0,1]^u} disc_u²` (box from each point to its nearest cube vertex, integrals done piecewise exactly); one centre point in 1-D gives 1/12 and the 1-D midpoint design gives 1/(12n²), n = 1 … 8.
- **(86.2) PROVED (positivity).** On `[0, 1]`, `z ≤ 1/2` gives `h ≥ 1`, and `|x − y| ≤ |x − 1/2| + |y − 1/2|` gives `g ≥ 1`; so every `P_i`, `K_ij` is positive and the ratios below are defined.
- **(86.3) PROVED (swap update).** Swapping rows `a ≠ b` in column `l` of a Latin hypercube changes only `P_a, P_b` and the entries of rows/columns `a, b` of `K`:
  `P'_a = P_a h(x_bl)/h(x_al)`, `P'_b = P_b h(x_al)/h(x_bl)`;
  for `j ∉ {a, b}`: `K'_aj = K_aj g(x_bl, x_jl)/g(x_al, x_jl)`, `K'_bj = K_bj g(x_al, x_jl)/g(x_bl, x_jl)` (and `K` stays symmetric);
  `K'_aa = K_aa (1 + z_bl)/(1 + z_al)`, `K'_bb = K_bb (1 + z_al)/(1 + z_bl)` (since `g(x, x) = 1 + |x − 1/2|`); `K'_ab = K_ab` (`g` is symmetric).
  Hence `ΔCD2² = −(2/n)(ΔP_a + ΔP_b) + n⁻² (2 Σ_{j∉{a,b}} (ΔK_aj + ΔK_bj) + ΔK_aa + ΔK_bb)`, `O(n)` per swap and `O(n³)` for all `C(n,2)` swaps of one column in one batched evaluation, as §83.4. Architect check: float64, 9 × 3 midpoint LHS, all 108 swaps, max relative error vs dense recompute 4.7e-14.
- **(86.4) Search.** `lhs(..., mode="uniform")` minimises `CD2²` by exactly the §83.5 steepest-descent swap search (same restarts, `max_iter`, tie rule) with (86.3) as the cached update. Minimising `CD2²` and `cd2` is the same order.
- **(86.5) Oracles.** Batched swap deltas equal a dense recompute (float64, 1e-12 relative). `CD2²` of a midpoint design has rational coordinates, so an exact `Fraction` evaluation of (86.1) is the oracle for `cd2` and for exhaustive optima on small `n`.

Independent math review (Architect loop, fresh reviewer, 2026-11-19): PASS — exact `Fraction`, 240 random midpoint LHS (n 3–8, k 1–4), all 8,758 swaps checked against exact recompute for every equation of (86.3); (86.1) checked against the definition as stated. The reviewer noted that (86.1)–(86.3) hold for any design in `[0, 1]^k`; only `maxpro` finiteness needs the Latin property.

## 87. API decision: `space_filling_diagnostics(X, *, p=15) -> dict`

Public function in `torchoed.sampling`, exported from `torchoed`. Returns `{"min_distance", "phi_p", "maxpro", "cd2"}` as 0-d tensors in `X`'s dtype and device, computed densely (`O(n² k)`). Validates only what the existing `lhs` validation helpers already offer for a floating 2-D tensor. `lhs(mode="uniform")` returns midpoint designs like the other optimised modes; `mode="random"` stays bit-identical.

## 88. Not in this section

Wrap-around or mixture discrepancies, star discrepancy, non-midpoint (continuous) optimisation, constrained regions (W13 F3), GPU timing claims.

## 89. Fast flexible filling on constrained regions (W13 F3)

Region `R = {x ∈ [0, 1]^k : A x ≤ b}` minus the `disallowed` combinations, tested by the existing `feasible_candidates`. Construction after Lekivetz and Jones (2015), "Fast flexible space-filling designs for nonrectangular regions", Quality and Reliability Engineering International 31, with the MaxPro point choice used by JMP.

- **(89.1) Candidates.** `S` = the first `N` points (`N = n_candidates`, a power of two, no skip) of the scrambled Sobol sequence `sobol(N, k, scramble=True, seed=seed)`; `F = S[feasible_candidates(S, A=A, b=b, disallowed=disallowed)]`, keeping Sobol order. If `|F| < n`, raise `ValueError` (too few feasible candidates for `n` runs).
- **(89.2) PROVED (distinct coordinates).** With `N = 2^m`, every one-dimensional projection of the first `N` points of a scrambled Sobol sequence has exactly one point in each interval `[r/N, (r + 1)/N)`, `r = 0 … N − 1`. Reason: each Sobol generator matrix is nonsingular upper triangular, so each coordinate's first `2^m` points form a (0, m, 1)-net; linear matrix scrambling (nonsingular lower-triangular) and a digital shift preserve this. Hence any two distinct candidates differ in every coordinate, and every MaxPro term in (89.4) is finite in exact arithmetic. The stratification gives no lower bound on coordinate gaps, so float64 overflow of a term is possible for large `k`. That is not handled here (§91). The required tests check this property on the candidates they use (OBSERVED there).
- **(89.3) Clustering (Lloyd k-means, batched).** Initial centroids are the first `n` rows of `F`. Repeat: assign each candidate to its nearest centroid in squared Euclidean distance (first index on ties); then set each centroid to the mean of its members, and an empty cluster keeps its previous centroid. Stop when no assignment changes or after `max_iter` iterations. PROVED: neither step increases the within-cluster sum of squares `W = Σ_c Σ_{x ∈ c} ‖x − μ_c‖²`. Cost: `O(|F| n k)` per iteration in one `torch.cdist`-style evaluation. The final clusters are those of the last assignment. If any final cluster is empty, the run raises `ValueError` (fewer than `n` non-empty clusters). This is rare (OBSERVED: about 1 in 3,000 small uniform-random cases in the review) but reachable on valid input.
- **(89.4) Point choice (MaxPro, cluster-wise exchange).** Objective (minimised), as §83.3 / §86.1: `Q(X) = Σ_{i<j} Π_l (x_il − x_jl)^{−2}` over the chosen points, one per cluster. Start: in each cluster, the member nearest to its centroid (first index on ties). Sweep the clusters `c = 1 … n` in order; for cluster `c`, every member `m` has the exact change
  `ΔQ(m) = Σ_{j ≠ c} (Π_l (m_l − x_jl)^{−2} − Π_l (x_cl − x_jl)^{−2})`,
  evaluated for all members of `c` in one batched operation, `O(|c| n k)`. Replace `x_c` by the member with the smallest `ΔQ` if that `ΔQ < 0` (first index on ties). Stop after a sweep with no replacement, or after `max_iter` sweeps. PROVED in exact arithmetic: `Q` strictly decreases at each replacement, so no design repeats and the loop would terminate (finitely many choices). In float64 a computed `ΔQ < 0` can be rounding noise, so the implementation terminates by a sweep with no replacement or by `max_iter`. Each sweep costs `O(|F| n k)`.
- **(89.5) Output.** The `n × k` chosen points, in cluster order, as a tensor in the requested dtype and device. Every row is a feasible Sobol candidate.
- **(89.6) Oracles (independent of the implementation).** (a) Lloyd fixed point: at the returned clustering, recomputed densely, each candidate's assigned centroid is a nearest centroid and each centroid equals its cluster mean. This holds only when clustering stopped because no assignment changed. (b) Exchange optimality: for each cluster, brute force over all of its members, no single replacement lowers `Q` by more than `1e-12 · Q(X)`, `X` the returned design (dense float64 recompute). This holds only when the exchange stopped after a sweep with no replacement. Tests call the private clustering and exchange helpers, which return their result together with a converged flag. They use a `max_iter` that reaches convergence in both loops and assert the flag. (c) Feasibility: every row satisfies `A x ≤ b`, matches no `disallowed` combination and is a row of `S`.

Independent math review (Architect loop, fresh reviewer, 2026-11-19): first pass found that (89.6b) was not implied when the exchange stops by `max_iter`, and that the float64 termination step was unproved. Both were fixed by the wording above. Second pass: PASS. Checks: (89.2) on 120 scrambled-Sobol runs (m ∈ {4, 8, 12}, d ≤ 40); ΔQ (89.4) in exact `Fraction` on 300 cases; Lloyd monotonicity (89.3) in `Fraction` on 300 runs.

## 90. API decision: `fast_flexible_filling(n, k, *, A=None, b=None, disallowed=(), n_candidates=2**14, seed=0, max_iter=100, dtype=torch.float64, device=None) -> torch.Tensor`

Public function in `torchoed.sampling`, exported from `torchoed`. Inputs are validated only with the existing helpers (`integer` for `n`, `k`, `n_candidates`, `seed`, `max_iter`; `sobol` already validates its own arguments) plus the `|F| < n` and empty-cluster `ValueError`s of §89. `n_candidates` must be a power of two (`ValueError` otherwise), as (89.2) needs. No change to `lhs`, `sobol` or `feasible_candidates`.

## 91. Not in this section

Ward hierarchical clustering (JMP's exact clustering; k-means is the batched torch substitute), the JMP "centroid" choice, categorical factors beyond `disallowed`, nonlinear constraints, overflow guards for large `k`, support points (`support::sp` is an external reference only), GPU timing claims.

## 92. Strict improvement in floating point (amends (83.5) and (86.4), all three `lhs` search modes)

Let `f` be the minimised search sum of the current design (`Σ D_ij^{−p/2}`, `Σ exp(−L_ij)` or `CD2²`; all are `> 0`) and `Δ` the batched float64 change of a swap from (83.2) / (86.3).

- **(92.1) OBSERVED (exact zero moves exist).** Some swaps change the design but leave the objective exactly unchanged. Architect check: on 30 random 8 × 3 midpoint LHS, 10 uniform searches revisited a design (2-cycles); every accepted move in a cycle had computed `Δ ≈ −1e-17` to `−3e-17` and exact `Fraction` `ΔCD2² = 0`. The test `f + Δ < f` accepts such a move whenever `|Δ|` exceeds half an ulp of `f`, so the move and its reverse can both be accepted and the search never stops. The independent review also found exact zero moves for maximin and MaxPro (n 3–7, k 2–3), so the rule applies to all three modes.
- **(92.2) Rule.** A swap is improving only if `Δ < −τ f` with `τ = 1e-12`. The best (most negative `Δ`) improving swap over all columns is applied, ties as (83.5); the search stops when no swap is improving or `max_iter` swaps are applied. Everything else in (83.5) / (86.4) is unchanged.
- **(92.3) PROVED (termination).** With exact `Δ`, every applied swap strictly decreases `f`; the set of designs is finite, so no design repeats and the search stops. The float64 search terminates whenever the (92.4) error bound holds (`|computed Δ − exact Δ| < τ f`), and always within `max_iter` swaps.
- **(92.4) OBSERVED (float64 separation).** Computed `Δ` differs from the exact change by at most about `5e-14 f` in Architect checks (relative error of (86.3) up to 4.7e-14 for a 9 × 3 design, §86.3), far below `τ f`; the smallest accepted genuine improvement over 200 uniform restarts (n 6–30, k 2–5) was `2.3e-5 f`, and the independent review found no nonzero exact swap change below `6.6e-6 f` (all swaps of 300 designs, n 4–10, k 2–4). So `τ` rejects rounding noise and no genuine improvement seen in practice. The CD2² error comes from cancellation and scales like `eps (13/12)^k`, not like `f`, so the margin narrows for large `n`, `k`; this is not a proof for all `n`, `k`.
- **(92.5) Oracle.** The default 8 × 3 uniform call of the #404 freeze terminates; every accepted swap has an exact `Fraction` decrease `> 0`.

## 93. "If Possible" terms: Bayesian D and I (W14 B1)

Notation. Candidates F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}, columns split into primary P (indices not in `if_possible`, p₀ of them) and potential Q (indices in `if_possible`, q = p − p₀ ≥ 1). A design is a set S of n distinct candidate ids, X = F[S]. τ > 0. K = diag(k_j) with k_j = 1 for potential columns and 0 otherwise. A(S) = XᵀX + K/τ².

(93.1) **DuMouchel–Jones scaling** (DuMouchel and Jones 1994, §3). R = Q − P(PᵀP)⁻¹PᵀQ over all m candidates (P of full column rank); Q̃_j = 2 R_j / (max_i R_ij − min_i R_ij). If P is not of full column rank, or some range max_i R_ij − min_i R_ij ≤ 1e-10 · max(1, max_i |Q_ij|) (a potential column numerically in the span of the primary columns; an exact-zero test fails in float64, independent review counterexample Q = x1 + 0.1·x2 on the 3² grid gives range 1.1e-15), raise `ValueError`. F̃ = [P, Q̃] in the original column order; all criteria and outputs below use F̃. PROVED (idempotent): if PᵀQ = 0 and every column of Q has max − min = 2, then R = Q and Q̃ = Q. Architect check: on the 3² grid, raw columns [x1x2, x1², x2²] give Q̃ = [x1x2, 2(x1² − 2/3), 2(x2² − 2/3)] and scaling Q̃ again returns Q̃ (float64; `Fraction` check of PᵀQ̃ = 0 and max − min = 2).

(93.2) **Criteria.** Bayesian D: maximise log det A(S). Bayesian I: minimise ψ(S) = tr(A(S)⁻¹ W), W = F̃ᵀF̃/m (the existing I moment matrix of the scaled candidates). n must satisfy m ≥ n ≥ p₀; n < p is allowed because A(S) ≻ 0 whenever X[:, primary] has full column rank; m ≥ n ≥ p₀ is necessary, not sufficient, so the search starts (and restarts are discarded, as today for singular starts) only from designs whose primary block X_P has full column rank. PROVED: for v = (v_P, v_Q) ≠ 0, vᵀAv = ‖Xv‖² + ‖v_Q‖²/τ² > 0 unless v_Q = 0 and X_P v_P = 0.

(93.3) **Pseudo-run identity.** Let E ∈ ℝ^{q×p} have rows e_jᵀ/τ for the potential columns j. Then [X; E]ᵀ[X; E] = XᵀX + K/τ² = A(S). PROVED (block product). Consequence used by the implementation: Bayesian D search is the existing D search on F_aug = [F̃; E] (m + q rows) with n + q runs and the q pseudo ids added to `fixed_ids`; because ids are distinct and the pseudo ids are fixed, no pseudo row can be chosen again. Bayesian I is the existing linear (A/I) exchange on the same F_aug and fixed pseudo ids with B = W from (93.2), computed from the m real candidates only — **not** F_augᵀF_aug/(m + q). The returned `ids` exclude the pseudo ids and are sorted real candidate ids.

(93.4) **Bayesian D efficiency floor** (equivalence theorem, Kiefer 1974 style). For an approximate design ξ (weights on the m real candidates, Σw = 1) let φ(ξ) = log det(n M(ξ) + K/τ²), M(ξ) = Σ w_x f̃_x f̃_xᵀ. φ is concave. For the exact design S (ξ_S uniform on S, n M(ξ_S) = XᵀX):
  g(S) = n · max_x f̃_xᵀ A(S)⁻¹ f̃_x − tr(A(S)⁻¹ XᵀX),
  and every exact design S* of n distinct runs has log det A(S*) ≤ log det A(S) + g(S). PROVED: concavity gives φ(ξ) ≤ φ(ξ_S) + ∇φ(ξ_S)·(ξ − ξ_S), the right side is linear in ξ so its maximum over the simplex is at a vertex δ_x, where the directional derivative is n f̃_xᵀA⁻¹f̃_x − tr(A⁻¹ n M(ξ_S)); and S* is one approximate design. Report `efficiency_lower_bound = exp(−g(S)/p)`, a lower bound on (det A(S)/det A(S*))^{1/p}. OBSERVED (Architect, float64, 3² grid above, n = 4, τ ∈ {0.5, 1, 2}): log det A(S) + g(S) ≥ Frank–Wolfe approximate optimum for all 126 subsets; the floor is loose when n < p (it is valid, not tight).

(93.5) **Bayesian I efficiency floor.** ψ is convex in ξ with directional derivative toward δ_x equal to −n f̃_xᵀA⁻¹WA⁻¹f̃_x + tr(A⁻¹WA⁻¹ n M(ξ_S)). Hence every exact S* has
  ψ(S*) ≥ L(S) = ψ(S) − n · max_x f̃_xᵀA(S)⁻¹WA(S)⁻¹f̃_x + tr(A(S)⁻¹WA(S)⁻¹XᵀX).
  PROVED (same argument as (93.4) with convexity). Report `efficiency_lower_bound = max(L(S), 0)/ψ(S)`, a lower bound on ψ(S*)/ψ(S). OBSERVED (same Architect check): L(S) ≤ the exhaustive exact minimum for all 126 subsets; L is often negative for n < p, giving the valid floor 0.

(93.6) **Independent math review (2026-11-20, fresh agent, Fraction + float64):** PASS on (93.1)–(93.5) — exhaustive all-pairs checks of (93.4)/(93.5) on 3², 2³ and 25 random cases, τ ∈ {1/2, 1, 2}; directional derivatives match finite differences; one defect (exact range-0 test in float64) fixed above with a relative tolerance.

(93.7) **Complexity.** Same as the existing D and linear exchanges on m + q candidates and n + q runs; scaling (93.1) is one least-squares solve O(m p₀²); floors O(m p²).

## 94. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, criterion="D", if_possible=None, tau=1.0, ...)`

`if_possible` is a list of distinct column indices of F (Python ints, 1 ≤ len < p); `tau` a finite positive float. Only string `criterion` "D" or "I"; CPU float64; combining with `blocks`, `whole_plots`, `hard`, `covariates`, `alias` or a compound criterion raises `ValueError`. User `fixed_ids` are honoured (real ids). Result keys as for the unstructured criterion, with `objective` = log det A(S) (D) or ψ(S) (I) on F̃, `efficiency_lower_bound` from (93.4)/(93.5), `criterion`, and `scaled_features` = F̃. `if_possible=None` leaves every existing path and result bit-identical.

## 95. Not in this section

Bayesian A, priors other than K/τ² (non-diagonal or estimated priors), Bayesian designs for nonlinear models (P3), structured (blocked, split-plot) Bayesian designs, and choosing τ automatically.

## 96. Model-robust D over a set of models (W14 B2)

Notation. Candidates F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}. A model is a nonempty list C_k of distinct column indices of F, k = 1…K, K ≥ 1, p_k = |C_k|; U = ∪_k C_k (sorted), p_U = |U|. Weights w_k > 0 with Σ_k w_k = 1 (user weights are divided by their sum; default w_k = 1/K). A design is a set S of n distinct candidate ids, X = F[S], M = XᵀX, and M_k = M[C_k, C_k] = X[:, C_k]ᵀX[:, C_k] (a principal submatrix of M). For an approximate design ξ on the m candidates, M_k(ξ) = Σ_x ξ_x f_{k,x} f_{k,x}ᵀ with f_{k,x} = F[x, C_k].

(96.1) **Criterion** (Läuter 1974; Li and Nachtsheim 2000, weighted geometric mean of D-efficiencies). Maximise Ψ(S) = Σ_k (w_k/p_k) log det(M_k/n) = φ(ξ_S), where ξ_S is uniform on S and φ(ξ) = Σ_k (w_k/p_k) log det M_k(ξ). PROVED (efficiency form): for any fixed positive reference determinants D_k*, Σ_k w_k log[(det(M_k/n)/D_k*)^{1/p_k}] = Ψ(S) − Σ_k (w_k/p_k) log D_k*, so maximising Ψ maximises the weighted geometric mean of the per-model D-efficiencies; the single-model optima are not computed. K = 1 with C_1 = all columns is the D criterion (1/p) log det(M/n).

(96.2) **Feasibility (decision).** Require m ≥ n ≥ p_U, and the search keeps M_U = M[U, U] positive definite (starts with singular M_U are discarded, as today). PROVED: M_U ≻ 0 implies every M_k ≻ 0 (principal submatrix of a positive-definite matrix). Designs with n < p_U whose every M_k is nonsingular are out of scope (§98).

(96.3) **Exchange step** (§65.6 applied per model; PROVED there). Swapping an outgoing design row v for an incoming candidate row u, with u_k = u[C_k], v_k = v[C_k], W_k = M_k⁻¹, a_k = u_kᵀW_k u_k, b_k = u_kᵀW_k v_k, c_k = v_kᵀW_k v_k, r_k = (1 + a_k)(1 − c_k) + b_k²: det M_k' = r_k det M_k, so the exact gain is ΔΨ = Σ_k (w_k/p_k) log r_k. A swap is admissible only if r_U > 0 (the same quantity for U), which keeps M_U' ≻ 0 (M_U + uuᵀ ≻ 0, and removing the rank-one term vvᵀ creates at most one nonpositive eigenvalue by interlacing, so det M_U' = r_U det M_U > 0 means none) and hence every r_k > 0 by (96.2). Implementation decision: `_compound_design` on F[:, U] (each C_k remapped to its positions within U) with the D gain term replaced by this sum over models (one whitening Z_k = L_k⁻¹F[:, C_k]ᵀ per model, L_k the Cholesky factor of M_k) and the admissibility mask r_U > 0; no second exchange loop or restart driver.

(96.4) **Concavity and sensitivity.** PROVED: each M ↦ det(M[C_k, C_k])^{1/p_k} is concave, positive and positively homogeneous of degree 1 on positive-definite M (Minkowski's determinant inequality composed with a linear map), so g = exp φ = Π_k det(M_k)^{w_k/p_k} is concave and degree-1 homogeneous (weighted geometric mean, as in §65.3), and φ is log-homogeneous: replacing every M_k by cM_k (c > 0) adds log c. The sensitivity of candidate x at ξ is d(x; ξ) = Σ_k (w_k/p_k) f_{k,x}ᵀ M_k(ξ)⁻¹ f_{k,x}, and Σ_x ξ_x d(x; ξ) = Σ_k (w_k/p_k) tr(M_k(ξ)⁻¹M_k(ξ)) = Σ_k w_k = 1.

(96.5) **Dual efficiency floor** (§65.5 argument with (96.4)). For any approximate design ν with every M_k(ν) ≻ 0 and an optimal approximate design ξ* for φ: g(ξ*) ≤ g(ν) · max_x d(x; ν), i.e. φ(ξ*) ≤ φ(ν) + log max_x d(x; ν). PROVED: concavity and homogeneity give g(ξ*) ≤ g(ν) + ⟨∇g(ν), M(ξ*) − M(ν)⟩ = g(ν)⟨∇φ(ν), M(ξ*)⟩ (Euler: ⟨∇φ(ν), M(ν)⟩ = 1), and ⟨∇φ(ν), M(ξ*)⟩ = Σ_x ξ*_x d(x; ν) ≤ max_x d(x; ν). Every exact n-run design is an approximate design, so the exact design S has robust efficiency exp(Ψ(S) − φ(ξ*)) ≥ exp(Ψ(S) − φ(ν)) / max_x d(x; ν). Decision: `_dual_floor` gains a model-robust branch: start ν uniform on the m candidates, multiplicative update ν ← ν · d(·; ν) (already normalised by (96.4)), the floor above at every iterate (maximum kept, together with the start value at ν = ξ_S), stop when 1/max d ≥ 1 − 1e-4 or after 1000 iterations, as the D branch. With K = 1 and C_1 = all columns, d = (1/p) fᵀM(ν)⁻¹f and the floor is exactly the existing D floor exp((log det(M/n) − log det M_ν)/p) · p / max fᵀM_ν⁻¹f. OBSERVED (Architect, float64, 3² grid of (96.7)): for all feasible subsets of three model sets, φ(ξ_S) + log max_x d(x; ξ_S) ≥ the multiplicative-algorithm approximate optimum (min margin 0.10), and Σ_x ξ_x d(x; ξ) = 1 to 1e-12.

(96.6) **Per-model floors (decision).** Also report, for each model k, the existing D dual floor of the returned design on F[:, C_k] (its D-efficiency lower bound relative to that model's own approximate optimum), as `model_efficiency_lower_bounds` in model order.

(96.7) **Exhaustive oracles** (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction` determinants, 2026-11-20). F = `polynomial_features(full_factorial([3, 3]).double() - 1)` = [1, x1, x2, x1², x2², x1x2] (m = 9, p = 6), all C(9, n) subsets; feasible = M_U ≻ 0, the search rule of (96.2). Values are (det M_1, det M_2) of the robust optimum; single-model maxima are taken over the feasible subsets.
  - (MR-a) models [0, 1, 2] and [0, 3, 4], equal weights, n = 6: 78 feasible subsets; the optimum (4 tied subsets) has (det M_1, det M_2) = (90, 10), Ψ = (1/6)(log 90 + log 10) − log 6; the second-best value has (68, 12); the feasible single-model maxima are 140 and 12, and no robust-optimal subset attains either, so neither single-model D-optimum is robust-optimal (discriminating oracle).
  - (MR-b) models [0, 1, 2] and [0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5], equal weights, n = 6: 76 feasible subsets; the optimum (4 tied) has (140, 256), Ψ = (1/6) log 140 + (1/12) log 256 − log 6; the second-best value has (116, 256), so a D-optimal subset for the full quadratic alone (det 256, §65.7 (C-a)) with det M_1 = 116 is not robust-optimal.
  - (MR-c) models [0, 1, 2, 5] and [0, 1, 2, 3, 4], weights (1, 2) (normalised to (1/3, 2/3)), n = 6 (p_U = 6): 76 feasible subsets; the optimum (4 tied) has (560, 64), Ψ = (1/12) log 560 + (2/15) log 64 − log 6; the second-best value has (464, 64); the feasible maxima are 560 and 96, so a D-optimum of the second model alone (det 96) is not robust-optimal. With n = 5 < p_U the call raises `ValueError` (96.2).

(96.8) **Complexity.** Per exchange pass O(m Σ_k p_k² + n m Σ_k p_k) for the K whitenings and the n × (m − n) tables of a_k, b_k, c_k, plus the same for U; O(K n m) extra memory. Floor O(m Σ_k p_k²) per iteration.

(96.9) **Independent math review (2026-11-20, fresh agent, Fraction + float64):** PASS on (96.1)–(96.6) after two oracle fixes. Verified: (96.2)/(96.3) exactly on 400 random integer cases, Σ_x ξ_x d = 1 exactly on 50 cases, (96.5) on 200 random model sets × 20 random ν (minimum margin +2.9e-11) and the K = 1 reduction to the D floor. Fixed above: the first (MR-c) used n = 5 < p_U, unreachable under (96.2); the (MR-a) count used the all-M_k rule (82) instead of M_U ≻ 0 (78); the interlacing step in (96.3) was added.

## 97. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, criterion="D", models=None, model_weights=None, ...)`

`models` is `None` (default: every existing path and result bit-identical) or a nonempty list of nonempty lists of distinct Python int column indices of F (duplicates across models allowed); `model_weights` is `None` (equal) or a list of len(models) finite positive numbers. Only string `criterion` "D"; CPU float64; `m ≥ n ≥ p_U`; combining with `blocks`, `whole_plots`, `hard`, `covariates`, `alias`, `if_possible` or a compound criterion raises `ValueError`. User `fixed_ids` are honoured. Result keys as for the compound criterion (sorted `ids`), with `criterion` = "D", `models` and `model_weights` (normalised) echoed, `objective` = Ψ(S), `certificate` = "LOCAL_MODEL_ROBUST_EXCHANGE" at a local optimum (else "NOT_CERTIFIED"), `efficiency_lower_bound` from (96.5) and `model_efficiency_lower_bounds` from (96.6).

## 98. Not in this section

Model-robust A or I, designs with n < p_U, a maximin (worst-case) criterion over models, Bayesian model averaging with priors on models, structured (blocked, split-plot, covariate) model-robust designs, automatic model enumeration (e.g. all models with a given number of two-factor interactions), GPU.

## 99. Compare Designs and the sample-size explorer (W14 B3)

Notation. Candidates F ∈ ℝ^{m×p}; region R ∈ ℝ^{r×p} (default R = F). Designs S_1, …, S_K (K ≥ 2), each a 1-D int64 tensor of n_i candidate ids (repeats allowed, as in `design_diagnostics`), with M_i = F[S_i]ᵀF[S_i] ≻ 0 and n_i > p (power needs ν_i = n_i − p ≥ 1; a saturated design raises `ValueError` through `design_diagnostics`). R must have at least one nonzero row (else I and G are 0/0). Relative prediction variance (σ² = 1) v_i(x) = f_xᵀ M_i⁻¹ f_x for each row f_x of R; `design_diagnostics(F, S_i, region=R)["spv"]` equals n_i v_i, so v_i = spv / n_i.

(99.1) **Relative efficiencies** of design i against design 1 (JMP Compare Designs convention: total information M_i, not per-run M_i/n_i, so designs of different sizes compare directly; values > 1 mean design i is better; design 1 gets 1 in every column):
  D_i = (det M_i / det M_1)^{1/p}, computed as exp((log det M_i − log det M_1)/p);
  A_i = tr(M_1⁻¹) / tr(M_i⁻¹);
  I_i = tr(M_1⁻¹W) / tr(M_i⁻¹W), W = RᵀR / r;
  G_i = max_x v_1(x) / max_x v_i(x), maxima over the rows of R.
PROVED (identity used by the implementation): tr(M⁻¹W) = (1/r) Σ_x f_xᵀM⁻¹f_x = mean_x v(x), so I_i = mean v_1 / mean v_i is computed from the same v vector as G and (99.2). PROVED: D_i equals the ratio of the D-efficiencies of designs i and 1 against any common reference determinant, provided both efficiencies use the same unscaled (total-information) criteria (the reference cancels; with per-run |M/n|^{1/p} the ratio gains a factor n_1/n_i); the same holds for A, I, G with their unscaled criteria.

(99.2) **Fraction of design space.** For each q in `quantiles` (default (0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1)), the q-quantile of {v_i(x) : x a row of R} by `torch.quantile` (linear interpolation). q = 0 and q = 1 are min v_i and max v_i. The full sorted vector v_i is also returned (the FDS curve, unscaled).

(99.3) **Power.** Per-coefficient power of design i is `design_diagnostics(F, S_i, effect=effect, alpha=alpha)["power"]` unchanged (two-sided t-test, ν_i = n_i − p, unit σ; requires n_i > p). External reference: reports/w14-skpr-power, skpr 1.9.2 `eval_design` on 8/12/16-run designs agrees ≤ 2.1e-10; an independent quadrature puts TorchOED within 5e-14 and the whole difference on the R side (OBSERVED, #437).

(99.4) **Sample-size explorer (decision).** An entry of `designs` that is a Python int n stands for the design `optimal_design(F, n, criterion=criterion, generator=generator)["ids"]`, so `compare_designs(F, [8, 12, 16], effect=1.0)` returns power and relative efficiencies against run size in one call, design 1 (the first entry) being the reference. Entries are processed in the given order with the one generator. Different n give different ν, so power is one `design_diagnostics` call per design; no cross-design batching and no GPU claim.

(99.5) **Independent math review (2026-11-20, fresh agent, `Fraction`):** PASS — (a) the trace identity exact in ~200 random cases, (c) the "> 1 is better" orientation consistent for D, A, I, G, and the worked oracle below exact; three gaps fixed in this text: the unscaled-criteria condition in (99.1), n_i > p and a nonzero region row in the notation, and `bool` entries rejected in §100.

(99.6) **Worked oracle** (Architect float64 + review `Fraction`). 3² grid {−1, 0, 1}², f = (1, x1, x2), R = F; design 1 = the four corners, design 2 = the corners plus the centre: D_2 = (5/4)^{1/3}, A_2 = 15/14, I_2 = 35/32, G_2 = 15/14; min v_1 = 1/4, max v_1 = 3/4, min v_2 = 1/5, max v_2 = 7/10.

(99.7) **Complexity.** K × (one Cholesky O(p³) + O(r p²) for v) plus the `optimal_design` calls for int entries.

## 100. API decision: `compare_designs(F, designs, *, region=None, effect=1.0, alpha=0.05, quantiles=(0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0), criterion="D", generator=None)`

In `src/torchoed/information.py`, exported from `torchoed`. `designs` is a list of length ≥ 2 whose entries are 1-D int64 id tensors or Python ints (run sizes, (99.4); `bool` is not a run size); anything else, or fewer than two entries, raises `ValueError`. Returns a dict: `ids` (list of K int64 tensors), `n` (list of K ints), `efficiency` (dict with keys "D", "A", "I", "G", each a float64 tensor of shape (K,), (99.1)), `power` (tensor (K, p), (99.3)), `fds_quantiles` (tensor (K, len(quantiles)), (99.2)), `fds` (list of K sorted v_i). Unstructured designs only (no blocks, whole plots, covariates); existing functions are unchanged.

## 101. Not in this section

Structured (blocked, split-plot, covariate) comparisons, Bayesian or model-robust criteria in Compare Designs, alias/correlation colour maps, power by simulation for GLMs (W15), and GPU batching across designs.

## 102. Split-split-plot D, A and I (W15 C1, part 1)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C1.** Status: `P2_C1A_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Split-plot notation, criteria and conventions are §7, §8, §16, §17 and §22; this section extends them to a second, nested stratum.

Notation: `w` is the number of whole plots, `m` the number of candidates, `m_{kj}` subplot sizes; `W` in item 4 is the 2-column matrix `[d q]`.

Model `y = Fθ + Z_wγ_w + Z_sγ_s + ε` for a design of `n` rows in `w` whole plots; whole plot `k` consists of subplots `j = 1…J_k` of sizes `m_{kj}` (`Σ_j m_{kj} = n_k`, `Σ_k n_k = n`); rows of a subplot are contiguous and subplots of a whole plot are contiguous. `Z_w[n, w]` and `Z_s[n, Σ_k J_k]` are the 0/1 whole-plot and subplot indicator matrices; `γ_w ~ (0, η_wσ²I)`, `γ_s ~ (0, η_sσ²I)`, `ε ~ (0, σ²I)` independent, known `η_w, η_s > 0`. Hence `Var y = σ²V`, `V = I + η_wZ_wZ_wᵀ + η_sZ_sZ_sᵀ`. Each candidate row `i` carries a whole-plot label `hᵢ` (very-hard-to-change setting) and a subplot label `uᵢ` (hard-to-change setting). A design is **feasible** iff all rows of each whole plot share `h`, all rows of each subplot share `u`, and all IDs are distinct.

1. **GLS information (PROVED).** `V` is block diagonal over whole plots, `V_k = A_k + η_w 11ᵀ`, `A_k = I + η_s·blockdiag_j(11ᵀ)`. By Sherman–Morrison per subplot, `A_k⁻¹ = I − blockdiag_j(c_{kj}11ᵀ)`, `c_{kj} = η_s/(1 + η_s m_{kj})`, and `A_k⁻¹1 = a_{kj}1` on subplot `j`, `a_{kj} = 1/(1 + η_s m_{kj})`. By Sherman–Morrison again, `V_k⁻¹ = A_k⁻¹ − d_k (A_k⁻¹1)(A_k⁻¹1)ᵀ`, `d_k = η_w/(1 + η_w Σ_j m_{kj}a_{kj})`. Hence, with subplot sums `s_{kj} = Σ_{i∈(k,j)} fᵢ` and `t_k = Σ_j a_{kj}s_{kj}`:
   `M = FₛᵀV⁻¹Fₛ = Σ_k [ Σ_j (F_{kj}ᵀF_{kj} − c_{kj} s_{kj}s_{kj}ᵀ) − d_k t_kt_kᵀ ]`.
   Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-20): equals dense `FₛᵀV⁻¹Fₛ` in 267/267 random cases (`p = 2…4`, 1–3 whole plots of 1–3 subplots of sizes 1–3, rational `η_w, η_s`).
2. **Rank and domination (PROVED).** `V ≽ I` is positive definite, so `M ≻ 0` iff `Fₛ` has full column rank (as §7.2) and `M ≼ FₛᵀFₛ` (as §22.2). Hence `n ≥ p` is necessary, and the §22.3 whole-plot floor (`g_j = f_j`, no shift) is valid for split-split-plot designs unchanged.
3. **Reductions (PROVED).** If every subplot has size 1, `V = (1 + η_s)(I + η′ZZᵀ)` with `η′ = η_w/(1 + η_s)`, so `M = M_split(η′)/(1 + η_s)`, with `M_split` the §7.1 information. If every whole plot has one subplot, `V = I + (η_w + η_s)ZZᵀ` and `M = M_split(η_w + η_s)`.
4. **Within-subplot exchange is a rank-2 update with `det K = −1` (PROVED).** Replace the row `f_o` of subplot `(k, j)` by a candidate `f_c`, `d = f_c − f_o`. With `q = FₛᵀV⁻¹e_o = f_o − c_{kj}s_{kj} − d_k a_{kj} t_k` and `v = e_oᵀV⁻¹e_o = 1 − c_{kj} − d_k a_{kj}²`: `ΔM = d qᵀ + q dᵀ + v d dᵀ = W K Wᵀ`, `W = [d q]`, `K = [[v, 1], [1, 0]]`, `K⁻¹ = [[0, 1], [1, −v]]`. The §16.3 ratio and gain formulas then hold verbatim with this `K⁻¹` (`det M′/det M = −det N`, `N = K⁻¹ + WᵀM⁻¹W`; `φ_B(M′) = φ_B(M) − tr(N⁻¹H)`; `M′ ≻ 0 ⟺ det(I₂ + KG) > 0`). The identity does not use labels. Fraction check (Architect): `q`, `v` equal the dense `FₛᵀV⁻¹e_o`, `V⁻¹_{oo}`; `ΔM` exact; ratio and A-gain equal the direct values, in every nonsingular case of item 1.
5. **Moves (decision).** Every accepted move keeps the design feasible and all sizes fixed. (i) *Exchange:* replace a row by an unused candidate with the same `(h, u)`, scored by item 4. (ii) *Subplot relabel:* for subplot `(k, j)` and a label `u′ ≠` its label with at least `m_{kj}` unused candidates of labels `(h_k, u′)`, build a greedy proposal as §7.5(ii) (D) / §16.4 (A, I) over that pool: each step takes, among unused candidates of that pool whose item-4 ratio from the current proposal state is `> 0`, the largest ratio (D) or the largest gain (A, I), ties by smallest candidate ID; if none has ratio `> 0` the proposal is discarded. (iii) *Whole-plot relabel:* for whole plot `k` and `h′ ≠ h_k`, replace the plot's rows one at a time in row order, each by the unused candidate with labels `(h′, u)`, `u` the row's subplot label, chosen by the step rule of (ii); discard if a step has no candidate with ratio `> 0`. Intermediate states mix labels, which item 4 allows; by §16.3 every intermediate state is positive definite. Local optimality is only with respect to (i)–(iii). No interchange between plots.
6. **Tiny exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration, Architect, exact `Fraction`).** Candidates `(A, B, x)`, `A, B ∈ {−1, 1}`, `x ∈ {−1, 0, 1}`, in this order (`m = 12`, IDs 0–11), `F = [1, A, B, x, x²]` (`p = 5`), `h = A`, `u = B`, whole plots `[4, 4]`, subplots `[2, 2, 2, 2]` (two per whole plot), `φ_A = tr M⁻¹`, `φ_I = tr(B_I M⁻¹)` with `B_I = FᵀF/m` (§16.1). Enumeration over the 648 feasible designs counted as ordered level assignments (whole-plot and subplot order distinguished, rows within a subplot unordered), 24 singular:
   - `η_w = η_s = 1`: `max det M = 1280/63` (96 designs), `min φ_A = 25/8` (16), `min φ_I = 2083/840` (96);
   - `η_w = 2`, `η_s = 1/2`: `max det M = 16` (96), `min φ_A = 89/24` (16), `min φ_I = 371/120` (96).
7. **Complexity (target).** As §16.7: one Cholesky of `M` per pass; exchange scores `O(m p² + n m p)` (apply `M⁻¹` and `M⁻¹BM⁻¹` once to the candidate rows, subplot sums and `t_k`); each relabel proposal `O(n_k (p³ + m p²))`; no `m×m` or `n×n` matrices; workspace checked against `max_elements`.

## 103. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, whole_plots=…, hard=…, subplots=None, subplot_hard=None, eta=1.0, eta_sub=1.0, criterion="D"|"A"|"I")`

- `subplots=None` (with `subplot_hard=None`): unchanged behaviour, byte-identical results. Otherwise `subplots` is a list/tuple of positive Python ints summing to `n` whose cumulative boundaries include every cumulative `whole_plots` boundary; `subplot_hard` is a length-`m` integer tensor on `F`'s device; `eta_sub` a finite positive Python `int` or `float` (`bool` rejected). `subplots` and `subplot_hard` are given together and require `whole_plots`/`hard`; with `blocks`, `fixed_ids`, `covariates`, `alias`, `if_possible`, `models` or a compound criterion → `ValueError`. CPU float64 only.
- Start: per draw, for whole plots in order `h = H[torch.randint(len(H), (1,), generator=generator)]`, `H = hard.unique()` (ascending); then for its subplots in order `u = U_h[torch.randint(len(U_h), (1,), generator=generator)]`, `U_h = subplot_hard[hard == h].unique()` (ascending, over all candidates, used or not); take the subplot's rows as `pool[torch.randperm(len(pool), generator=generator)[:m_{kj}]]`, `pool` the unused candidates with `(h, u)` in ascending ID. On an insufficient pool or a failed §8 start rule the whole design is redrawn; after `100·restarts` failed draws in total across all restarts (as §8) raise the existing "No feasible start …" `ValueError`.
- Search: each iteration scans exchanges (whole plots, subplots, rows in order; candidates of the same `(h, u)` in ascending ID), then subplot relabels (subplots in order, `u′` ascending), then whole-plot relabels (whole plots in order, `h′` ascending). As §17, an exchange is tried only if its item-4 ratio is `> 0` (and, for A/I, its gain `> 0`); a tried exchange or a completed relabel proposal is accepted iff a dense recomputation (item 1 `M`, Cholesky) improves the objective by more than the existing `_tolerance`. The first accepted move ends the iteration and the next iteration rescans from the start. An iteration with no accepted move stops with `stop_reason="LOCAL_SPLIT_SPLIT_PLOT_EXCHANGE"`. `max_iter`, restarts and the best-restart rule as §8.
- Result: as §17, plus `subplots`, `eta_sub`; `certificate="LOCAL_SPLIT_SPLIT_PLOT_EXCHANGE"` when that is the stop reason else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`; `efficiency_lower_bound = _dual_floor(F, M, n, criterion, 0.0)` (item 2).

## 104. Not in this section

Strip-plot designs (C1 part 2), `fixed_ids` with subplots, structure-aware diagnostics and power for split-split-plot designs, `subplots` with `blocks`, unknown variance ratios, more than two random strata, GPU or float32 search.

## 105. Strip-plot D, A and I (W15 C1, part 2)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C1.** Status: `P2_C1B_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Criteria, conventions and the exchange algebra are §16, §17, §22 and §102; this section replaces the nested strata of §102 by two crossed strata.

Model `y = Fθ + Z_rγ_r + Z_cγ_c + ε` for a design of `n = r·c` runs laid out as an `r × c` grid; run `o = i·c + j` (row-major, `i = 0…r−1`, `j = 0…c−1`) is the cell in row `i` and column `j`. `Z_r[n, r]` and `Z_c[n, c]` are the 0/1 row and column indicator matrices, so `Z_rZ_rᵀ = I_r ⊗ J_c` and `Z_cZ_cᵀ = J_r ⊗ I_c` (`J` all-ones); `γ_r ~ (0, η_rσ²I)`, `γ_c ~ (0, η_cσ²I)`, `ε ~ (0, σ²I)` independent, known `η_r, η_c > 0`. Hence `Var y = σ²V`, `V = I + η_rZ_rZ_rᵀ + η_cZ_cZ_cᵀ`. Each candidate `i` carries a row label `hᵢ` and a column label `gᵢ` (the scalar of item 1 is `b`, unrelated to these labels and to the §22.3 `g_j`). A design is **feasible** iff all cells of each grid row share `h`, all cells of each grid column share `g`, and all IDs are distinct.

1. **GLS information (PROVED).** With the commuting projectors `P_R = I_r ⊗ J_c/c`, `P_C = J_r/r ⊗ I_c`, `P_0 = P_RP_C = J/n`, `V = I + η_rc·P_R + η_cr·P_C`, and `I − P_R − P_C + P_0`, `P_R − P_0`, `P_C − P_0`, `P_0` are orthogonal projectors summing to `I` with eigenvalues `1`, `λ_r = 1 + η_rc`, `λ_c = 1 + η_cr`, `λ_0 = 1 + η_rc + η_cr` of `V`. Hence `V⁻¹ = I − a_r Z_rZ_rᵀ − a_c Z_cZ_cᵀ + b J`, `a_r = η_r/λ_r`, `a_c = η_c/λ_c`, `b = (1 − 1/λ_r − 1/λ_c + 1/λ_0)/n`, and with row sums `R_i = Σ_j f_{ic+j}`, column sums `C_j = Σ_i f_{ic+j}` and total `T = Σ_o f_o`:
   `M = FₛᵀV⁻¹Fₛ = FₛᵀFₛ − a_r Σ_i R_iR_iᵀ − a_c Σ_j C_jC_jᵀ + b TTᵀ`.
   The formula also holds at `η_c = 0` (then `a_c = 0`, `b = 0`), where it is the §7.1 information with the grid rows as whole plots of size `c`. Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-20): equals dense `FₛᵀV⁻¹Fₛ` in 300/300 random cases (`r, c ∈ {1, 2, 3}`, `p = 2…4`, integer `F` in [−2, 2], rational `η_r, η_c`), and the `η_c = 0` case equals the dense split-plot information in all 300.
2. **Rank and domination (PROVED).** `V ≽ I` is positive definite, so `M ≻ 0` iff `Fₛ` has full column rank, `n ≥ p` is necessary, and `M ≼ FₛᵀFₛ`; hence the §22.3 floor (`g_j = f_j`, no shift) is valid for strip-plot designs unchanged.
3. **Within-cell exchange (PROVED).** Replace the row `f_o` of cell `(i, j)` by a candidate `f_c`, `d = f_c − f_o`. With `q = FₛᵀV⁻¹e_o = f_o − a_rR_i − a_cC_j + bT` and `v = e_oᵀV⁻¹e_o = 1 − a_r − a_c + b`, `ΔM = d qᵀ + q dᵀ + v d dᵀ`, the §102 item 4 rank-2 form with `K = [[v, 1], [1, 0]]`, `K⁻¹ = [[0, 1], [1, −v]]`; the §16.3 ratio, gain and positive-definiteness formulas hold verbatim. Fraction check (Architect): `q`, `v` equal the dense `FₛᵀV⁻¹e_o`, `V⁻¹_{oo}`, and `ΔM` is exact, in 300/300 cases of item 1.
4. **Moves (decision).** Every accepted move keeps the design feasible and `(r, c)` fixed. (i) *Exchange:* replace a cell's candidate by an unused candidate (the cell's current candidate excluded) with the same row and column labels, scored by item 3. (ii) *Row relabel:* for row `i` and a label `h′ ≠ h_i`, replace the row's cells one at a time in column order, cell `(i, j)` by an unused candidate with labels `(h′, g_j)`, each chosen by the §102 item 5(ii) step rule (ratio `> 0`; largest ratio for D, largest gain for A and I; ties by smallest candidate ID); discard the proposal if a step has no candidate with ratio `> 0`. (iii) *Column relabel:* the same for column `j` and `g′ ≠ g_j`, cells in row order, labels `(h_i, g′)`. Intermediate states mix labels, which item 3 allows; by §16.3 every intermediate state is positive definite. Local optimality is only with respect to (i)–(iii).
5. **Tiny exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration, Architect, exact `Fraction`).** Candidates `(A, B, x)`, `A, B ∈ {−1, 1}`, `x ∈ {−1, 0, 1}`, in this order (`m = 12`, IDs 0–11), `F = [1, A, B, x, x²]` (`p = 5`), `h = A`, `g = B`, grid `r = 2`, `c = 3` (`n = 6`), `φ_A = tr M⁻¹`, `φ_I = tr(B_I M⁻¹)` with `B_I = FᵀF/m` (§16.1). Enumeration over the `2² · 2³ · 3⁶ = 23,328` assignments (A per row, B per column, x per cell; grid positions distinguished), keeping the 4,032 with distinct `(A, B, x)` cells, 864 of them singular:
   - `η_r = η_c = 1`: `max det M = 80/9` (144 designs), `min φ_A = 119/30` (96), `min φ_I = 167/60` (144);
   - `η_r = 2`, `η_c = 1/2`: `max det M = 6` (144), `min φ_A = 109/24` (96), `min φ_I = 571/168` (144).
6. **Complexity (target).** As §102.7: one Cholesky of `M` per pass; exchange scores `O(m p² + n m p)` using `R_i`, `C_j`, `T`; each relabel proposal `O(max(r, c)·(p³ + m p²))`; no `m×m` or `n×n` matrices; workspace checked against `max_elements`.

## 106. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, strip=None, row_hard=None, col_hard=None, eta_row=1.0, eta_col=1.0, criterion="D"|"A"|"I")`

- `strip=None` (with `row_hard=col_hard=None`): unchanged behaviour, byte-identical results. Otherwise `strip` is a pair of positive Python ints `(r, c)` with `r·c = n`; `row_hard` and `col_hard` are length-`m` integer tensors on `F`'s device; `eta_row`, `eta_col` finite positive Python `int` or `float` (`bool` rejected). `strip`, `row_hard` and `col_hard` are given together (`eta_row`, `eta_col` keep their defaults unless passed); with `whole_plots`, `subplots`, `blocks`, `fixed_ids`, `covariates`, `alias`, `if_possible`, `models` or a compound criterion → `ValueError`. CPU float64 only.
- Start: per draw, for rows in order `h_i = H[torch.randint(len(H), (1,), generator=generator)]`, `H = row_hard.unique()` (ascending); then for columns in order `g_j = G[torch.randint(len(G), (1,), generator=generator)]`, `G = col_hard.unique()`; then for cells in row-major order the cell's candidate is `pool[torch.randint(len(pool), (1,), generator=generator)]`, `pool` the unused candidates with `(h_i, g_j)` in ascending ID. On an empty pool or a failed §8 start rule (the start information `M` of item 1 not positive definite) the whole design is redrawn; after `100·restarts` failed draws in total (as §8) raise the existing "No feasible start …" `ValueError`.
- Search: each iteration scans exchanges (cells in row-major order; candidates with the cell's row and column labels in ascending ID; ties and step choices compared exactly as in §102/§103, no extra tolerance), then row relabels (rows in order, `h′` ascending), then column relabels (columns in order, `g′` ascending), with the §103 trial, dense-recomputation acceptance (item 1 `M`, Cholesky, existing `_tolerance`) and rescan rules. An iteration with no accepted move stops with `stop_reason="LOCAL_STRIP_PLOT_EXCHANGE"`. `max_iter`, restarts and the best-restart rule as §8.
- Result: as §17, rows in row-major grid order, plus `strip`, `eta_row`, `eta_col`; `certificate="LOCAL_STRIP_PLOT_EXCHANGE"` when that is the stop reason else `"NOT_CERTIFIED"`; `efficiency_lower_bound = _dual_floor(F, M, n, criterion, 0.0)` (item 2).

## 107. Not in this section

Strip-plot designs with whole plots, subplots or blocks, incomplete grids, `fixed_ids`, structure-aware diagnostics and power for strip-plot designs, unknown variance ratios, GPU or float32 search.

## 108. Minimum-aberration and blocked regular fractional factorials (W15 C2)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C2.** Status: `P2_C2_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Design, generator convention (Yates bits) and word-length pattern are §28 items 1, 2, 6 and §34.2; this section adds a search over generator sets and blocking.

Notation. `m = log₂ runs` base factors. A **mask** is an integer `g`, `1 ≤ g < 2^m`; its column is `Π_{c : bit c of g set} x_{·c}`, and `bits(g)` lists its set bits ascending. A regular `2^(k−p)` design with `p = k − m ≥ 0` is a sequence of `p` distinct masks `G = (g₁ < … < g_p)`, each with `popcount(g_t) ≥ 2`; factor `m + t − 1` is the column of `g_t`. For a nonempty `S ⊆ {1 … p}` write `x_S = XOR_{t∈S} g_t`; its word has length `ℓ(S) = popcount(x_S) + |S|`. `W(G) = (A₁ … A_k)` with `A_j = #{S ≠ ∅ : ℓ(S) = j}`. `<_lex` compares vectors from `A₁` upward.

1. **WLP from masks (PROVED).** The defining words of the design of §28 item 1 are exactly the sets `{m + t − 1 : t ∈ S} ∪ bits(x_S)` for nonempty `S`, one per `S` (the added-factor part determines `S`, the base part is forced), so `W(G)` equals `word_length_pattern(fractional_factorial(m, [bits(g) for g in G]))` (§28 item 2). For `p = 0` the design is `ff2n(m)` and `W = 0`.

2. **Completeness of the search space (PROVED).** A regular two-level design with `k` distinct factors in `2^m` runs (no factor constant, no two factors equal or opposite, full rank `m`) is a set of `k` distinct nonzero vectors of `F₂^m` spanning it; its words are the nonempty zero-sum subsets of these vectors, so its WLP is invariant under any factor relabelling and any `GL(m, 2)` change of base. Choosing `m` independent factors as the base maps it to `e₀ … e_{m−1}` and the other `p` factors to distinct vectors that are not unit vectors, i.e. masks of popcount ≥ 2. Hence the set `𝒢(k, m)` of all increasing `p`-tuples of distinct masks with popcount ≥ 2 attains every WLP that any regular design of that size attains, and `min_lex {W(G) : G ∈ 𝒢}` is the minimum-aberration WLP over all regular designs.

3. **Monotone bound (PROVED).** For a prefix `G' = (g₁ … g_r)` of `G`, the partial pattern `P(G')` (subsets `S ⊆ {1 … r}`) satisfies `W(G) ≥ P(G')` componentwise, because every word counted in `P(G')` is a word of `G` with the same length (`ℓ(S)` depends only on the masks in `S`). Componentwise `≥` implies `≥_lex`. Hence if `P(G') ≥_lex I` for an incumbent `I`, no completion of `G'` is `<_lex I`.

4. **Search (decision).** Masks with popcount ≥ 2 in increasing integer order. Depth-first over increasing tuples: extend the prefix with each later mask in order, leaving enough masks to reach length `p`; update `P` incrementally (for each existing subset XOR `x` of the prefix, including `0` with size 0, the new word has XOR `x ⊕ g` and size `+1`); skip a child whose `P ≥_lex I` (item 3). A complete tuple that is feasible (item 6 when blocked, always otherwise) and has `W <_lex I` becomes the incumbent; the initial `I` is "none" (no pruning). Result: the **first** tuple in this order attaining the minimum (ties keep the earlier tuple). By items 2 and 3 the returned WLP is the exact minimum over `𝒢`, and over all regular designs in the unblocked case.

5. **Blocks (PROVED).** `2^b` blocks are defined by `b` linearly independent masks `B = (β₁ … β_b)` over the base factors; run `x` is in block `Σ_t 2^(t−1)·[Π_{c∈bits(β_t)} x_c = −1]`. Base bit `z_c = 1` means `x_c = −1`, so this is `Σ_t 2^(t−1)·(β_t·z mod 2)`; runs are in the §28 (`ff2n`) row order. Item 6 returns the first feasible `B`, not a best one among feasible `B`. Every factor and every effect (product of factors) has a vector `u ∈ F₂^m`: the base factor `c` has `e_c`, factor `m + t − 1` has `g_t`, a product has the XOR. Its ±1 column `(−1)^{u·z}` (`z` the run's base bits) is constant within every block iff `u ∈ span(B)`: the blocks are the cosets of `ker = {z : β_t·z = 0 ∀t}`, the column is constant on every coset iff `u·y = 0` for all `y ∈ ker`, iff `u ∈ ker^⊥ = span(B)`. Such an effect is **confounded with blocks**.

6. **Blocked feasibility and choice (decision).** A design `G` is feasible for `2^b` blocks iff some independent `B` has `span(B) \ {0}` disjoint from `D(G) = {v_i} ∪ {v_i ⊕ v_j : i < j}` (the vectors of all main effects and two-factor interactions; FrF2's default `alias.block.2fis = FALSE`). `B` is found by depth-first search: `β₁ < β₂ < …` in increasing integer order, each `β ∉ span` of the earlier ones and with every new span element `s ⊕ β` outside `D(G)`; the first complete `B` is returned. The blocked result is the first `G` in item 4's order with lex-minimal `W(G)` among feasible designs (item 4 with feasibility at leaves; item 3 stays valid because feasibility only removes leaves). If no `G ∈ 𝒢` is feasible, raise `ValueError` (FrF2 2.3-5 errors on BL2, `reports/w15-frf2-ext`).

7. **Alias report (decision).** `block_confounded`: all factor-index tuples `(i₁ < … < i_r)`, `r ≤ 3`, whose vector lies in `span(B) \ {0}` (by item 6, only `r = 3` can occur), sorted by `(r, indices)`; empty when unblocked.

8. **Oracles.**
   - **Exhaustive (PROVED by enumeration, required test):** brute force over all of `𝒢` (and, blocked, all independent `B` sets) without pruning, on small sizes, gives the same minimal WLP as item 4/6.
   - **Catalogue regression (OBSERVED, Architect, pure-Python integers, 2026-11-20):** for all 22 sizes of §34.1 the item-4 WLP equals the WLP of the frozen `.1` catalogue entry.
   - **FrF2 2.3-5 references (OBSERVED, Architect prototype, 2026-11-20; frozen in `reports/w15-frf2-ext`):** MA1 (32 runs, 13 factors) `A₄ … A₁₂ = 55, 0, 96, 0, 87, 0, 16, 0, 1`; MA2 (32, 14) `77, 0, 168, 0, 203, 0, 56, 0, 7`; MA3 (64, 12) `A₄ … A₉ = 6, 24, 16, 0, 9, 8`; BL1 (16, 6, 2 blocks) and BL3 (32, 7, 4 blocks) `A₄ = 3`, rest 0; RD1 (8, 3, 2 blocks) `W = 0`, `B = (7)`; BL2 (16, 5, 4 blocks) infeasible. All lower lengths 0. All seven agree with item 4/6.

9. **Cost (OBSERVED).** Worst case `O(C(2^m − m − 1, p) · 2^p)`; item 3 prunes. Architect prototype node counts (root included): MA1 34,974, MA2 72,158, MA3 166,699 (≤ 3.5 s pure Python); blocked infeasible cases visit the whole tree. Covered sizes (decision, the measured domain): unblocked `runs ∈ {8, 16, 32, 64}` with `m ≤ k ≤ k_max(runs) = 7, 15, 14, 12`; blocked `blocks ∈ {2, 4, 8}` with `m ≤ k ≤ min(11, k_max(runs))`, and `blocks = 8` only for `runs ≤ 32`. Any other request raises `ValueError` naming the covered sizes. Covered blocked sizes that are infeasible by item 6 (e.g. 16 runs, 2 blocks, `k ≥ 8`; 32 runs, 4 blocks, `k = 11`; 32 runs, 8 blocks, `k ≥ 10`; 8 runs with 4 or 8 blocks; reviewer-confirmed by whole-tree search) raise the item 6 `ValueError`, not the size error.

## 109. API decision: `minimum_aberration(k, runs, *, blocks=1, dtype=torch.float64, device=None) -> dict`

- In `src/torchoed/factorial.py`, exported from `torchoed`. `k`, `runs`, `blocks` are Python ints in the covered sizes of §108.9.
- Returns `design` (`runs × k` ±1: `fractional_factorial(m, generators)`, or `ff2n(m)` when `p = 0`), `generators` (`[bits(g) for g in G]`), `wlp` (`word_length_pattern(design)`), `block` (int64 block number per run, §108.5; all 0 when `blocks = 1`), `block_generators` (`[bits(β) for β in B]`, empty when `blocks = 1`), `block_confounded` (§108.7).
- Existing `fractional_factorial`, `fractional_factorial_generators` and `word_length_pattern` outputs are unchanged.

## 110. Not in this section

Non-regular or mixed-level designs, 128 or more runs, sizes outside §108.9, other blocking criteria (clear 2FIs, Sitter–Chen–Feder), allowing 2FIs confounded with blocks, randomization, split-plot fractional factorials, minimum-aberration ranking beyond the first design, and GPU timing.

## 111. Power by simulation for GLMs (W15 C3)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C3.** Status: `P2_C3_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Normal-theory power (§22, §74) is unchanged; this section adds Monte Carlo Wald power for logistic and Poisson regression on a given design.

Inputs: a model matrix `X[n, p]` of full column rank (the rows of a given design, already expanded), true coefficients `β[p]`, family `binomial` (Bernoulli responses, logit link) or `poisson` (log link), level `α ∈ (0, 1)`, `S` simulations. `η* = Xβ`, `μ* = h(η*)` with `h` the inverse link. `ε = 2⁻⁵²`; `Φ` the standard normal cdf; `Q(a, x)` the regularized upper incomplete gamma function (`torch.special.gammaincc`).

1. **Simulation (decision).** One draw `Y[S, n]`: `torch.bernoulli(μ*.expand(S, n), generator=generator)` (binomial) or `torch.poisson(μ*.expand(S, n), generator=generator)` (poisson). Rows are independent simulations.
2. **Fit (decision: R `glm.fit` with default `glm.control(epsilon = 1e-8, maxit = 25)`, unit weights, no offset; applied to every row independently, batched).**
   - Inverse link and derivative, with R's clamps: binomial `h(η) = t/(1 + t)`, `t = ε` if `η < −30`, `t = 1/ε` if `η > 30`, else `t = exp(η)`; `h′(η) = ε` if `|η| > 30`, else `exp(η)/(1 + exp(η))²`. Poisson `h(η) = max(exp(η), ε)`, `h′(η) = max(exp(η), ε)`. Variance `v(μ) = μ(1 − μ)` (binomial), `μ` (poisson).
   - Deviance `D(y, μ) = −2Σᵢ[yᵢ log μᵢ + (1 − yᵢ) log(1 − μᵢ)]` (binomial, `0·log 0 = 0`) or `2Σᵢ[yᵢ log(yᵢ/μᵢ) − (yᵢ − μᵢ)]` (poisson, `0·log 0 = 0`).
   - Start `μ⁰ = (y + ½)/2` (binomial) or `y + 0.1` (poisson), `η⁰ = log(μ⁰/(1 − μ⁰))` or `log μ⁰`, `D⁰ = D(y, μ⁰)`; the first iteration uses `μ⁰` itself (not `h(η⁰)`) for `v(μ)` and `y − μ`, as R does.
   - Iteration `t = 1, …, 25`: `w = h′(η)²/v(μ)`, `z = η + (y − μ)/h′(η)`, `A = Xᵀdiag(w)X`, `β̂ = A⁻¹Xᵀ(w ∘ z)` (Cholesky solve), `η = Xβ̂`, `μ = h(η)`, `Dᵗ = D(y, μ)`. The row is **converged** at the first `t` with `|Dᵗ − Dᵗ⁻¹|/(|Dᵗ| + 0.1) < 1e-8`; its `β̂`, `A` and `η` are then frozen while other rows continue. A row that has not converged after `t = 25`, or for which `torch.linalg.cholesky_ex(A)` returns non-zero `info` (no jitter) or `β̂` or `Dᵗ` is non-finite at some iteration, is **non-converged** (frozen at that point). R's step-halving for non-finite deviance and its boundary checks are deliberately omitted (decision). Each row's result depends only on its own response, never on the other rows of the batch.
   - Wald test (R `summary.glm`, dispersion 1): `Cov = A⁻¹` with `A` from the row's final iteration (weights at the iterate *before* the final `β̂`), `zⱼ = β̂ⱼ/√Covⱼⱼ`; coefficient `j` is rejected iff `2Φ(−|zⱼ|) < α`, computed as `erfc(|zⱼ|/√2) < α`. For a column-index group `g`, `W = β̂_gᵀ(Cov_gg)⁻¹β̂_g`, `(Cov_gg)⁻¹β̂_g` by a Cholesky solve, rejected iff `Q(|g|/2, W/2) < α`. Non-converged rows reject nothing (TorchOED decision; R would still report p-values for a fit stopped at `maxit`).
   - Power: `p̂ⱼ = (1/S)Σₛ 1[row s rejects j]` over **all** `S` rows (separated and non-converged rows included as they fall, as skpr counts them); group power likewise; Monte Carlo standard error `√(p̂(1 − p̂)/S)`.
3. **Single-column groups (PROVED).** `Q(½, x) = erfc(√x)` for `x ≥ 0`, so for `g = {j}`, `W = zⱼ²` and `Q(½, zⱼ²/2) = erfc(|zⱼ|/√2)`: the group rule equals the coefficient rule. Numerical check (Architect, SciPy float64, 25 points `z ∈ [−6, 6]`): max deviation `3.0e−15`.
4. **Separation flag (OBSERVED, reporting only; does not change power).** A converged row is counted **separated** iff `max_i |η_i| > 10` (binomial) or `min_i η_i < −10` (poisson), `η = Xβ̂` at the final iterate. Observation (Architect, NumPy float64 implementation of item 2, 2026-11-20): on the saturated design of #468 G3 (2² factorial × 6, model `1 + X1 + X2 + X1X2`, `β = (0.5, 1, 0.5, 0.25)`), 10,000 simulations, the flagged rows are exactly the rows with an all-0 or all-1 cell (MLE does not exist), 0 mismatches for thresholds 5, 8, 10, 15; separated rows had `max|η| ≥ 18.57`, the others `≤ 1.61`; flagged fraction 0.6532 vs. the exact separation probability `1 − Π_c(1 − p_c⁶ − (1 − p_c)⁶) = 0.6475`. Not an exact separation test in general.
5. **Exact-enumeration oracle (PROVED).** Each row's rejection is a deterministic function `r(y)` of its response, so `p = E r(Y) = Σ_y P(y) r(y)` and `S·p̂ ~ Binomial(S, p)`: `E p̂ = p`, `sd p̂ = √(p(1 − p)/S)`. For binomial the sum is over all `2ⁿ` responses. For poisson with all `yᵢ ≤ K` enumerated, `p_K ≤ p ≤ p_K + τ_K`, `p_K` the truncated sum and `τ_K = 1 − Πᵢ P(Yᵢ ≤ K)`. Tiny cases (sanity values, OBSERVED with the NumPy implementation above; tests recompute the sum with the implementation's own per-response rule):
   - **B:** `X = [1, X1, X2]` on the 2² factorial (X1 fastest) stacked 4 times (`n = 16`), `β = (0, 1, 0.5)`, binomial, `α = 0.05`: 65,536 responses; `p = (0.001945, 0.108394, 0.011026)`, group `{1, 2}`: 0.020456.
   - **P:** `X = [1, x]`, `x = (−1, −1, 1, 1)`, `β = (0.5, 0.5)`, poisson, `α = 0.05`, `K = 20` (194,481 responses): `p = (0.211672, 0.067840)`, `τ_K ≤ 3.9e−12`.
6. **External agreement (OBSERVED).** The NumPy implementation of item 2 with 10,000 simulations from an independent generator agrees with the frozen skpr 1.9.2 references G1–G3 (`reports/w15-glmpower-ext`, #468) for all 12 coefficients within 4 combined Monte Carlo standard errors (largest |z| 2.16 for the Architect's seed and 2.24 for the independent reviewer's seed) `√(p̂₁(1 − p̂₁)/S₁ + p̂₂(1 − p̂₂)/S₂)`.
7. **Complexity (target).** At most 25 iterations, each `O(S n p² + S p³)` (one batched `Xᵀdiag(w)X`, one batched Cholesky); workspace `S·(n + p²)` floats checked against `max_elements`; no Python loop over simulations. Device of `X` (CPU or CUDA), float64.

## 112. API decision: `glm_power(X, beta, family, *, alpha=0.05, nsim=1000, groups=None, generator=None, max_elements=10_000_000)`

- In `information.py`, exported from `torchoed`. `X` a 2-D float64 tensor with `n ≥ p`, `beta` a length-`p` float64 tensor on `X`'s device, `family` `"binomial"` or `"poisson"`, `0 < alpha < 1`, `nsim` a positive Python `int`, `groups` `None` or a list of non-empty lists of distinct Python `int` column indices in `[0, p)`; `alpha` a Python `int` or `float`; `bool` is rejected wherever an `int` is expected; `generator` `None` or a `torch.Generator` on `X`'s device; otherwise `ValueError`. If `nsim·(n + p²) > max_elements`, `ValueError` (no chunking). Rank deficiency of `X` surfaces as non-converged rows; no separate rank check.
- Result: a `dict` with `power` (length `p`), `mc_se` (length `p`), `group_power` and `group_mc_se` (length `len(groups)`, only when `groups` is given), `nonconverged` (item 2) and `separated` (item 4; converged rows only), Python ints, `nsim`, `alpha`, `family`.
- Determinism: one generator draw per call as item 1; the same generator state gives identical results on the same device and torch version.

## 113. Not in this section

Firth or penalized fits, likelihood-ratio or score tests, overdispersion, other links or families, effect-coded categorical helpers, exact (non-simulation) GLM power, GLM design optimisation (P3), GPU timing claims (a separate same-host evidence task under #374).

## 114. Split-split-plot diagnostics and power (W15 C1, part 3)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C1.** Status: `P2_C1C_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Model, notation and closed form are §102 (`V = I + η_wZ_wZ_wᵀ + η_sZ_sZ_sᵀ`, `w` whole plots, `s = Σ_k J_k` subplots of sizes `m_{kj}`, `a_{kj} = 1/(1 + η_s m_{kj})`, `T_k = Σ_j m_{kj}a_{kj}`); diagnostics conventions are §19 (`S = F[ids]`, `n × p`, `J` the non-constant columns of `S`, region rows `R`, unit error variance). `F` may contain an intercept column.

1. **Information and covariance (PROVED).** `M = SᵀV⁻¹S` by the §102.1 closed form. `V` is known, so the GLS estimate `θ̂ = M⁻¹SᵀV⁻¹y` has covariance `M⁻¹`; `M ≻ 0` iff `S` has full column rank (§102.2), else `torch.linalg.cholesky` raises.
2. **SPV and FDS (decision).** `SPV(x) = n·f(x)ᵀM⁻¹f(x)` (uncentered, as §19.2 for whole plots); `fds` is `spv` sorted ascending.
3. **Correlation and VIF (decision; PROVED closed form).** `C = XᵀPX` for `X = S[:, J]`, `P = V⁻¹ − V⁻¹1(1ᵀV⁻¹1)⁻¹1ᵀV⁻¹` (GLS centring, as §19.3). On subplot `(k, j)`, `V⁻¹1 = a_{kj}/(1 + η_wT_k)·1` (from §102.1: `A_k⁻¹1 = a_{kj}1` and `1 − d_kT_k = 1/(1 + η_wT_k)`). Hence `C = M[J, J] − u uᵀ/τ` with `u = Σ_k t_k[J]/(1 + η_wT_k)`, `t_k = Σ_j a_{kj}s_{kj}` (§102.1) and `τ = Σ_k T_k/(1 + η_wT_k)`; no `n × n` matrix. `correlation = Δ^{−1/2}CΔ^{−1/2}`, `Δ = diag C`, `vif = diag(correlation⁻¹)` by Cholesky, raising when `C` is not numerically positive definite (as §19.3).
4. **Power (decision: containment rule; PROVED exact in the balanced orthogonal case).** Coefficient `j` is **whole-plot** if column `j` of `S` is constant within every whole plot (exact float comparison; an intercept is whole-plot), else **subplot** if constant within every subplot, else **run-level**. With `p_w`, `p_s`, `p_r` such columns (`p_w + p_s + p_r = p`): `ν_j = w − p_w`, `s − w − p_s`, `n − s − p_r` respectively; `λ_j = δ_j²/(M⁻¹)_jj`; `power_j` by §11.2–11.6 with `ν_j`. A group `G` must lie in one stratum (else `ValueError`); its F-test has `λ_G = δ_Gᵀ[(M⁻¹)_GG]⁻¹δ_G` and degrees of freedom `(|G|, ν)` of that stratum (§56). Requires `ν_j ≥ 1` for every coefficient `j` (else `ValueError`); a stratum with no column (e.g. run level in §114.5 (R-a)) has no `ν_j` and is not checked.
   - **Exact case (PROVED).** All whole plots of size `m_w`, all subplots of size `b`; every subplot column sums to zero within every whole plot, every run-level column sums to zero within every subplot. Then whole-plot columns lie in `span Z_w`, subplot columns in `span Z_s ⊖ span Z_w`, run-level columns in `(span Z_s)^⊥`, the three eigenspaces of `V` with eigenvalues `1 + η_s b + η_w m_w`, `1 + η_s b`, `1`. So `V⁻¹` scales each stratum, `M = blockdiag(S_wᵀS_w/(1 + η_s b + η_w m_w), S_sᵀS_s/(1 + η_s b), S_rᵀS_r)` (GLS = OLS), and each coefficient's estimate and the residual mean square of its stratum (whole-plot means on `w − p_w` df; subplot means minus whole-plot means on `s − w − p_s` df; within-subplot deviations on `n − s − p_r` df) are independent under joint normality, so each stratum t-test has exactly the `F′(1, ν_j, λ_j)` law. Otherwise the power is APPROXIMATE (unequal sizes or non-orthogonal columns; no Kenward–Roger), as §19.5; the result reports `df`.
   - Architect check (OBSERVED, NumPy/SciPy dev-only, 4·10⁴ simulated responses, item 6 design, `η_w = 2`, `η_s = 1/2`, `δ = (0, 0.9, 0.7, 0.5)`, `α = 0.05`): stratum t-test rejection rates `0.0489, 0.1085, 0.2849, 0.4071` vs `0.05, 0.1082, 0.2843, 0.4080`.
5. **Reductions (PROVED).** (R-a) Every subplot of size 1: `V = (1 + η_s)(I + η′ZZᵀ)`, `η′ = η_w/(1 + η_s)` (§102.3), so `M` and `C` are `1/(1 + η_s)` times those of `design_diagnostics(F, ids, whole_plots=…, eta=η′)`; `correlation`, `vif` are equal, `spv` is `(1 + η_s)` times its `spv`; no column is run-level, `ν` equals its `df`; `power` with `δ` equals its `power` with `δ/√(1 + η_s)` (same for groups). (R-b) Every whole plot one subplot (`s = w`): `V = I + (η_w + η_s)ZZᵀ`; no column is subplot-only; `ν_W = w − p_w`, `ν_R = n − w − p_r`; every output equals `design_diagnostics(F, ids, whole_plots=…, eta=η_w + η_s)`. Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-20): both `M` identities against dense `SᵀV⁻¹S` in 40/40 random cases; the `V⁻¹1` closed form of item 3 in 60/60 random nested layouts (1–3 subplots of sizes 1–3 per whole plot, rational `η_w, η_s`).
6. **Oracle (PROVED).** `F = [1, A, B, x]`, `n = 16`: whole plots `[4, 4, 4, 4]` at `A = −1, −1, 1, 1`; each whole plot has subplots `[2, 2]` at `B = −1, 1`; each subplot has `x = −1, 1` (`w = 4`, `s = 8`, `p_w = 2`, `p_s = 1`, `p_r = 1`). Exact case of item 4: `M = diag(16/q, 16/q, 16/r, 16)`, `q = 1 + 2η_s + 4η_w`, `r = 1 + 2η_s`; `ν = (2, 2, 3, 7)`; `C = diag(16/q, 16/r, 16)`, `correlation = I₃`, `vif = 1`; `SPV = 2q + r + 1 = 4 + 6η_s + 8η_w` at every design run (`= 23` at `η_w = 2`, `η_s = 1/2`, exact `Fraction`, Architect).
7. **Complexity (target).** The existing `_nested_information` closed form once, one Cholesky of `M`, one triangular solve for the region rows: `O(np² + |R|p² + p³)`; no `n × n` matrices.

## 115. API decision: `design_diagnostics(F, ids, *, whole_plots=…, eta=…, subplots=None, eta_sub=1.0)`

- `subplots=None`: unchanged behaviour, byte-identical results. Otherwise `subplots` is a list/tuple of positive Python ints summing to `len(ids)` whose cumulative boundaries include every whole-plot boundary, `whole_plots` is required, and `eta_sub` is a finite positive Python `int` or `float` (`bool` rejected); with `blocks`, `covariates` or `alias` → `ValueError`. Row order of `ids` is as returned by `optimal_design(..., subplots=...)`.
- Result: the §19/§20 keys computed by §114.1–114.4 (`columns`, `correlation`, `vif`, `spv`, `fds`; with `effect`, `power` and `df`; with `groups`, `group_power` and `group_df`).

## 116. Not in this section

Strip-plot diagnostics and power, Kenward–Roger or Satterthwaite degrees of freedom, unknown variance ratios, subplots with blocks or covariates, structure-aware alias matrices, GPU timing.

## 117. Strip-plot diagnostics and power (W15 C1, part 4)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C1.** Status: `P2_C1D_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Model, grid order, `V`, `λ_r = 1 + η_rc`, `λ_c = 1 + η_cr`, `λ_0 = 1 + η_rc + η_cr` and the closed form of `M` are §105 (item 1, `_strip_information`); diagnostics conventions are §19 and §114 (`S = F[ids]`, `n = r·c` rows in row-major grid order, `J` the non-constant columns of `S`, region rows `R`, unit error variance). `F` may contain an intercept column.

1. **Information and covariance (PROVED).** `M = SᵀV⁻¹S` by §105.1; `V` is known, so the GLS estimate has covariance `M⁻¹`; `M ≻ 0` iff `S` has full column rank (§105.2), else `torch.linalg.cholesky` raises.
2. **SPV and FDS (decision).** `SPV(x) = n·f(x)ᵀM⁻¹f(x)` (uncentered, as §114.2); `fds` is `spv` sorted ascending.
3. **Correlation and VIF (decision; PROVED closed form).** `C = XᵀPX` for `X = S[:, J]`, `P = V⁻¹ − V⁻¹1(1ᵀV⁻¹1)⁻¹1ᵀV⁻¹` (GLS centring, as §114.3). `1` lies in the `P_0` eigenspace of `V`, so `V⁻¹1 = 1/λ_0` and `1ᵀV⁻¹1 = n/λ_0`; hence `C = M[J, J] − T_J T_Jᵀ/(n λ_0)` with `T = Σ_o s_o` the column totals of `S`; no `n × n` matrix. `correlation`, `vif` from `C` exactly as §114.3.
4. **Power (decision: containment rule; PROVED exact in the balanced orthogonal case with an intercept).** Coefficient `j` is **row** if column `j` of `S` is constant within every grid row (exact float comparison; any constant column, including an intercept, is row), else **column** if constant within every grid column, else **cell**. With `p_R`, `p_C`, `p_I` such columns (`p_R + p_C + p_I = p`): `ν_j = r − p_R`, `c − 1 − p_C`, `(r − 1)(c − 1) − p_I` respectively (they sum to `n − p`); noncentrality `κ_j = δ_j²/(M⁻¹)_jj` (the §114.4 `λ_j`; the noncentral-t parameter is `√κ_j`); `power_j` by §11.2–11.6 with `(ν_j, κ_j)`. The test this power describes is the known-`V` stratum test `t_j = θ̂_j / √((MS_s/λ_s)·(M⁻¹)_jj)`, `MS_s` the residual mean square of coefficient `j`'s stratum `s` and `λ_s = λ_r`, `λ_c` or `1` its eigenvalue. Groups, the one-stratum rule, `group_df` and the per-coefficient `ν_j ≥ 1` check (else `ValueError`; a stratum with no column is not checked) exactly as §114.4.
   - **Exact case (PROVED).** `S` has an all-ones column; every other row column sums to zero over the design, every column column sums to zero over the design, and every cell column sums to zero within every grid row and within every grid column. Then the intercept lies in the range of `P_0`, the other row columns in that of `P_R − P_0`, the column columns in that of `P_C − P_0`, and the cell columns in that of `I − P_R − P_C + P_0` (§105.1 eigenspaces with eigenvalues `λ_0`, `λ_r`, `λ_c`, `1`); `V⁻¹` scales each, `M = blockdiag(n/λ_0, S_RᵀS_R/λ_r, S_CᵀS_C/λ_c, S_IᵀS_I)` up to column order (GLS = OLS). The stratum residuals (row means minus the fitted intercept and row columns, on `r − p_R` df, all in the `λ_r` eigenspace because `range(P_R) ⊖ span{1, row columns}` ⊂ `range(P_R − P_0)`; column means minus the grand mean and fitted column columns, on `c − 1 − p_C` df; double-centred cell residuals minus fitted cell columns, on `(r − 1)(c − 1) − p_I` df) are independent of every estimate under joint normality, so every `t_j`, the intercept included, has exactly the `F′(1, ν_j, κ_j)` law. Otherwise — non-orthogonal columns, or no intercept (then the `1` direction, eigenvalue `λ_0`, falls in the row residual) — the power is APPROXIMATE (no Kenward–Roger), as §19.5; the result reports `df`.
   - Checks (OBSERVED, NumPy/SciPy dev-only, 4·10⁴ simulated responses, item 5 design): Architect, `η_r = η_c = 1`, `δ = (0, 1.2, 1.0, 0.5)`, `α = 0.05`, rejection rates `0.2417`, `0.1906`, `0.4206` for `A`, `B`, `x` vs item 4 powers `0.2411`, `0.1872`, `0.4211`. Independent reviewer, own seed: `0.2444`, `0.1850`, `0.4211` at `(1, 1)`; `0.2186`, `0.1945`, `0.5604` vs `0.2183`, `0.1956`, `0.5591` at `(2, 1/2)`, `δ = (0.7, 1.5, 0.8, 0.6)`; intercept size `0.0503` and rate `0.0817` vs `0.0824` (`δ₀ = 0.7`, `(2, 1/2)`). Without the intercept (`F = [A, B, x]`) the `A` test has size `0.0389` and rate `0.2643` vs `0.3223`: APPROXIMATE, as stated. The unscaled test `θ̂₀/√(MS_R/n)` is not the one described (size `0.085`).
5. **Oracle (PROVED).** `F = [1, A, B, x]`, grid `r = c = 4` (`n = 16`), `A = (−1, −1, 1, 1)` by grid row, `B = (−1, −1, 1, 1)` by grid column, `x = (−1)^{i+j}` in cell `(i, j)` (`p_R = 2`, `p_C = 1`, `p_I = 1`; `ν = (2, 2, 2, 8)`). Exact case of item 4: `M = diag(16/λ_0, 16/λ_r, 16/λ_c, 16)` with `λ_r = 1 + 4η_r`, `λ_c = 1 + 4η_c`, `λ_0 = 1 + 4η_r + 4η_c`; `C = diag(16/λ_r, 16/λ_c, 16)`, `correlation = I₃`, `vif = 1`; `SPV = λ_0 + λ_r + λ_c + 1 = 4 + 8η_r + 8η_c` at every design run (exact `Fraction` against dense `SᵀV⁻¹S`, Architect, 2026-11-20: `20` at `(1, 1)`, `24` at `(2, 1/2)`, `436/21` at `(3/7, 5/3)`).
6. **Complexity (target).** `_strip_information` once, one Cholesky of `M`, one triangular solve for the region rows: `O(np² + |R|p² + p³)`; no `n × n` matrices.
   - Fraction check (Architect, 2026-11-20): `V⁻¹1 = 1/λ_0`, §105.1 `M` and the item 3 `C` equal the dense values in 60/60 random cases (`r ∈ {1, 2, 3}`, `c ∈ {2, 3}`, `p = 2…4`, integer `S` in [−2, 2] with intercept, rational `η_r, η_c`).

## 118. API decision: `design_diagnostics(F, ids, *, strip=None, eta_row=1.0, eta_col=1.0)`

- `strip=None`: unchanged behaviour, byte-identical results.
