Otherwise `strip` is a pair of positive Python ints `(r, c)` with `r·c = len(ids)`, `eta_row`, `eta_col` finite positive Python `int` or `float` (`bool` rejected); `strip` given together with any of `whole_plots`, `subplots`, `blocks`, `covariates` or `alias` → `ValueError`. Row order of `ids` is row-major as returned by `optimal_design(..., strip=...)`.
- Result: the §19/§20 keys computed by §117.1–117.4 (`columns`, `correlation`, `vif`, `spv`, `fds`; with `effect`, `power` and `df`; with `groups`, `group_power` and `group_df`).

## 119. Not in this section

Kenward–Roger or Satterthwaite degrees of freedom, unknown variance ratios, strip-plot with blocks, covariates or fixed runs, structure-aware alias matrices, GPU timing.

## 120. `fixed_ids` with split-split-plot and strip-plot designs (W15 C1, part 4)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-20 · Week 15 goal C1.** Status: `P2_C1D_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Labels as above. Split-split-plot designs are §102–§103 and strip-plot designs §105–§106; the `fixed_ids` conventions are §25–§26 (whole plots). `fixed_ids` is validated as in the unblocked path: a 1-D integer sequence of distinct candidate IDs in `[0, m)` with `n_f = len(fixed_ids) < n`; an empty sequence is treated as `None`. This section supersedes the "`fixed_ids` → `ValueError`" items of §103 and §106 and the `fixed_ids` items of §104 and §107.

1. **Fixed positions (decision).** As §25.1. Positions are subplot by subplot within whole plots (§102), or row-major grid cells `o = i·c + j` (§105). Fixed run `t` occupies position `t` (`t = 0 … n_f − 1`) during the search, and a position is **fixed** iff it is `< n_f` (the returned order is §121).
   - Split-split-plot: every subplot or whole plot all of whose positions are `< n_f` is fully fixed. At most one subplot and at most one whole plot are partly fixed, namely those holding position `n_f − 1` when position `n_f` lies in the same unit.
   - Strip-plot: row `i` is touched iff `i·c < n_f`. Column `j` is touched iff `j < min(n_f, c)`. A unit (subplot, whole plot, row or column) is **touched** iff it holds a fixed position.
2. **Feasibility (decision; trust boundary).** For each touched unit, all fixed runs in it must share that unit's label, or `ValueError` is raised. This means `hard` per whole plot and `subplot_hard` per subplot, or `row_hard` per row and `col_hard` per column. That shared label is the unit's label. A touched unit is never relabelled. Its free positions take only candidates with the labels of all units containing them: `(h_k, u_{kj})` for split-split-plot, `(h_i, g_j)` for strip-plot. Item 2 is the only `ValueError` check. If the unused candidates for a free position run out, that is not a `ValueError` here; it is the §8 redraw failure of item 4.
3. **Moves (decision; PROVED invariant).** Only free positions change. An exchange (§102.5(i), §105.4(i)) is scored and tried only at a free position. A relabel (§102.5(ii)–(iii), §105.4(ii)–(iii)) is tried only for an untouched unit: an untouched subplot (its whole plot may be touched, and its `h` then stays), an untouched whole plot, or an untouched row or column. A relabel replaces only positions of that unit, all of which are free: every position of an untouched unit is free by the definition of touched, and an untouched whole plot contains only untouched subplots. The scoring identities (§102.4, §105.3) and acceptance rules are unchanged, because they hold for any rows. Every move leaves positions `0 … n_f − 1` unchanged, and by item 2 every move keeps every unit's labels consistent. By induction from the start (item 4), every visited design, and so the returned design, is feasible (§102, §105) and holds fixed run `t` in the unit(s) containing position `t`.
4. **Start (decision).** Without `fixed_ids`, the draws are exactly those of §103 and §106 and the results are byte-identical. With `fixed_ids`, each draw initialises `unused` as every ID except the fixed ones, then proceeds as follows.
   - **Split-split-plot.** Whole plots are taken in order. For a touched whole plot, `h` is its fixed label (no draw); otherwise `h` is drawn by §103. Within it, subplots are taken in order:
     - a fully fixed subplot gets no draw;
     - a partly fixed subplot keeps its fixed label `u` (no draw), and its free positions get `pool[torch.randperm(len(pool), generator=generator)[:m_{kj} − f_{kj}]]`, where `pool` is the unused `(h, u)` candidates in ascending ID and `f_{kj}` is its number of fixed positions;
     - an untouched subplot is drawn by §103 (`torch.randint` label from `U_h`, then `torch.randperm` of its pool).
   - **Strip-plot.** Rows are taken in order: a touched row takes its fixed label with no draw, and an untouched row is drawn by §106. Columns follow in order with the same rule. Then the free cells are filled in row-major order by the §106 cell draw (`torch.randint` over the unused `(h_i, g_j)` pool in ascending ID).
   - Drawn IDs are marked used. If a pool is too small or empty, or the §8 start rule fails, the whole design is redrawn. The failure count and its error are as in §8.
5. **Floor (PROVED).** As §25.5. The designs containing the fixed runs at their positions are a subset of all feasible designs with the given sizes (`(r, c)`), so the §102.2 / §105.2 floor remains a valid lower bound on efficiency relative to the best design containing the fixed runs. `efficiency_lower_bound` is computed exactly as in §103 / §106 on the returned design, fixed runs included. The certificate strings are unchanged, and local optimality now refers to the item-3 moves.
6. **Exhaustive oracles (PROVED by enumeration; Architect, exact `Fraction`, 2026-11-20).** Candidates, IDs, `F`, labels and `φ_A`, `φ_I` are exactly §102.6 and §105.5. Optima are taken over all designs that satisfy items 1–2 and have distinct IDs, with the §102.6 / §105.5 counting conventions. The same enumerator reproduces the unconstrained §102.6 (648 designs, 24 singular) and §105.5 (4,032, 864) counts and optima.
   - **Split-split-plot** (whole plots `[4, 4]`, subplots `[2, 2, 2, 2]`):
     - (SSF-a) `fixed_ids = [1, 0, 4, 3]` (whole plot 1 fully fixed at `A = −1`: subplot 1 `B = −1`, `x = 0, −1`; subplot 2 `B = 1`, `x = 0, −1`; augmentation by one whole plot): 18 designs, 2 singular. At `η_w = η_s = 1`: `max det M = 7936/441` (4 designs), `min φ_A = 27/8` (2), `min φ_I = 1903/744` (4). At `η_w = 2`, `η_s = 1/2`: `336/25` (4), `4` (2), `229/72` (4). The unconstrained optima `1280/63` and `16` (§102.6) are not attained, so the fixed runs bind.
     - (SSF-b) `fixed_ids = [1, 0, 3]` (subplot 1 fully fixed; subplot 2 partly fixed at `B = 1` by ID 3, `x = −1`): 36 designs, 2 singular. At `η_w = η_s = 1`: `1280/63` (4), `933/280` (2), `2083/840` (4). At `η_w = 2`, `η_s = 1/2`: `16` (4), `157/40` (2), `371/120` (4). `φ_A` binds (`25/8`, `89/24` unconstrained), and this case also checks the partly-fixed subplot label rule.
   - **Strip-plot** (grid `r = 2`, `c = 3`):
     - (STF-a) `fixed_ids = [1, 4]` (row 0 partly fixed at `A = −1`; columns 0 and 1 fixed at `B = −1, 1`): 72 designs, 16 singular. At `η_r = η_c = 1`: `max det M = 128/27`, `min φ_A = 137/32`, `min φ_I = 103/32` (4 designs each). At `η_r = 2`, `η_c = 1/2`: `24/7`, `115/24`, `271/72` (4 each). This binds against `80/9` and `6` (§105.5).
     - (STF-b) `fixed_ids = [1, 4, 2, 7]` (row 0 fully fixed; all columns touched; cell `(1, 0)` fixed, so row 1 has `A = 1`): 6 designs, 2 singular. At `η_r = η_c = 1`: `80/27`, `111/20`, `69/20` (1 design each). At `η_r = 2`, `η_c = 1/2`: `18/7`, `131/24`, `835/216` (1 each).
   - **Infeasible fixed runs** (item 2, `ValueError`): split-split-plot `[0, 3]` (subplot 1 mixes `B`) and `[0, 2, 6]` (whole plot 1 mixes `A`); strip-plot `[0, 8]` (row 0 mixes `A`) and `[0, 4, 2, 9]` (column 0 mixes `B`).
7. **Complexity (target).** Per pass as in §102.7 and §105.6, with fixed positions and touched units masked out. No new workspace.

## 121. API decision: `optimal_design(F, n, *, fixed_ids=…, whole_plots=…, subplots=…, …)` and `optimal_design(F, n, *, fixed_ids=…, strip=…, …)`

- `fixed_ids` is now accepted with `subplots` (§103) and with `strip` (§106), for criteria D, A and I. Every other exclusion of §103 and §106 is unchanged. `fixed_ids` is validated as in the unblocked path, and the label rule of §120.2 applies.
- Result: the keys and layout of §103 / §106 are unchanged. Split-split-plot `ids` are sorted within each subplot, so each fixed run appears inside its subplot but not necessarily at its own position. Strip-plot `ids` are in row-major cell order, so fixed run `t` is `ids[t]`. No new key.
- Without `fixed_ids`, split-split-plot and strip-plot results are byte-identical to `main`. Every other `fixed_ids` path is unchanged.

## 122. Not in this section

Interchange of runs between units, re-assignment of fixed runs to other positions, `fixed_ids` with `subplots` and `blocks`, incomplete strip grids, diagnostics changes (diagnostics take any `ids`), GPU or float32 search.

## 123. Least-squares analysis (W17 G1, P2.5)

**Chief Architect · 2026-11-22 · Week 17 goal G1.** Status: `P25_G1_MATH_FROZEN` once the independent fresh-context review verdict is recorded in its dispatch Issue and this section is committed byte-identically. Model `Y = Xβ + ε`, `ε` i.i.d. `N(0, σ²)` per response. `X` is `n × p` with full column rank `p < n`. `Y` is `n × J` (`J` responses sharing one `X`; a 1-D `y` is the case `J = 1`). `ν = n − p`. Columns of `X` are split, in order, into consecutive **terms** of sizes `c₁, …, c_k` (`Σ c_t = p`); `C_t = c₁ + … + c_t`, `C₀ = 0`.

1. **One factorisation (PROVED).** Let `X = QR` be the reduced QR factorisation (`Q` is `n × p` with orthonormal columns, `R` is `p × p` upper triangular and nonsingular). Then `Z = QᵀY` (`p × J`), `β̂ = R⁻¹Z` (triangular solve), residuals `E = Y − QZ`, `RSS = Σᵢ Eᵢ²` per column (computed from `E`, not as `‖Y‖² − ‖Z‖²`), `s² = RSS/ν`. `(XᵀX)⁻¹ = R⁻¹R⁻ᵀ`, so `SE(β̂ⱼ) = s · ‖row j of R⁻¹‖₂` and `tⱼ = β̂ⱼ / SE(β̂ⱼ)`. Cost `O(np² + npJ + p³)`; no `n × n` matrix.
2. **Sequential (type I) sums of squares (PROVED).** For each `t`, the first `C_t` columns of `Q` are an orthonormal basis of the span of the first `C_t` columns of `X` (triangularity of `R`). So `‖P_{C_t} Y‖² = Σ_{j ≤ C_t} Z_j²`, and `SS_t = RSS(first C_{t−1} columns) − RSS(first C_t columns) = Σ_{C_{t−1} < j ≤ C_t} Z_j²`, with `df_t = c_t`. This is the table of R `anova(lm(...))`: for its column order, each row is one term. `F_t = (SS_t / c_t) / s²` on `(c_t, ν)` df. The signs of the columns of `Q` do not matter.
3. **Intercept row (decision).** `intercept=True` (default) means that term 1 is the intercept column alone. As in R, it gets no ANOVA row. `intercept=False` lists every term. Its value is not checked against `X`; with `intercept=True` the caller guarantees that column 1 is the constant column and `c₁ = 1` (otherwise a real term is silently left out of the table).
4. **Lack of fit against pure error (PROVED).** Group the rows of `X` by exact equality, giving `G` groups (design points are distinct rows of `X`, so settings that the model matrix cannot tell apart, e.g. `x = ±1` under `[1, x²]`, share a group; the partition below still holds). Let `D` be the `n × G` group indicator matrix. Every row of `X` is constant within its group, so `X = D X_G` and `span(X) ⊆ span(D)`. Hence `RSS = SS_PE + SS_LOF` with `SS_PE = Σ_g Σ_{i∈g} (Yᵢ − Ȳ_g)²` on `df_PE = n − G` and `SS_LOF = RSS − SS_PE` (`≥ 0` in exact arithmetic; returned as computed, so rounding can make it slightly negative) on `df_LOF = G − p`. Under the model, `F_LOF = (SS_LOF/df_LOF) / (SS_PE/df_PE)` is `F(df_LOF, df_PE)`. The test is reported iff `G > p` and `n > G`; otherwise it is `None`. This equals R `anova(fit, lm(y ~ factor(g)))` with `g` the group label.
5. **Residual diagnostics (PROVED).** Leverage `hᵢ = ‖row i of Q‖²` (the diagonal of `X(XᵀX)⁻¹Xᵀ`). Internally studentized residual `rᵢ = Eᵢ / (s √(1 − hᵢ))` (R `rstandard`). Cook's distance `Dᵢ = rᵢ² hᵢ / (p (1 − hᵢ))` (R `cooks.distance`). Decision (as R `lm.influence` / `rstandard`): if `hᵢ ≥ 1 − 10·eps`, set `hᵢ = 1` and return `rᵢ = Dᵢ = NaN`.
6. **p-values (PROVED + decision).** With `I_x(a, b)` the regularized incomplete beta function, `P(F(d₁, d₂) > f) = 1 − I_x(d₁/2, d₂/2)` with `x = d₁f / (d₁f + d₂)`, and the two-sided t p-value is the F(1, ν) p-value at `f = t²`. `I_x` at half-integer arguments uses the §11.3 recurrences and the four bases `I_x(½, ½) = (2/π) asin √x`, `I_x(½, 1) = √x`, `I_x(1, ½) = 1 − √(1 − x)` and `I_x(1, 1) = x`. A statistic `f ≤ 0` (e.g. a rounding-negative `SS_LOF`) gives `p = 1` (decision). Otherwise start from the base whose arguments match the parities of `d₁` and `d₂`. Raise `b` to `d₂/2` with `+U` terms, then raise `a` to `d₁/2` with `−T` terms. This is a finite sum of `O(d₁ + d₂)` terms, each evaluated in log space as in §11.3. Decision: p-values are accurate in absolute terms only (a tiny p can have a large relative error) and are compared to R in absolute terms. Accuracy (OBSERVED, not proved): the Architect's float64 sandbox agrees with `scipy.stats.f.sf` (SciPy 1.18.1, dev-only) to 8.0·10⁻¹⁵ absolute on the grid `d₁ ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 8}`, `d₂ ∈ {1, 2, 3, 7, 30, 48, 200}`, `f ∈ {10⁻³, 0.5, 1, 3, 10, 50, 400}`. The fresh-context reviewer found that the error grows with `ν` through the `lgamma` differences: 5.8·10⁻¹³ at `ν = 2000`, 1.0·10⁻¹¹ at `2·10⁴` and 1.5·10⁻¹⁰ at `2·10⁵` (F(3, ν), f = 0.49). The work also grows as `O(d₁ + d₂)`. Tests use the item 7 closed forms (not SciPy, which is itself off by 2.8·10⁻¹¹ at `d₁ = d₂ = 1, f = 10⁻¹²`) with absolute tolerance 1e-12 and `ν ≤ 200`.
7. **Closed-form oracles (PROVED).** `ν = 1`: `p = 1 − (2/π) atan|t|`. `ν = 2`: `p = 1 − |t| / √(2 + t²)`. `F(2, d₂)`: `P(F > f) = (1 + 2f/d₂)^(−d₂/2)`. `F(d₁, 2)`: `P(F > f) = 1 − x^{d₁/2}`, `x = d₁f/(d₁f + 2)` (since `I_x(a, 1) = xᵃ`; this exercises the `−T` steps for `d₁ ≥ 3`).
8. **Exact oracle (PROVED).** For integer `X` and rational `Y`, `β̂ = (XᵀX)⁻¹XᵀY`, `RSS`, the nested-model differences of item 2, and `SS_PE` of item 4 are rational and can be computed exactly by `Fraction` Gauss–Jordan elimination, independently of the QR path.

## 124. API decision: `fit_least_squares(X, Y, *, terms=None, intercept=True)`

- Inputs: `X` is 2-D float64 (any device). `Y` has shape `(n,)` or `(n, J)` with the same dtype and device as `X`. `terms` is a tuple of positive ints summing to `p`; `None` means one column per term. Trust boundary: these shapes and dtypes, `n > p`, the `terms` sum, and full column rank, checked as `torch.linalg.matrix_rank(X) == p` with its default SVD tolerance (`ValueError` otherwise). The diagonal of an unpivoted `R` does not reveal rank, so it is not used. Near-collinear `X` that R `lm` would alias (pivoted QR, tolerance 1e-7) passes this check and is returned as computed; columns are expected to be coded or of comparable scale. Nothing else is validated.
- Returns a dict of tensors. The trailing response dimension is dropped when `Y` is 1-D.
  - Coefficients: `coef`, `se`, `t`, `p_t` (each `p`), `sigma2`, `df_resid = ν`.
  - `anova`: a dict with `df`, `ss`, `f` and `p` (one row per listed term, §123.3) and `ss_resid`.
  - `lack_of_fit`: a dict with `df_lof`, `ss_lof`, `df_pe`, `ss_pe`, `f` and `p`, or `None` (§123.4).
  - Diagnostics: `residuals`, `leverage`, `studentized`, `cooks` (each `n`).
- CPU and CUDA run the same code. No timing claim. No model formula parser. Users build `X` with the existing feature functions. No design search is called.

## 125. Not in this section

Weighted or generalized least squares, rank-deficient (aliased) models, type II/III sums of squares, mixed models and REML (W17 G2 decision), GLM fitting, prediction intervals and profiler data (W18), Lenth's method for unreplicated factorials, formula parsing, and an adapter to statsmodels or any other package.
